# STATUS.md: Project Change Log

This file records all significant changes, decisions, and progress made on the Multi-Tenant Web-Based Accounting Platform project. Entries are in reverse-chronological order.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Added a Feedback Feature Every Role Can See and Use

**What/Why:** User asked for a feedback feature that every role should see. Modeled directly on the existing in-app Help Assistant widget (`HelpAssistantWidget.tsx`), which is already mounted unconditionally in `MainLayout.tsx` and therefore visible to every authenticated role regardless of RBAC scoping - the same mount point guarantees "every role sees it" without inventing a new exemption from the existing role-gating system.

**Fix:** New `FeedbackWidget.tsx`, a floating button (bottom-left, so it doesn't collide with the Help widget's bottom-right button) mounted once in `MainLayout.tsx` alongside `HelpAssistantWidget`. Opens a small form (category: General/Bug/Feature Request + a message) that posts to `POST /api/v1/feedback`, open to any authenticated role - unlike most write routes in this app, submitting feedback isn't an operational action scoped to a role's job, it's every user's channel to reach the tenant's Admin/Auditor. Submissions are reviewed on a new Admin/Auditor-only `/feedback` page (`FeedbackInbox.tsx`), gated the same way as the existing "AI Assistant Activity" screen (`requireRole('Auditor')`, which also lets Admin through via the role hierarchy) - lists feedback with a New/All toggle and a "Mark Reviewed" action (`PUT /feedback/:id/status`).

**Schema:** New `Feedback` model (`tenantId`, `userId`, `userName`, `userRole` denormalized at submission time, `category`, `message`, `status` defaulting to `NEW`) - same shape and convention as the existing `Notification` model (plain `public`-schema table, tenant-isolated by a `tenantId` column and index, not part of the separate per-tenant-schema `TENANT_MIGRATIONS` system). Migration `20260817135426_add_feedback`.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit`/`tsc -b` clean (backend and frontend); frontend `vite build` clean. New `feedback.test.ts` (7 tests, run against a real local Postgres): rejects an empty message and an unknown category; any role (a Viewer) can submit and gets their own name/role stamped on the row; a Viewer is correctly 403'd from `GET /feedback` (scoped role, not explicitly granted, same rule that already gates AI Assistant Activity); Admin can list and never sees another tenant's feedback; Admin can mark an item reviewed and it drops out of the New-only filter; patching another tenant's feedback id 404s. Full backend suite run in the background to confirm no regressions from the `app.ts`/`schema.prisma` changes.

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260817135426_add_feedback/` (new); `backend/src/routes/feedback.ts` (new); `backend/src/app.ts`; `backend/src/tests/feedback.test.ts` (new); `frontend/src/components/FeedbackWidget.tsx` (new); `frontend/src/pages/feedback/FeedbackInbox.tsx` (new); `frontend/src/components/layout/MainLayout.tsx`; `frontend/src/App.tsx`; `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Backfilled Historical POS Revenue That Predates the Ledger-Posting Fix

**What/Why:** Direct same-day follow-up to the POS revenue-posting fix below. After confirming the fix was deployed and live on Render, the user reported the Dashboard's Total Revenue was *still* GHS 0.00. Diagnosed via `AskUserQuestion`: the sale in question was the same one made *before* the fix went live - a real, correctly-understood limitation of the original fix, not a new bug. `journalId` only gets set going forward (new sales, or an existing sale re-touched via a `clientTxnId` replay); a COMPLETED sale that was already sitting in the database with `journalId = null` at deploy time has nothing that will ever revisit it, so it stays permanently unposted with no user-facing way to fix it.

**Fix:** New `POST /tills/backfill-revenue` route (Admin/Accountant only, mirrors `requireRole` gating used elsewhere) - finds every COMPLETED `CashSale` for the tenant with `journalId IS NULL` and posts its Cash/Revenue entry via the same `postCashSaleRevenue()` used for new sales. Idempotent by construction (an already-posted sale is never touched again), so it's safe to call on every Dashboard load rather than requiring the user to find and click a hidden admin button - `Dashboard` (in `App.tsx`) now fires it once per mount for Admin/Accountant (guarded by a ref, restricted roles skip it entirely since the route 403s them anyway), shows a toast only if it actually found and fixed something ("Synced N POS sale(s) recorded before revenue tracking was fixed..."), and refetches the P&L data so the tile updates without a manual reload. `useProfitAndLoss` gained a `refetch()` for this. Mirrors the "self-heals automatically, no manual action needed" philosophy already used for the account-default-role backfill (see 2026-08-15 entry) rather than asking a non-technical shop owner to run a script or find a maintenance page.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit`/`tsc -b` clean (backend and frontend). New tests in `cashTill.test.ts`: one proves the backfill route correctly restores a sale's revenue when its journal entry was genuinely never posted (simulated by creating a real sale, then deleting the ledger/journal-entry rows it just posted - not merely nulling the CashSale's pointer, which would silently double-count on backfill instead of proving the fix works) and that a second run is a true no-op; another proves a Cashier is blocked (403) from triggering it. Full backend suite passing (same one pre-existing environmental failure as every other entry today - a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in this sandbox). **Live-verified via Playwright**, reproducing the exact reported scenario end-to-end against the real dev servers: seeded a tenant, rang up a real POS sale, then genuinely undid its journal posting (deleted the real ledger/journal_entries rows, not a shortcut) to faithfully simulate a sale from before this fix existed, then logged in as that tenant's Admin - the Dashboard auto-fired the backfill, the "Synced 1 POS sale..." toast appeared, and the Total Revenue tile updated to the correct amount with no manual action.

**Files:** `backend/src/routes/cashTill.ts`; `backend/src/tests/cashTill.test.ts`; `frontend/src/hooks/useProfitAndLoss.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Fixed Cross-Account Session Bleed: Two Staff Logging In on the Same Machine Shared One Session

**What/Why:** User reported (garbled but decodable): admin and staff can't be logged in on the same machine - clicking "Show all" on Expense Claims as a Shop Manager unexpectedly landed them in the Admin dashboard. Root cause found by reading `AuthContext.tsx`/`api.ts`: the login session (`accountgo-token`/`accountgo-user`/`accountgo-tenant-id`) was stored in `localStorage`, which is shared by **every tab of the same browser** - there was no per-tab isolation and no `storage`-event reconciliation. So an Admin logged in, then a Shop Manager logging in later on the same machine (new tab, or the same tab reused) silently overwrote the *one* shared session - whichever account logged in most recently is who the whole browser is authenticated as, regardless of which tab you're looking at. The "Show all claims" button is Admin/Accountant-gated (`ExpenseClaims.tsx`), so a Shop Manager seeing it at all was itself proof they were actually still inside the Admin's session, not their own.

**Fix:** Switched the three session keys from `localStorage` to `sessionStorage` in `AuthContext.tsx` and `api.ts` (every read/write site) - `sessionStorage` is genuinely isolated per browser tab (confirmed live below), so Admin and Shop Manager can now be logged in simultaneously in two tabs of the same browser with zero bleed-through. Trade-off, called out to the user: a session no longer survives closing that tab/browser (no more persistent "stay logged in"), which is the right default for a shared shop computer where staff log in per shift anyway. `ThemeContext.tsx`'s unrelated `localStorage` use for the light/dark preference was left untouched (not identity-sensitive, and arguably *should* persist/share across tabs).

**Verification:** `tsc -b` clean (frontend). Grepped the whole frontend for any other reader of the three keys - only `AuthContext.tsx`/`api.ts`, both fully migrated, nothing else needed changing. **Live-verified via Playwright**, reproducing the exact reported scenario: one browser context (one "machine"), two tabs - Tab 1 logs in as a real Admin, Tab 2 (same context) then logs in as a real Shop Manager, Tab 1 is reloaded. Confirmed each tab's `sessionStorage` token differs and independently resolves (via `/auth/me`) to the correct account (`admin@...`/Admin in Tab 1, `mgr@...`/Shop Manager in Tab 2), `accountgo-token` no longer exists in `localStorage` at all, and each tab's rendered page still shows its own account's name after the other tab logged in - `PASS: sessions stayed isolated per tab`. Backend needed no changes (JWT verification is already fully stateless/per-request, confirmed via code read - the bug was 100% frontend storage design).

**Files:** `frontend/src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/api.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Fixed a Real, Long-Standing Gap: POS Cash Sales Never Posted Revenue to the Ledger

**What/Why:** User made a real sale through the POS terminal and asked why the Admin Dashboard's Total Revenue still read GHS 0.00 (this came right after walking through the Expense Claim reimbursement flow, which correctly showed GHS 5.00 of Expenses - so the asymmetry was the tell). Investigated by reading `useProfitAndLoss.ts` → `routes/reports.ts` → `reportingService.getProfitAndLoss` → `reportRepository.getProfitAndLoss` first, to rule out a date-range/period-filter bug (ruled out - with no date params the P&L query genuinely has no date `WHERE` clause at all, a true all-time sum). Then read `routes/cashTill.ts`'s `POST /tills/sales` and `POST /tills/close` handlers in full: neither has ever called `journalEntryService.createJournalEntry` or referenced any REVENUE account - confirmed with `git log --all -p -- backend/src/routes/cashTill.ts | grep "journalEntryService|createJournalEntry|REVENUE"`, which returned zero hits across this file's entire history. POS sales only ever wrote to `CashSale`/`CashSaleLine`/`CashTill.cashSalesTotal`/`DailyCloseoutReport` - real operational/reconciliation records, but never the double-entry `ledgers` table the Dashboard, P&L, Trial Balance, and Balance Sheet all actually sum from. This directly contradicted `data/helpAssistantKnowledge.ts`'s own documented claim that "POS sales are cash-only and post revenue immediately" - the in-app Help Assistant was telling users something the code had never actually done. This was a real, pre-existing gap affecting every tenant's Revenue/P&L/Balance Sheet reporting for every POS sale ever recorded on this platform, not a bug the user introduced. Reported the finding in full before writing any code, and asked how to proceed; user confirmed.

**Fix:** `POST /tills/sales` now posts a real Debit Cash / Credit Revenue journal entry (`postCashSaleRevenue()`) once the sale's own atomic stock-deduction transaction commits - deliberately called as a **separate step after** that transaction, not nested inside it, since `journalEntryService.createJournalEntry()` opens its own top-level `withCurrentTenantDb` transaction and Prisma doesn't support true nested interactive transactions (mirrors the existing two-step "domain transaction, then journal posting" shape already used by `reimburseExpenseClaim`/`recordInvoicePayment`/`createCreditNote`, not a new pattern). A failure posting the journal is deliberately swallowed (logged, not thrown) rather than failing the whole request - the sale, its stock deduction, and the cash already physically collected are all real and must not be reported as a failed sale just because revenue posting hit a snag. The resulting `journalId` is stamped onto the `CashSale` row; if a retry (clientTxnId replay, or the existing `DuplicateSaleReplayError` refetch path) finds an existing sale with no `journalId`, it posts the missing journal then, self-healing a prior partial failure rather than leaving it permanently unposted. `POST /tills/sales/:id/void` now posts the mirror-image reversal (Debit Revenue / Credit Cash) via the same "own journal entry for the reversal" convention `creditDebitNoteService` already uses for revenue reversals (not `journalEntryService.voidJournalEntry`, which is the generic journal-entries-page mechanism, not used by any other domain service) - stored as `voidJournalId`. Both amounts use straightforward `resolveDefaultAccount(..., 'CASH'/'REVENUE')` lookups, same as every other auto-posting service in this codebase; no tax or COGS line, matching the existing precedent that invoice payments don't post COGS either (no inventory-costing/COGS auto-posting exists anywhere in this platform yet - a separate, bigger project).

**Schema:** `CashSale.journalId`/`voidJournalId` (both nullable strings, no FK relation - same pattern as `Invoice.journalId` et al., since the referenced `journal_entries` table lives in a different, dynamically-resolved Postgres schema per tenant) via a plain `prisma migrate dev` migration (`20260817122153_add_cash_sale_journal_ids`). Confirmed via inspection that `CashSale`/`CashTill` (unlike `accounts`/`journal_entries`/`ledgers`) were never part of the separate raw-SQL `TENANT_MIGRATIONS` system that duplicates a handful of "core" tables into each tenant's own Postgres schema - they're ordinary Prisma-migrated tables living in the shared `public` schema, isolated purely by `tenantId` filtering (same as most other feature tables added since the original core-tables/schema-per-tenant design) - so no `TENANT_MIGRATIONS` entry was needed, just the standard migration.

**Also fixed:** `helpAssistantKnowledge.ts`'s claim that POS sales post revenue immediately was checked - it's now genuinely true, so no correction was needed there after all (the fix made the documentation accurate instead of the other way around).

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (backend and frontend). New tests: `cashTill.test.ts` gained a test proving a sale's `journalId` resolves to a real `POSTED` journal entry with a matching Debit-Cash/Credit-Revenue line pair, and that `/reports/profit-loss`'s `totalRevenue` moves by exactly the sale amount. `cashSaleVoid.test.ts`'s existing self-authorized-void test extended to assert revenue increases on sale and returns to its exact prior value after void, with a real `voidJournalId` present. Both required adding a real Chart of Accounts (Cash/Revenue) to those test tenants' setup, which they didn't have before (silently harmless before this change, since nothing read those accounts). Full backend suite 654/655 (the 1 failure is the same pre-existing environmental mismatch noted in every entry this session - a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set in this sandbox). Confirmed `cashSaleIdempotency.test.ts`/`posCart.test.ts` (neither sets up a Chart of Accounts) still pass unaffected, since a missing Cash/Revenue account now just logs and no-ops rather than failing the sale.

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260817122153_add_cash_sale_journal_ids/` (new); `backend/src/routes/cashTill.ts`; `backend/src/tests/cashTill.test.ts`, `cashSaleVoid.test.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Toned Down Admin Broadcast Email Copy After a Real Send Landed in Spam

**What/Why:** The Gmail SMTP migration above went fully live end-to-end: the user ran a real broadcast from the Admin Core Engine console (the "New Feature" template) to a real tenant Admin, and it landed in Gmail's spam folder. Root cause wasn't a bug - it's a real deliverability limitation of sending via a personal Gmail SMTP account (no domain-level SPF/DKIM/DMARC reputation beyond Gmail's own, no sending history for this pattern) combined with content that reads as promotional/phishing-adjacent to Gmail's filters: an emoji-laden subject (`📢 Ledgio Notice: 🎁 New Feature: ...`), hype language ("New Feature!"), and a footer reading "This is an official administrative broadcast sent by Ledgio ERP System Administrators" - phrasing that pattern-matches spam/phishing templates more than genuine transactional mail.

**Fix (content only, not a transport change):** `broadcastService.ts`'s email wrapper rewritten to read like an ordinary one-to-one business email - no emoji in the subject (`Ledgio: {subject}` instead of `📢 Ledgio Notice: ...`), dropped the boxed "official administrative broadcast... System Administrators" framing in favor of a plain "Hi {name}, ... - The Ledgio Team" body with a low-key "you're receiving this because you're an admin on {tenant}" footer line. The three preset templates in `AdminCoreEngine.tsx` (System Upgrade / Maintenance / New Feature) had their emoji and exclamation-heavy copy toned down the same way. This reduces the *likelihood* of landing in spam - it's not a guarantee, since the underlying personal-Gmail-sending-reputation limitation is unchanged; genuinely reliable bulk deliverability would need a real transactional ESP with domain authentication, which is a separate, larger decision left open.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (backend and frontend). Found Postgres and Redis were both down in this sandbox mid-verification (unrelated to this change - likely stopped earlier in this long-running session) - restarted both (`pg_ctlcluster 16 main start`, `service redis-server start`) before re-running tests. Full backend suite 652/654 (the 2 failures are the same pre-existing environmental issues noted in every entry today - a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in this sandbox, and a flaky WebSocket timeout confirmed to pass in isolation), including `adminBroadcastEncrypted.test.ts`'s 4 tests. **Not independently re-verified with a second live send** (the fix is content-only and doesn't change the actual email transport tested in the entry above) - deliverability improvement can only really be confirmed by the user sending another real broadcast and checking the inbox.

**Files:** `backend/src/services/broadcastService.ts`; `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Outbound Email Moved Back to Direct Gmail SMTP (Nodemailer) After Render Starter Upgrade

**What/Why:** User upgraded Render's backend service from Free to Starter and asked to re-test email. Outbound SMTP had previously been confirmed blocked on Render's Free tier (ETIMEDOUT on both port 465 and 587, tested twice - once on 2026-07-29 and re-confirmed on 2026-08-11 "now that Render billing is active"), which is why email moved to SendGrid's HTTP API (port 443, never subject to that block). Rather than trust that a prior finding still held after a real infrastructure change, verified live: walked the user through Render's Shell (Starter+ only - Free tier doesn't have it) to test raw TCP connectivity directly, since `nc` isn't installed there - `timeout 5 bash -c 'echo > /dev/tcp/smtp.gmail.com/465'` and a `node -e` net.createConnection equivalent both returned real `OPEN` results (one apparent `BLOCKED/TIMEOUT` in the pasted output was a terminal bracketed-paste rendering artifact, not a genuine second test - confirmed by the two clean runs). Port 465 is now genuinely reachable, so this reverts the SendGrid migration and switches back to nodemailer.

**Backend:** `EmailService.ts` rewritten to use `nodemailer.createTransport()` (Gmail SMTP, host `smtp.gmail.com`, port 465/implicit TLS, `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` auth) instead of axios-posting to SendGrid's API, reusing the exact retry-once-after-5-minutes and audit-logging behavior the SendGrid version had. Kept the `dns.setDefaultResultOrder('ipv4first')` fix and `family: 4` transport option from the original (pre-SendGrid) nodemailer implementation - that was a real, separate bug fix for Gmail SMTP's IPv6 `ENETUNREACH` failure mode, unrelated to the port-blocking issue, so it's still needed. `isConfigured()` now checks `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` instead of `SENDGRID_API_KEY`/`EMAIL_FROM` - propagated through `/health`'s integrations diagnostic, `GET /admin/integrations`, and all three call sites that previously toggled the SendGrid vars for their configured/unconfigured test branches (`dunningReminderService.ts`/`invoiceEmailService.ts` only ever called the shared `EmailService.isConfigured()`, so neither needed changes themselves).

**Ops:** `render.yaml`'s backend service `plan` updated from `free` to `starter` (matching the real upgrade, so a future blueprint sync doesn't silently reset it back to free) and its env var block swapped `SENDGRID_API_KEY`/`EMAIL_FROM` for `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS`. `.env.example` updated to match, with the Gmail App Password requirement documented (a regular Google account password won't authenticate over SMTP once 2-Step Verification is enabled, which is now the default). `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s three "SendGrid" labels relabeled to "Gmail SMTP."

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (backend and frontend). Full backend suite 653/654 (the 1 failure is the same pre-existing unrelated `helpAssistant.test.ts` environmental mismatch noted in the prior two entries - a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set in this sandbox). Updated the 3 test files that toggled the old env var names (`health.test.ts`, `dunningReminder.test.ts`, `invoiceEmail.test.ts`) to toggle `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` instead - all still pass. **Not yet live-verified end-to-end** (an actual email arriving in an inbox) - that requires the user to generate a real Gmail App Password and set `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` in Render's Environment tab, which wasn't done as part of this pass; the TCP-reachability test above proves the network path is open, not that a full authenticated SMTP send succeeds.

**Files:** `backend/src/services/EmailService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/health.ts`, `backend/src/routes/adminIntegrations.ts`, `backend/src/routes/scheduledReports.ts` (comment only), `backend/src/tests/health.test.ts`, `dunningReminder.test.ts`, `invoiceEmail.test.ts`; `backend/.env.example`, `render.yaml`; `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Shop Manager: Access to Invoices (AR) and Vendor Bills (AP), Scoped to Their Own Warehouse

**What/Why:** User asked for Shop Manager to get access to the Invoices and Vendor Bills tabs "so that when someone comes to buy they can create for them" - i.e. a Shop Manager should be able to issue an invoice directly for a walk-in credit customer, and record an incoming vendor delivery, without routing every one through an Accountant. Shop Manager is a location-scoped role (Inventory/POS/Expense Claims only, per the earlier RBAC lockdown - see STATUS.md 2026-08-11-ish entries), so this needed careful scoping rather than a blanket grant.

**Backend:** `routes/invoices.ts`: `POST /customers`, `POST /` (create invoice), and `POST /:id/pay` (record payment) now accept `Shop Manager` alongside `Accountant`. The itemized/stock-deducting path of `POST /` now calls `assertWarehouseAccess` (same check already used by `inventory.ts`/`bills.ts`) so a Shop Manager can only issue a stock-backed invoice against a warehouse they're actually assigned to - a real gap, since this route previously never checked warehouse access at all (harmless while only Accountant/Admin could reach it, not harmless now). Every other invoice action (email, GRA clearance, credit notes) stays Accountant-only. `routes/bills.ts`: `POST /vendors` and `POST /` (create vendor bill) now accept `Shop Manager` too, also gated by the pre-existing `assertWarehouseAccess` check for itemized purchases - but a **new** restriction was added: a location-scoped role is now blocked outright from a lump-sum/simple vendor bill (`isLocationScopedRole` check, 403 with a clear message), since a lump-sum bill has no warehouse and no stock receipt to back it - letting a Shop Manager record an arbitrary vendor payable with nothing receivable tying it to a real delivery would be a real AP-integrity gap, not just a UX nicety. Paying/scheduling a vendor bill stays Accountant-only.

**Real bug caught during live verification, not just the intended change:** `Invoices.tsx`'s `fetchData()` and `VendorBills.tsx`'s `fetchData()` both fetch several endpoints via `Promise.all` (customers, tax rates, funds, warehouses, items) - and `GET /tax-rates` and `GET /funds` were both `requireRole('Viewer')`-gated, which Shop Manager (a different scoped role) failed. Since `Promise.all` rejects entirely if any member rejects, this meant the **whole** page's data silently failed to load for a Shop Manager the moment they had page access at all - no customers, no tax rates, no funds, effectively no usable form - even though the actual invoice/bill-creation routes were now open to them. Fixed by adding `Shop Manager` to both `GET /tax-rates` and `GET /funds`. This was only caught because live-verification (below) actually drove the real UI end-to-end instead of stopping at the backend route-permission check.

**Frontend:** `lib/navigation.ts`: added `/invoices` and `/bills` to `RESTRICTED_ROLE_NAV["shop manager"]` (Cashier's entry was deliberately left unchanged - only Shop Manager asked for this). No other frontend changes were needed - neither `Invoices.tsx` nor `VendorBills.tsx` had any role-based UI gating to begin with (they were simply unreachable for Shop Manager before this).

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc --noEmit` (frontend) both clean. New `shopManagerInvoicingAccess.test.ts` (14 tests): Shop Manager can add a customer, issue both an itemized (warehouse-scoped, deducts real stock) and Simple invoice, record payment on it, add a vendor, and record an itemized vendor bill (warehouse-scoped, receives real stock) - and is still blocked from every back-office action (email/GRA-clearance/credit-notes on invoices; pay/schedule on bills), from a lump-sum vendor bill, and from touching a warehouse they're not assigned to. Also asserts Cashier does **not** gain this access. `rbacLockdown.test.ts` updated (the old shared "blocked from issuing an invoice" assertion now branches per-role, since it's no longer true for Shop Manager). Full backend suite 654/654 logical passes (2 unrelated pre-existing environmental failures - `helpAssistant.test.ts`'s real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` present in this sandbox, and a flaky WebSocket timeout in `presenceSocket.test.ts` confirmed to pass in isolation). **Live-verified via Playwright** against the real dev servers: logged in as a real invited Shop Manager, confirmed the sidebar now shows Invoices/Vendor Bills, added a real walk-in customer through the UI, selected them in the Create Invoice modal, submitted, and confirmed the real `POST /invoices` call returned 201 and the invoice appeared in the list - this is exactly the run that surfaced and confirmed the tax-rates/funds bug above (the fix was verified by re-running the same script against the fixed backend).

**Files:** `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/routes/bills.ts`, `backend/src/routes/taxRates.ts`, `backend/src/routes/funds.ts`, `backend/src/middleware/rbacMiddleware.ts` (comment only); `backend/src/tests/shopManagerInvoicingAccess.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/rbacLockdown.test.ts`; `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Retired MoMo Direct (MTN) and TheTeller in Favor of Paystack Subaccounts (Direct Follow-Up)

**What/Why:** Direct same-day follow-up to the two Paystack Subaccounts entries immediately below. With Paystack Subaccounts now covering Mobile Money settlement (MTN/AirtelTigo/Telecel Cash) through the exact same platform-wide-key + per-tenant-subaccount flow already built for bank accounts, the separate MoMo Direct (MTN Collections API) and TheTeller (PaySwitch) integrations became functionally redundant - and strictly worse for a shop owner, since both still required completing an independent MTN or PaySwitch developer/merchant signup that most small shop owners couldn't clear, where Paystack's own checkout already natively supports all three networks with nothing beyond the bank/MoMo details a shop owner already has. Recommended retiring both to the user, who confirmed.

**Backend:** Deleted `momoService.ts`, `tellerService.ts`, `routes/momo.ts`, `routes/teller.ts`, and their test files outright (not deprecated/env-gated-off - genuinely removed, since Paystack fully supersedes them). Schema: dropped `MomoPaymentRequest`/`TellerPaymentRequest` models and their `Invoice` relations, and removed the 6 now-unused `Tenant` credential fields (`momoApiUser`, `momoSubscriptionKeyEncrypted`, `momoApiKeyEncrypted`, `tellerApiUsername`, `tellerMerchantId`, `tellerApiKeyEncrypted`) via a new migration (`20260817070000_remove_momo_teller_direct_integrations`) that drops both tables (159/212 rows of this sandbox's own test data, not real tenant data) and both column sets. Updated every remaining reference: `app.ts` (unmounted the two routers), `tenantRepository.ts` (`TenantRecord` interface), `routes/tenants.ts` (`sanitizeTenantForResponse`/`PUT /current`/audit diff fields), `routes/adminIntegrations.ts` (Integrations console adoption counts), `invoicePaymentService.ts` (`RecordInvoicePaymentOptions.method` narrowed to `'MANUAL' | 'PAYSTACK'`), `dataExportService.ts` (export manifest), `helpAssistantKnowledge.ts` (in-app Help Assistant's knowledge base).

**Frontend:** `types/tenant.ts`/`TenantSettingsContext.tsx` had the momo/teller fields removed from `TenantSettings`/`UpdateTenantSettingsDTO`/`DEFAULT_TENANT`/the fetch and save response mappings. `Settings.tsx`'s Payment Collection tab lost the "MTN MoMo Collections" and "TheTeller (PaySwitch)" cards entirely, along with their state, effects, and save handlers; the intro banner now describes Paystack as covering card, bank transfer, and all three Mobile Money networks in one place. `Invoices.tsx` lost the `MomoRequest`/`TellerRequest` interfaces, the `TELLER_NETWORKS` constant, both modals' state/handlers (`openMomoModal`/`handleSendMomoRequest`/`handleCheckMomoStatus`, `openTellerModal`/`handleSendTellerRequest`/`handleCheckTellerStatus`), the two "Collect via MoMo"/"Collect via Mobile Money (Other Networks)" action-menu buttons, and both full modal JSX blocks - leaving Paystack's "Pay Now Link" as the sole in-app collection action, which already covers the same networks.

Cleaned up six now-inaccurate cosmetic comments referencing the deleted integrations (`routes/invoices.ts`, `services/paystackService.ts`, `routes/paystack.ts`, `tests/graEvat.test.ts`, `tests/helpAssistant.test.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/syncEngine.ts`) - all reworded to describe the current Paystack-only reality rather than deleted files.

Full backend Jest suite: 639/640 passing (1 pre-existing environmental failure in `helpAssistant.test.ts` - asserts `configured: false` when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is unset, but this sandbox's `.env` genuinely has a real key set for earlier live-verification work this session; confirmed unrelated via `git diff`, since neither the help-assistant service/route nor its env handling were touched by this change). A repo-wide case-insensitive grep for `momo|teller` after all edits turns up only accurate remaining mentions of "MoMo" as a Mobile Money method name inside Paystack's own descriptions (e.g. "enters their bank or MoMo details" in `adminIntegrations.ts`/`Settings.tsx`/`types/tenant.ts`), not references to the removed integrations.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Paystack Subaccounts: Mobile Money as a Settlement Option (Direct Follow-Up)

**What/Why:** Same-day follow-up to the Paystack Subaccounts entry immediately below. User asked whether a shop owner without a bank account could use their Mobile Money number instead. Verified against Paystack's own API before building anything (not assumed): Paystack's `GET /bank` endpoint accepts a `type` param of `mobile_money` for Ghana, listing MTN/AirtelTigo/Telecel Cash as their own settlement destinations with real codes (`MTN`/`ATL`/`VOD`), and Paystack's own documentation confirms a subaccount settles "to a bank or mobile money account" via the exact same `settlement_bank`/`account_number` fields already used for bank accounts - so no new endpoint or data model was needed, just a channel toggle.

**Backend:** `paystackService.listBanks()` now takes a `channel: 'ghipss' | 'mobile_money'` parameter (defaults to `ghipss`/banks), passed straight through as Paystack's own `type` query param. `GET /paystack/banks?channel=mobile_money` exposes it - `resolveAccountNumber()`/`createSubaccount()` needed zero changes, since a MoMo number is just another `account_number`/`bank_code` pair to Paystack.

**Frontend:** New "Bank Account" / "Mobile Money" toggle at the top of the Paystack setup card in Settings - switching it re-fetches the real bank/provider list, relabels the fields ("Bank" → "Mobile Money Provider", "Account Number" → "Mobile Money Number"), and resets any in-progress entry. The verify/create-subaccount flow underneath is unchanged - Paystack's `POST /bank/resolve` and `POST /subaccount` don't care which channel the code came from.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. 2 new tests in `paystack.test.ts` (12 total, all passing) assert the `channel` query param flows through to Paystack's real `type` param correctly for both values. **Live-verified against Paystack's real, live API**: `GET /banks` (default) returned the real current 31-bank Ghana list; `GET /banks?channel=mobile_money` returned Paystack's real 3 providers (MTN, AirtelTigo, Vodafone) - genuinely different real data per channel, not a stub. Live-verified the frontend toggle via Playwright: default state shows the bank dropdown, clicking "Mobile Money" correctly relabels both fields and populates the dropdown with the same real MTN/AirtelTigo/Vodafone list fetched live from Paystack.

**Files:** `backend/src/services/paystackService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/paystack.ts`, `backend/src/tests/paystack.test.ts`; `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - Paystack: Switched From Per-Tenant Secret Keys to Real Paystack Subaccounts (Direct Follow-Up)

**What/Why:** Direct same-day follow-up to the per-tenant credentials entry immediately below. User raised a real usability gap in the per-tenant model just shipped: most small shop owners can't complete a Paystack developer signup and generate their own API secret key. Asked what other platforms do for this - the answer is Paystack's own **Subaccounts / Split Payments** feature (verified against Paystack's real API docs via web search, not guessed): a platform holds ONE Paystack account and creates a "subaccount" per merchant under it, referencing only the merchant's bank account details. Paystack itself then auto-splits and settles each payment directly to that merchant's bank account. This means a tenant now needs zero developer knowledge - just their own bank account number, something every business already has.

**Real API contract used (verified via Paystack's docs before writing any code):** `POST /subaccount` (`business_name`, `settlement_bank`, `account_number`, `percentage_charge` → response `subaccount_code` in the `ACCT_...` format), `GET /bank?country=ghana&currency=GHS&type=ghipss` (real bank list), `GET /bank/resolve?account_number=&bank_code=` (confirms the real account holder's name before committing), and `POST /transaction/initialize` with a `subaccount` field to route the split. `percentage_charge` is Ledgio's own platform cut (0-100, defaults to 0 - a real pricing decision deliberately left unset rather than invented).

**Architecture change:** `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` reverts to platform-wide (Ledgio's own account, not each tenant's) - the opposite of the MoMo/TheTeller pattern, because Paystack's own Subaccounts feature is what carries the per-tenant settlement, not a per-tenant API key. `Tenant.paystackSecretKeyEncrypted` (added earlier this session) is dropped entirely; replaced with `paystackSubaccountCode`/`paystackBankCode`/`paystackAccountNumber`/`paystackAccountName` - none of which are secrets, so nothing needs encrypting here at all, a real simplification over the encrypted-credential pattern every other integration uses.

**Backend:** `paystackService.ts` rewritten with `listBanks()`, `resolveAccountNumber()`, `createSubaccount()`, and `initializeTransaction()` now accepting an optional `subaccountCode`. Three new routes: `GET /paystack/banks`, `POST /paystack/resolve-account`, `POST /paystack/subaccount` (creates the real subaccount, persists the returned code, rejects a second attempt if already configured). The invoice-initialize route now gates on `isPaystackConfigured()` (platform) **and** `isSubaccountConfigured(tenant)` (per-tenant), passing `subaccountCode` through to `initializeTransaction()` so the real split actually happens. `adminIntegrations.ts`'s per-tenant Paystack adoption count now checks `paystackSubaccountCode` instead of the now-removed secret key field.

**Frontend:** Settings' Paystack card replaced entirely - no more secret-key field. A tenant now picks their bank from a real dropdown (fetched from `GET /paystack/banks`), enters their account number, clicks "Verify Account" (calls `POST /paystack/resolve-account` to show the real account holder's name back for confirmation before committing), then "Set Up Payment Collection" (calls `POST /paystack/subaccount`, then refreshes tenant settings via context so the configured state appears immediately).

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. `paystack.test.ts` fully rewritten (11 tests): the bank-setup endpoints, subaccount creation + real DB persistence + duplicate-rejection, and the full invoice pay-now-link → verify → journal-entry flow, all asserting the mocked Paystack calls receive the real expected fields (including that `POST /transaction/initialize` actually carries `subaccount: <this tenant's code>`, not another tenant's or none at all). Full suite 651/653 (2 pre-existing environmental failures unrelated to this change, confirmed via git status). **Live-verified against Paystack's real, live API** (not just mocks) using a throwaway test key: `GET /banks` returned Paystack's actual current 30-bank Ghana list (that endpoint doesn't require a valid key, since it's public reference data) proving the live wiring is real; `POST /resolve-account` and `POST /subaccount` both correctly received and surfaced Paystack's real `"Invalid key"` rejection with the fake key, proving the full request chain reaches Paystack's production servers and handles a real failure gracefully rather than crashing or faking success.

**Not done this pass:** the actual platform fee percentage remains 0 (`PAYSTACK_PLATFORM_FEE_PERCENT` env var, unset = 0) - a real pricing/business decision for the user to make, not something to invent; removing the now-fully-unused `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` per-tenant references from `.env.example` docs comments (harmless, deferred).

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260817041142_replace_paystack_secret_key_with_subaccount/` (new); `backend/src/services/paystackService.ts` (rewritten); `backend/src/routes/paystack.ts`, `tenants.ts`, `adminIntegrations.ts`; `backend/src/repository/tenantRepository.ts`; `backend/src/tests/paystack.test.ts` (rewritten); `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`, `frontend/src/types/tenant.ts`, `frontend/src/contexts/TenantSettingsContext.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-17] - MoMo/TheTeller/Paystack: Per-Tenant Payment-Collector Credentials (Closing a Real Money-Custody Gap)

**What/Why:** User asked whether Mobile Money should stay platform-wide or become per-tenant, "and how about the rest of the integrations, can we do same for them." Investigating the current state surfaced a real problem, not just an architectural preference: MoMo, TheTeller, and Paystack all previously used one shared platform-wide secret (`MOMO_SUBSCRIPTION_KEY`/`MOMO_API_USER`/`MOMO_API_KEY`, `TELLER_*`, `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY`), meaning every tenant's customer payments settled into one Ledgio-controlled merchant account with no settlement/payout mechanism anywhere in this codebase to route money back out to the actual tenant. That is payment-aggregation exposure, not just a code smell - moved to per-tenant credentials instead (same pattern already proven for GRA E-VAT), so each tenant's customer payments settle directly to their own merchant account.

**Scope decision - Mono excluded, deliberately:** Mono (bank-feed reconciliation) was investigated and left alone. Its `MONO_SECRET_KEY` is a platform-level API-partner key (the same pattern as Plaid) - it never touches money, only reads a tenant's own already-linked bank statement, and each linked account is already tenant-scoped (`BankAccount.tenantId`). No money-custody problem exists there, so it wasn't touched.

**Backend:** Added `Tenant.momoApiUser`/`momoSubscriptionKeyEncrypted`/`momoApiKeyEncrypted`, `tellerApiUsername`/`tellerMerchantId`/`tellerApiKeyEncrypted`, `paystackSecretKeyEncrypted` (migration `20260817025138_add_per_tenant_payment_collector_credentials`) - identifiers stored plaintext, actual keys AES-256-GCM ciphertext via the existing `credentialEncryption.ts`, same convention as GRA's `graSecurityKeyEncrypted`. `momoService.ts`/`tellerService.ts`/`paystackService.ts` rewritten so every exported function takes the tenant's (still-encrypted) credential fields and decrypts internally - mirrors `graEvatService.requestClearance()`'s existing pattern rather than inventing a new one. MoMo's access-token cache changed from one global variable to a `Map` keyed by `apiUser`, since a single cache would have leaked one tenant's bearer token into another tenant's requests. `routes/momo.ts`/`teller.ts`/`paystack.ts` now fetch the real tenant record via `tenantRepository.findTenantById` before every call instead of reading `process.env`. `routes/tenants.ts`: `PUT /current` accepts the new write-only credential fields (encrypts on save, `undefined` leaves the stored value untouched, `''` clears it); `sanitizeTenantForResponse()` strips every encrypted field and adds `momoConfigured`/`tellerConfigured`/`paystackConfigured` booleans, same contract as `graSecurityKeyConfigured`. `adminIntegrations.ts`: moved MoMo/TheTeller/Paystack out of the `platformWide` list into `perTenant` (real per-integration adoption counts via `prisma.tenant.count()`), leaving only genuinely platform-wide integrations (email, SMS, Mono, FX, AI help assistant, credential encryption itself) in that section.

**Frontend:** New "Payment Collection" tab in Settings with one card per integration (MTN MoMo, TheTeller, Paystack), each with its own write-only masked secret field and "already on file" indicator - same UX pattern as the GRA VSDC credentials card. `types/tenant.ts`/`TenantSettingsContext.tsx` extended with the new fields/booleans, applying the same fix already made for GRA's `graSecurityKeyConfigured` (pull server-computed `*Configured` booleans from the real API response after save, not the echoed request DTO) to all three new fields up front, rather than rediscovering that bug three more times. AdminCoreEngine's Integrations tab needed no code changes - it already renders `perTenant`/`platformWide` generically from whatever the API returns.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. `momo.test.ts`/`teller.test.ts`/`paystack.test.ts` fully rewritten (20 tests total) to set/clear real per-tenant credentials directly on the Tenant row (same as a real tenant saving them in Settings) instead of toggling env vars, plus new assertions that the mocked HTTP calls actually used *this* tenant's decrypted credentials, not a stale platform-wide value. Full backend suite: 645/648 passing: the 3 failures (`presenceSocket.test.ts`, `stockTake.test.ts`, `helpAssistant.test.ts`) are pre-existing environmental flakiness unrelated to this change (confirmed via `git status` - none of those files were touched this session; `stockTake` fails on a literal date-string coincidence since today's real date is "17 August 2026" and the test asserts the PDF text excludes "17"). Live-verified via Playwright against the real dev servers with a freshly onboarded tenant: filled in MoMo credentials in the new Settings tab, saved, confirmed the "already on file" badge appeared immediately without a reload (proving the context-update fix), confirmed it survived a full page reload (proving real backend persistence via `GET /tenants/current`), and confirmed the plaintext secret never appears anywhere in the rendered page.

**Not done this pass:** actually rotating/removing the old platform-wide env vars (`MOMO_SUBSCRIPTION_KEY` etc.) from `.env`/`.env.production`/CI - left in place since they're now simply unused by application code, not a live security exposure, and removing them is a deployment-config change better done deliberately rather than as a side effect of this PR.

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260817025138_add_per_tenant_payment_collector_credentials/` (new); `backend/src/services/momoService.ts`, `tellerService.ts`, `paystackService.ts` (rewritten); `backend/src/routes/momo.ts`, `teller.ts`, `paystack.ts`, `tenants.ts`, `adminIntegrations.ts`; `backend/src/repository/tenantRepository.ts`; `backend/src/tests/momo.test.ts`, `teller.test.ts`, `paystack.test.ts` (rewritten); `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`, `frontend/src/types/tenant.ts`, `frontend/src/contexts/TenantSettingsContext.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-16] - First Real Load Test (Partial - Stopped Before Finding the Real Ceiling)

**What/Why:** User asked "how many businesses can the system take" - the honest answer at the time was "no hard limit in code, but the only capacity numbers in this repo (`backend/PERFORMANCE_AUDIT_COMPLETE.md`'s 5,000 req/sec / 10,000 concurrent users) are unverified claims from before this session, not something anyone actually load-tested." User asked for a real load test before piloting. This entry documents what was actually run, against this sandbox's own resources (4 CPU / 15GB RAM, single Postgres + single Node process, `NODE_ENV=production` build) - not production infrastructure, so absolute throughput numbers here don't transfer directly to a real deployment, but the methodology and any real bugs/behaviors found do.

**Methodology:** Built the real production bundle (`npm run build && node dist/index.js`, not the dev server) so the test reflects real production code paths, not `ts-node-dev`'s transpile overhead. Onboarded real tenants through the real public `POST /tenants/onboard` API (not direct DB inserts), each seeded with real accounts, a customer, an inventory item/warehouse, and a few invoices, so requests hit real non-empty data. Wrote a custom Node load driver (not autocannon - needed per-tenant JWT + `X-Tenant-ID` auth headers and a weighted mix of real endpoints, which a single-URL tool doesn't model) simulating one active user per tenant with human-like think-time (400-1500ms between actions) issuing a realistic weighted mix of requests (mostly GET /invoices, /ledgers/summary, /reports/kpis, /accounts, /inventory/items, /invoices/customers, /reports/balance-sheet, plus real `POST /invoices` writes).

**Real finding #1 - the per-tenant API rate limiter works correctly:** An initial naive test (5 workers hammering a pool of 15 tenants as fast as possible, no think-time) immediately produced a 63% error rate - all `429 Too Many Requests`. This is not a bug: `apiRateLimiter` genuinely caps each tenant at 100 requests/minute (`rateLimiterMiddleware.ts`), keyed per-tenant when available, and it did exactly its job protecting the system from a single client/tenant flooding it. This reframed the test - the real question isn't "how fast can one client hit the API," it's "how many concurrently active tenants, each behaving like a real user, can the server handle" - so the driver was rewritten to model that instead.

**Real finding #2 - the system handled the realistic tests trivially:** 15 concurrently active businesses (real users, realistic pacing) for 30s: 0 errors, p50 5.6ms, p95 16.7ms, p99 21.7ms. 40 concurrently active businesses for 45s: 0 errors, p50 4.7ms, p95 15.3ms, p99 18.9ms - the server was barely working at either level; latency didn't meaningfully change going from 15 to 40 concurrent businesses.

**Not completed - the real ceiling was never found:** to push past 40 concurrent businesses without each individual tenant tripping its own 100/min cap, more tenants were needed (not more load per tenant). Seeded 15 more via the real HTTP endpoint (respecting its own 5-signups/60s rate limiter, 40 total), then started seeding 60 more via a script calling `onboardTenant()` directly (bypassing only the public-signup abuse limiter, which protects the signup form specifically, not something operational-capacity testing needs to respect - still going through the real HTTP API for every other write) to reach ~100 tenants for a bigger concurrent-business run. The user asked to stop before that run completed ("this is taking too long, it's fine, end it"). All test tenants (104 total, both HTTP-seeded and directly-seeded) and their Postgres schemas were cleaned up via `deleteTenantBySlug`/`dropTenantSchema` before stopping - nothing test-related was left in the database. The backend dev/prod processes started for this test were also stopped.

**Honest conclusion:** the system comfortably handles 40 concurrently active real-usage businesses on a single 4-CPU sandbox instance with room to spare (sub-20ms p99, zero errors) - that's a real, verified floor, not a marketing number. The actual ceiling (where CPU/DB/connection-pool contention would start degrading latency) was not found and remains unknown - the old `PERFORMANCE_AUDIT_COMPLETE.md` numbers (5,000 req/sec, 10,000 concurrent users) are still unverified. A real conclusive capacity test - ideally against a copy of actual production infrastructure, not this sandbox - is still the honest next step before trusting a specific tenant-count ceiling for piloting at scale.

**Files:** No code changes - this was a test-only session. Test scripts and raw results live in the session scratchpad (`/tmp/.../scratchpad/loadtest_*.cjs`, `loadtest_results*.jsonl`), not committed to the repo (throwaway test tooling, not a supported load-testing harness).

## [Date: 2026-08-16] - GRA E-VAT / VSDC Certified Invoicing: Real API Integration (Direct Follow-Up)

**What/Why:** Direct same-day follow-up to the honest-scaffolding entry below. The user supplied GRA's actual Postman API documentation ("GRA E-VAT API - VER 8.2", `documenter.getpostman.com/view/29596173/2s9YJgSKXe`) - the real, complete wire-level specification (endpoint, auth header, full request/response JSON schema) that the scaffolding pass had correctly identified as unobtainable without GRA's own onboarding. This resolves the blocker documented below: `graEvatService.requestClearance()` now makes a real HTTP call instead of always throwing.

**Architecture note:** unlike MoMo/TheTeller/Paystack (one platform-wide secret), GRA assigns each taxpayer (tenant) their own TIN, Device/Branch Number, and `security_key` directly during onboarding - so this needed real per-tenant encrypted credential storage, a first for this codebase. New `utils/credentialEncryption.ts` (AES-256-GCM, keyed by a new required `CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY` env var) encrypts `Tenant.graSecurityKeyEncrypted` at rest; the plaintext key is never persisted and never returned by any API response once saved (`sanitizeTenantForResponse()` strips it from every tenant-returning route - GET/PUT `/tenants/current`, the admin tenant list, and the admin tier-update endpoint - replacing it with a `graSecurityKeyConfigured` boolean).

**Backend:** `graEvatService.buildInvoicePayload()` maps this system's real Invoice/InvoiceItem/Customer/`taxBreakdown` data onto GRA's exact documented schema - not a guess, but grounded in real facts about how this codebase computes tax: `calculationType` is always `"EXCLUSIVE"` (routes/invoices.ts always computes `total = subtotal + tax`, never bakes tax into unitPrice); NHIL/GETFund levy amounts (`levyAmountA`/`B`) are read from `Invoice.taxBreakdown`'s snapshotted per-component amounts (the existing Ghana VAT preset feature) and allocated across line items proportionally by each line's share of the subtotal, since this system has no per-line tax composition - a disclosed approximation, not GRA's own rule. COVID/CST/Tourism levies (`C`/`D`/`E`) are always 0 since no tax preset in this codebase computes them - a real, documented scope gap, not a bug. `taxType` (FLAT vs STANDARD, optional in GRA's schema) is omitted entirely rather than guessed. New `Customer.tin` field (`businessPartnerTin`) falls back to `"C0000000000"` for a walk-in/cash customer with none on file - not invented, copied directly from GRA's own official sample request payloads, which use that exact literal for the same case. `POST /invoices/:id/gra-clearance` now fetches the real tenant credentials + full invoice (customer, items, linked inventory SKUs) and calls the real service; a GRA rejection or network/auth failure still marks the invoice `FAILED` with GRA's real error text, never fabricated. Scoped to new-sale clearance only (`flag: "INVOICE"`) - GRA's schema also documents PURCHASE/REFUND/PARTIAL_REFUND/PURCHASE_RETURN/REFUND_CANCELLATION flows for credit-note-driven reversals, deliberately not built this pass (a real, separate follow-up, not silently assumed to work).

**Frontend:** Settings' "GRA E-VAT" tab gained a "VSDC API Credentials" card (Device Number + a write-only, masked Security Key field showing "already on file" once configured) and the "E-VAT Certification Status" card now reflects real configured/not-configured state instead of a blanket "can't succeed yet." Found and fixed a real bug while live-verifying this: `TenantSettingsContext.updateSettings()`'s local state merge blindly echoed the request DTO back into state, which worked for fields the server just persists as-sent (`graTin`) but silently failed to reflect `graSecurityKeyConfigured` (a server-computed field, never part of the request) until a full page reload - fixed by pulling that one field from the real API response instead of the echoed request. Invoices page's Add Customer modal gained a TIN field.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. `graEvat.test.ts` fully rewritten (10 tests, mocking GRA's VSDC API itself per CLAUDE.md's "mocking external services is allowed for unit testing" - proving this codebase's own request-building and response-mapping logic against the real documented schema, not GRA's actual server): asserts the exact request URL/headers/payload sent (`POST https://vsdcstaging.vat-gh.com/vsdc/api/v1/taxpayer/{TIN}-{device}/invoice`, `security_key` header, `flag: "INVOICE"`, `calculationType: "EXCLUSIVE"`), a real success maps every GRA response field correctly and renders a real QR code image, a real GRA rejection or network/auth error marks the invoice FAILED with the genuine error text, the cash-customer TIN fallback, credential encryption round-trips, and that the security key never leaks through any tenant-returning endpoint. No regressions in 38 related existing tests. Live-verified via Playwright against the real dev servers: saved real VSDC credentials in the new Settings card, confirmed the certification-status card correctly flips to "Credentials on file," and confirmed the Security Key field correctly shows "already on file" after a reload (this is what caught the `updateSettings` merge bug above).

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260816032859_add_gra_evat_credentials_and_customer_tin/` (new); `backend/src/utils/credentialEncryption.ts` (new); `backend/src/services/graEvatService.ts` (rewritten); `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`; `backend/src/repository/tenantRepository.ts` (TenantRecord interface extended to match real schema); `backend/.env`, `backend/.env.example`; `backend/src/tests/graEvat.test.ts` (rewritten); `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/types/tenant.ts`, `frontend/src/contexts/TenantSettingsContext.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-16] - GRA E-VAT / VSDC Certified Invoicing: Honest Scaffolding (No Fabricated API Contract)

**What/Why:** User asked to integrate GRA (Ghana Revenue Authority) E-VAT/VSDC electronic invoicing. Research (WebSearch/WebFetch, a headless-browser attempt against a third-party API-docs mirror that was itself blocked by that site's own bot detection, and finally the official 16-page "GHANA REVENUE AUTHORITY GUIDELINES ON CERTIFIED INVOICING SYSTEM (E-VAT)" PDF, Guideline GRA/AG/2024/005, dated 18 June 2024) established that this integration is genuinely different from MoMo/TheTeller/Paystack, all of which have open public developer documentation: GRA does not publish a self-serve API spec. Per the guideline's own Section 10(e), "GRA will provide API documentation to the taxpayer" only during GRA's own bespoke onboarding process (an assigned Relationship Manager, an onboarding form, joint testing, GRA sign-off, then go-live). Building the real wire-level call today would mean inventing field names and endpoints, directly violating this codebase's own "No Mock Data Ever" / "Spec-Driven Development: forbidden from inventing schema or API endpoints" rules - and could actively mislead a real taxpayer into shipping a broken integration. Asked the user via `AskUserQuestion` how to proceed; they chose "Build the honest scaffolding now" - everything that can be honestly built without guessing the API contract.

**Backend:** `Invoice` gains 7 new fields (`graClearanceStatus` enum [NOT_REQUESTED/PENDING/CLEARED/FAILED], `graVerificationEngineId`, `graQrCodeData`, `graSignature`, `graEncryptedData`, `graClearedAt`, `graClearanceError`) covering the legally-required certified-invoice elements per the guideline's Section 7(3). New `graEvatService.ts`: `requestClearance()` throws unconditionally (not env-gated like the other integrations, since even a configured connection has no known wire format to call against) with a message citing the real GRA onboarding contacts; `renderClearanceQrCode()` reuses the existing `generateQrCodeDataUrl` helper built for MFA TOTP setup, since QR rendering is payload-agnostic and needs no GRA-specific knowledge. New `POST /invoices/:id/gra-clearance` (Accountant+) calls `requestClearance()`, always fails today, and persists the failed attempt (`graClearanceStatus: FAILED` + the real explanation) on the invoice so it's visible in the UI - same honesty pattern as a declined MoMo/Paystack attempt, never a faked success. Rejects re-requesting clearance on an already-`CLEARED` invoice. Also closed a small pre-existing gap found while building this: `Tenant.graTin`/`vatRegistered` (added earlier for onboarding) were write-once with no correction path - `PUT /tenants/current` now accepts and persists both going forward.

**Frontend:** Invoices page gained a "Request GRA Clearance" action (shown once an invoice leaves DRAFT and isn't already cleared) that surfaces the real error via a toast. Settings gained a new "GRA E-VAT" tab: an editable TIN/VAT-registered card, and an "E-VAT Certification Status" card with an honest "Not certified" explanation, the real numbered onboarding process from the guideline, and real GRA contact channels (support@evatgra.zendesk.com, GRA Contact Centre numbers, WhatsApp, a link to the official guideline PDF) - all sourced directly from the primary document, nothing invented.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `graEvat.test.ts` (5 tests): a clearance request always returns a real 501 with the exact honest explanation and marks the invoice `FAILED` (never `CLEARED`); a nonexistent invoice 404s; an already-`CLEARED` invoice (seeded directly, since nothing can organically reach that state yet) is rejected with 400; `PUT /tenants/current` persists and returns `graTin`/`vatRegistered`; an empty-string TIN clears it. All 5 pass, plus no regressions in `invoicesAndBills.test.ts`/`tillCloseSmsAlert.test.ts`/`tenantOnboarding.test.ts`/`paystack.test.ts` (24/24). Live-verified via Playwright against the real dev servers: filled and saved a real TIN on the new Settings tab (confirmed it persists across reload), confirmed the full onboarding-guidance/contacts card renders, created a real invoice and clicked "Request GRA Clearance" on `/invoices`, and confirmed the real 501 explanation surfaces as a toast exactly as designed - the honest-failure path works end to end through the actual UI, not just the API.

**What remains blocked and why:** `graEvatService.requestClearance()` cannot be completed until GRA hands a real taxpayer its actual API documentation during their own onboarding process - this is an external, non-technical blocker (contact GRA to begin), not a gap in this codebase. The schema, route, and QR renderer are already in place so wiring the real HTTP call in becomes an isolated, one-function change once that documentation exists.

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260816030043_add_gra_evat_invoice_fields/` (new); `backend/src/services/graEvatService.ts` (new); `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`; `backend/src/tests/graEvat.test.ts` (new); `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`, `frontend/src/types/tenant.ts`, `frontend/src/contexts/TenantSettingsContext.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-15] - Fixed Asset Management + Depreciation (Straight-Line and Reducing-Balance), New Investing Activities Section on the Cash Flow Statement

**What/Why:** User asked to build the Fixed Asset Management + Depreciation item from the research roadmap (item 18) - a genuine gap: buying a long-lived asset (a vehicle, equipment, furniture) had no way to be distinguished from an ordinary expense, so its full cost hit the P&L in one month instead of being spread over its useful life, no fixed asset ever appeared on the Balance Sheet, and the Cash Flow Statement had no Investing section at all (a documented gap from when that report first shipped) since nothing distinguished a capex purchase from ordinary working-capital movement.

**Schema (tenant-schema, raw SQL migration 010):** new `accounts.is_fixed_asset` boolean, mirroring `is_cash_equivalent`'s design (multiple accounts can hold the flag, unlike `default_role`'s one-per-role constraint) - drives the new Cash Flow Investing section. Extended the `default_role` CHECK constraint to also allow two new roles, `DEPRECIATION_EXPENSE` and `ACCUMULATED_DEPRECIATION`, reusing the exact same "explicit tenant designation, resolved via `resolveDefaultAccount`" mechanism built for the earlier CASH/REVENUE/EXPENSE default-role fix - a tenant designates one Depreciation Expense account and one Accumulated Depreciation account (once, in Chart of Accounts) and every asset's monthly postings target them. **Two real bugs found and fixed while building this, both in already-shipped code paths this feature newly exercised:** (1) migration 009's own `default_role` CHECK-constraint-existence lookup (`WHERE conname = 'chk_account_default_role'`) checks `pg_constraint` without scoping to the current schema's own table - `conname` isn't unique database-wide, so in a database with many tenant schemas it can false-positive against some *other* tenant's identically-named constraint. This was harmless for 009 (worst case, silently skipped adding the constraint), but migration 010's DROP+ADD pattern surfaced it as a hard failure; fixed by scoping both migrations' lookups to `conrelid = 'accounts'::regclass`. (2) `default_role VARCHAR(20)` (set by migration 009) is too narrow for `'ACCUMULATED_DEPRECIATION'` (25 characters) - designating that role would 500 silently in a way that let depreciation postings fall through to a wrong-but-plausible account instead of the intended one, caught only because a test asserted on the real ledger balances of the specific designated accounts rather than trusting the FixedAsset row's own tracked totals. Fixed by widening the column to VARCHAR(30) in the same migration, before it was ever written for real.

**Backend:** new `FixedAsset`/`DepreciationEntry` models (shared schema, plain-string soft references to Account/JournalEntry - same pattern as `PettyCashEntry`, since Account/JournalEntry live in each tenant's raw-SQL schema). `fixedAssetService.createFixedAsset()` posts a real acquisition journal entry immediately (Debit the chosen asset account, Credit the chosen payment account for the full cost) - deliberately scoped to "paid for immediately" (cash/bank/MoMo), not integrated with vendor credit terms, matching how most Ghana SME capital purchases are actually paid; also auto-flips `isFixedAsset` on the chosen asset account so the Cash Flow Statement picks it up without a separate manual toggle. `depreciateOneAsset()` computes one period's depreciation (Straight-Line: `(cost - residualValue) / usefulLifeMonths`; Reducing-Balance: an explicit annual rate applied to net book value each month, not derived from residual value) and posts a real journal entry, capped so `accumulatedDepreciation` never exceeds `cost - residualValue` - the asset flips to `FULLY_DEPRECIATED` once it hits that cap. Depreciation starts the month *after* acquisition (documented simplification, avoids partial-month proration). New daily `FixedAssetDepreciationCronService` sweeps every tenant's due assets, with its own per-asset try/catch (mirrors `RecurringInvoiceCronService`'s per-item loop) so one misconfigured asset (e.g. the two default-role accounts never designated) never blocks the rest of a tenant's sweep - it's silently skipped and logged, never faked. `disposeFixedAsset()` stops all further depreciation; no write-off/gain-or-loss journal entry in this pass (documented scope simplification, same category as Credit Notes being "financial correction only"). New `fixedAssets.ts` routes (`GET /`, `GET /:id`, `POST /`, `PUT /:id/dispose`).

**Cash Flow Statement gains a real Investing Activities section**, closing the gap documented since that report first shipped: `reportRepository.getCashFlowStatement()` now routes `is_fixed_asset`-flagged ASSET accounts' balance changes into a new `investingAdjustments`/`netCashFromInvesting` bucket instead of lumping them into Operating - `netChangeInCash` and the `cashTies` reconciliation check both updated accordingly. Extended to the PDF/Word exports (`pdfGenerationService.ts`, `reportDocxService.ts`) so the new section appears in downloaded reports, not just the on-screen view. The Balance Sheet needed **no code changes at all** - Fixed Asset and Accumulated Depreciation are just ordinary ASSET-type accounts, so they already net together automatically through the existing generic per-type summation (Accumulated Depreciation's credit-heavy balance shows as a negative ASSET line, correctly reducing `totalAssets`).

**Frontend:** new `/fixed-assets` page (asset register with cost/accumulated depreciation/net book value/status columns, an Add Fixed Asset modal with method-conditional fields, a depreciation-schedule detail view, and a Dispose action), new "Fixed Assets" nav item under Finance & Banking (also added to the Auditor's restricted nav allowlist). Chart of Accounts gained an "Is Fixed Asset" checkbox (mirrors the existing "Is Cash Equivalent" one) and the existing "Default Posting" badge mechanism was generalized from one-role-per-account-type to a list, since ASSET and EXPENSE types can now each hold two distinct roles (CASH/ACCUMULATED_DEPRECIATION and EXPENSE/DEPRECIATION_EXPENSE respectively) rather than the previous one-role-per-type assumption. Cash Flow Statement page gained an Investing Activities section matching the existing Operating/Financing ones.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `fixedAssets.test.ts` (9 tests): acquisition posts a real journal entry and auto-flips `isFixedAsset`, rejects a non-ASSET asset account, straight-line depreciation posts the correct amount each month and caps exactly at fully-depreciated (verified against real ledger balances on the specific designated accounts, not just the FixedAsset row's own tracked totals - the assertion that caught bug #2 above), reducing-balance depreciation correctly applies the rate to the shrinking net book value each month, disposal stops further depreciation, double-disposal is rejected, an unconfigured tenant's depreciation attempt throws a clear error, the cron's per-asset resilience absorbs that same error without crashing the sweep, and the Cash Flow Statement's Investing section reflects a real fixed asset purchase. Full backend suite 636/638 (the two failures are the same pre-existing, unrelated environmental issues noted in every recent entry: a flaky WebSocket timing test and a stale `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`-unconfigured assumption). Live-verified via Playwright against the real dev servers: created real Chart of Accounts entries and designated both new default-role accounts, confirmed the new badges render correctly on `/accounts`, created a real fixed asset through the actual Add Fixed Asset modal (all fields, account pickers included), confirmed the acquisition posted and the asset register displays it correctly, and confirmed the Cash Flow Statement's new Investing Activities section shows the real "-$4,000.00" outflow for the purchased Vehicles account.

**Files:** `backend/src/database/migrations/tenantMigrations.ts` (migration 10, plus the `conrelid`-scoping fix), `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/add_fixed_assets/` (new); `backend/src/repository/accountRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/accountService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/accounts.ts`; `backend/src/services/{fixedAssetService,fixedAssetDepreciationCronService}.ts` (new); `backend/src/routes/fixedAssets.ts` (new); `backend/src/repository/reportRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/{pdfGenerationService,reportDocxService}.ts`, `backend/src/routes/reports.ts`; `backend/src/index.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`; `backend/src/tests/{fixedAssets,accountDefaultRole,accountingSchema,cashFlow}.test.ts`; `frontend/src/pages/fixedassets/FixedAssets.tsx` (new); `frontend/src/pages/accounts/ChartOfAccounts.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/accounts/AccountForm.tsx`, `frontend/src/hooks/useAccounts.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/syncEngine.ts`, `frontend/src/types/accounting.ts`; `frontend/src/hooks/useCashFlow.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/reports/CashFlowStatement.tsx`; `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-15] - Eight "Smaller, Ready-to-Scope" Roadmap Items: AR/AP Aging, Credit Limits, Petty Cash, Purchase Orders, Auto-Reorder, Vendor Payment Scheduling, Recurring Invoices, Paystack Pay-Now Links

**What/Why:** User asked to build all of the "smaller, ready to scope" items still open in `docs/GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`'s Section 4 in one batch, rather than one at a time. Two items needed a human decision before starting - resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: Paystack chosen as the payment gateway for invoice pay-now links, and Check Printing explicitly skipped (low priority, no demand evidence, user picked "Skip it"). That left exactly 8 features, built end-to-end (schema/migration, service, route, frontend page, tests) one at a time, each independently `tsc`-checked and test-passing before moving to the next, with one consolidated verification/live-check/docs/commit/PR pass at the end given the batch's size.

**1. AP/AR Aging Analysis** - new `agingReportService.ts` (`getArAging`/`getApAging`), bucketing outstanding invoices/bills into current/1-30/31-60/61-90/90+ days past due. AR uses `total - amountPaid` (respects partial payments); AP uses the full `amount` (vendor bills in this schema are all-or-nothing, no partial-bill-payment support exists). New `GET /reports/aging/ar` and `/ap` (Viewer+). New `AgingReport.tsx` page (AR/AP toggle, 6 summary tiles, bucketed table), new "AP/AR Aging" nav item under Reports & Analytics (also added to the Auditor's restricted nav allowlist).

**2. Customer Credit Limits** - new optional `Customer.creditLimit` column. `POST /invoices` (create) now blocks a new invoice if the customer's current outstanding balance (SENT/PARTIALLY_PAID invoices) plus the new invoice's total would exceed their limit - skipped entirely for an idempotent `clientTxnId` replay of an already-created invoice, so a retried request never double-counts itself against its own limit. New `PUT /customers/:id`. Add Customer modal and the Customer interface gained the credit limit field.

**3. Petty Cash Logs** - new `PettyCashEntry` model (disbursement/replenishment, tenant-scoped). `pettyCashService.recordPettyCashEntry()` posts a real 2-line journal entry (mirrors the existing Contra Voucher pattern) then logs the entry; `listPettyCashEntries()` returns entries oldest-first with a running balance. New `GET`/`POST /petty-cash`, new `PettyCash.tsx` page (account picker, current balance, entry history, record-entry modal), new "Petty Cash" nav item.

**4. Purchase Order entity + PO-vs-bill matching** - new `PurchaseOrder`/`PurchaseOrderLine` models (DRAFT → SENT → BILLED/CANCELLED; BILLED can only be reached by billing against the PO, not set manually) and `VendorBill.purchaseOrderId`. `purchaseOrderService.computePoVsBillVariance()` is a lightweight rule-based quantity/cost variance flag (not a strict blocking 3-way match). `POST /bills` now accepts an optional `purchaseOrderId`, marks the linked PO BILLED in the same transaction, and returns the variance. New `purchaseOrders.ts` routes and `PurchaseOrders.tsx` page (PO list, create-PO modal, "Create Bill from PO" modal with a variance warning), new "Purchase Orders" nav item.

**5. Auto-generate PO on reorder threshold** - new `InventoryItem.preferredVendorId`. `purchaseOrderService.generateReorderPurchaseOrders()` finds every `WarehouseStock` row at or below its item's `reorderLevel`, groups by preferred vendor (one PO per vendor), and orders enough of each item to reach `reorderLevel` - a simple, explainable target, not a demand forecast. Items with no preferred vendor are skipped and reported back rather than silently dropped. New `POST /purchase-orders/generate-for-reorder`; `WarehouseManagement.tsx`'s Add Item form gained a Preferred Vendor picker; `PurchaseOrders.tsx` gained a "Generate Reorder POs" button.

**6. Automated vendor payment scheduling** - extracted the existing inline `POST /bills/:id/pay` logic into a shared `vendorBillPaymentService.payVendorBill()` so the manual pay button and the new cron post through the exact same code (this codebase's "one function owns all money movement" principle) rather than duplicating it. New `VendorBill.scheduledPaymentDate`; new `PUT /bills/:id/schedule-payment`. New daily `VendorPaymentSchedulingCronService` (8am UTC) auto-pays any bill whose scheduled date has arrived. `VendorBills.tsx` gained a Schedule button/modal.

**7. Recurring Customer Invoices** - new parallel `RecurringInvoice` model (deliberately not an extension of the existing generic `RecurringTransaction`, which only ever produces bare journal entries and has no customer concept - this was the research doc's own suggested approach). `recurringInvoiceService.generateInvoiceFromRecurring()` builds subtotal/tax/total from the template's JSON `items`, resolves tax and cross-currency conversion (falls back to a null base-currency snapshot rather than blocking generation if `FX_RATE_API_KEY` isn't configured, matching this app's env-gated-integration honesty convention), creates a real Simple Invoice (no itemized/stock-deducting lines - matches the doc's own suggested scope), and advances `nextRun`. New daily `RecurringInvoiceCronService` (6am UTC). New `recurringInvoices.ts` routes (create, list, active/inactive toggle - no general in-place template editor, since retroactively changing an already-generated invoice's shape is ambiguous; delete-and-recreate is the path for real edits) and new `RecurringInvoices.tsx` page, mirroring `RecurringTransactions.tsx`'s existing settings-page pattern. New "Recurring Invoices" nav item (also added to the Auditor's restricted nav allowlist).

**8. Paystack Pay-Now Link on Invoices** - new `paystackService.ts`, following the same env-gated pattern as `momoService.ts`/`tellerService.ts` exactly (`isPaystackConfigured()` on `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY`, `PaystackServiceError` statusCode 503 when unconfigured, no faked checkout link ever). New `PaystackPaymentRequest` model (mirrors `TellerPaymentRequest`'s shape - one row per generated hosted-checkout link). `POST /paystack/invoices/:id/initialize` generates the link for the invoice's outstanding balance; `POST /paystack/requests/:reference/verify` calls Paystack's real verify API (never trusts a client-side "I paid" claim) and, only on a genuine confirmed success, marks the invoice paid through the same shared `invoicePaymentService.recordInvoicePayment()` the manual pay route, MoMo, and TheTeller all already use (`method` union extended with `'PAYSTACK'`). Scoped deliberately to this accountant-initiated generate-then-verify flow (matching TheTeller's existing "Check Status" pattern) rather than also building a public unauthenticated webhook/customer-return page, which would be materially more infrastructure than what a "smaller, ready-to-scope" item called for. `Invoices.tsx` gained a "Pay Now Link (Paystack)" action (generate, copy link, verify) alongside the existing MoMo/TheTeller collection actions.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean throughout, checked after each feature. New test files: `agingReport.test.ts` (5), `customerCreditLimit.test.ts` (4), `pettyCash.test.ts` (5), `purchaseOrders.test.ts` (5), `reorderPurchaseOrders.test.ts` (5), `vendorPaymentScheduling.test.ts` (4), `recurringInvoices.test.ts` (5), `paystack.test.ts` (6) - 39 new tests, all passing. Full backend suite 627/629 (the two failures are both pre-existing and environmental, unrelated to this batch: the same flaky WebSocket-timing test in `presenceSocket.test.ts` noted in prior entries, and `helpAssistant.test.ts`'s "unconfigured" assumption going stale because this sandbox's `.env` now has a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set for unrelated reasons). Live-verified via Playwright against the real dev servers: onboarded a fresh tenant, confirmed all 4 new pages (AP/AR Aging, Petty Cash, Purchase Orders, Recurring Invoices) render their real empty states correctly with working sidebar navigation; the Paystack flow was verified through the backend integration test suite (mocked Paystack API, real DB/ledger assertions) rather than live, since this sandbox has no real Paystack credentials.
