**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`; migrations `add_customer_credit_limit`, `add_petty_cash_entries`, `add_purchase_orders`, `add_inventory_item_preferred_vendor`, `add_vendor_bill_scheduled_payment_date`, `add_recurring_invoices`, `add_paystack_payment_requests` (all new); `backend/src/services/{agingReportService,pettyCashService,purchaseOrderService,vendorBillPaymentService,vendorPaymentSchedulingCronService,recurringInvoiceService,recurringInvoiceCronService,paystackService}.ts` (all new); `backend/src/services/invoicePaymentService.ts`; `backend/src/routes/{pettyCash,purchaseOrders,recurringInvoices,paystack}.ts` (all new); `backend/src/routes/{reports,invoices,bills,inventory}.ts`; `backend/src/app.ts`; `backend/src/index.ts`; `backend/.env.example`; `backend/src/tests/{agingReport,customerCreditLimit,pettyCash,purchaseOrders,reorderPurchaseOrders,vendorPaymentScheduling,recurringInvoices,paystack}.test.ts` (all new); `frontend/src/pages/reports/AgingReport.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/pettycash/PettyCash.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/purchaseorders/PurchaseOrders.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/recurringinvoices/RecurringInvoices.tsx` (all new); `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`; `frontend/src/pages/bills/VendorBills.tsx`; `frontend/src/pages/inventory/WarehouseManagement.tsx`; `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts`; `frontend/src/App.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-15] - Help Assistant Conversation Logging, Daily Retention Cron, and an Admin Review Screen

**What/Why:** Direct follow-up to the Help Assistant shipped earlier the same day. User asked whether the assistant could "learn" from usage via a background script running daily. Real model fine-tuning isn't realistic or safe here (Anthropic doesn't offer accessible retraining for an app like this, and an autonomous job silently rewriting what the assistant treats as fact is a real risk for an accounting platform). Explicitly confirmed with the user first: conversations stay strictly per-tenant, never pooled or mixed across businesses, and any knowledge-base update drawn from them goes through a human, never an automatic rewrite. Built the safe version of "learning from usage": log conversations, run a daily background job, and surface what needs a human's attention - no autonomous behavior change anywhere in the loop.

**Backend:** New `HelpAssistantConversation` table (shared schema, plain `tenantId` string column with no FK, same pattern as `AuditLog`) - one row per chat turn: the question, the answer, which tools were used, and a `flagged`/`flagReason` pair computed from real signals (a tool lookup was denied/failed, or the assistant gave up after `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS`), not from fragile string-matching on the reply text. `helpAssistantService.chat()` now returns `toolsUsed`/`hadToolError` alongside the reply so the caller can log accurately. `POST /help-assistant/chat` logs every real turn (success or failure) after the fact - a logging failure is caught and never turns an otherwise-successful chat reply into a 500; a plain "message is required" 400 never reached the assistant at all, so it isn't logged. New `GET /help-assistant/conversations` (Admin/Auditor, tenant-scoped, defaults to flagged-only) is the human side of the loop. New daily `HelpAssistantMaintenanceCronService` (registered in `index.ts` alongside the other daily crons) prunes conversation logs older than 90 days - the literal "background script that runs every day" the user asked for, and a real privacy/retention safeguard given this table holds raw chat content.

**Frontend:** New "AI Assistant Activity" page (`/help-assistant/activity`, Administration nav group, Admin/Auditor-visible via the existing `RESTRICTED_ROLE_NAV` allowlist mechanism) - a simple table of conversations with a Needs Review / All Conversations toggle, mirroring the Audit Trail page's shape but without its filter complexity, since the daily-created flagged queue is the only view that matters for this screen's purpose.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. 18 new tests across `helpAssistant.test.ts` (logging correctness for clean/tool-success/tool-denied/iteration-limit-exceeded turns, no log on validation failure, `GET /conversations`' default flagged-only filter, tenant-scoped results, and a Viewer correctly denied 403) and `helpAssistantMaintenanceCron.test.ts` (prunes conversations past the retention window, leaves recent ones untouched). Full backend suite 589/590 (the one failure is the same pre-existing flaky WebSocket timing test in `presenceSocket.test.ts` noted in every prior entry, unrelated). Live-verified via Playwright: seeded one flagged and one clean conversation directly, confirmed the Needs Review view shows only the flagged one (with its reason on hover) and the All Conversations view shows both, and confirmed the new sidebar link renders.

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260815044438_add_help_assistant_conversation_log/` (new), `backend/src/repository/helpAssistantConversationRepository.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/helpAssistantService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/helpAssistant.ts`, `backend/src/services/helpAssistantMaintenanceCronService.ts` (new), `backend/src/index.ts`, `backend/src/tests/helpAssistant.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/helpAssistantMaintenanceCron.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/help/HelpAssistantActivity.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-15] - In-App AI Help Assistant (Claude), Read-Only, Available on Every Plan

**What/Why:** User asked what AI could be integrated so the system could "check the background work" and help a stuck user via a Help button, fed with a system manual, without duplicating a lower-security path into tenant data. Recommended and built (after explicit "yes go ahead") a floating in-app Help chat widget on every page, grounded in a condensed knowledge base of how Ledgio actually works, that can also look up the logged-in user's own real tenant data - scoped deliberately narrow for v1: read-only, no ability to take any action, ~7 lookup tools, available to every plan tier (not gated behind Business/Enterprise, since a Shop-tier single-location business is exactly who benefits most from not having to read documentation).

**The key architectural decision - the assistant can never see or do more than the logged-in user already can, by construction, not by policy.** Its tools don't reimplement business logic or role checks (considered and rejected reimplementing `hasRequiredRole()` per-tool, since that risks silently drifting from the real routes' access rules over time). Instead each tool makes a real internal HTTP call back into this same running Express server (`http://localhost:${PORT}/api/v1${path}`), forwarding the *original calling user's own* `Authorization` and `X-Tenant-Id` headers unchanged - so every lookup passes through the exact same `authenticateJwt`/`tenantContextMiddleware`/`requireRole()` gates any other request to that endpoint would. A Shop Manager asking the assistant about the ledger gets the identical 403 they'd get clicking the real page.

**Backend:** New `helpAssistantKnowledge.ts` - a condensed system-prompt knowledge base covering the cash-basis accounting model, POS/Till, Invoicing (partial payments, stock-linked invoices, credit notes, dunning), Vendor Bills, Expense Claims, Chart of Accounts (including the `defaultRole` mechanism from the entry below), Reports, the Business-tier-and-up modules, the three plan tiers, Team & Roles, Multi-Currency, and offline support. New `helpAssistantService.ts` implements the Anthropic Messages API tool-use loop (capped at `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS = 5` against runaway cost/loops) against 7 read-only tools (list invoices, get ledger summary, till status, expense claims, chart of accounts, tenant/plan info, and a general knowledge lookup), each mapped to a real internal route. New `GET /help-assistant/status` (frontend can show a graceful "not set up" state) and `POST /help-assistant/chat` (new `helpAssistantRateLimiter`, 40 req/hour/tenant, reusing the existing Redis-backed `createRateLimiter()` factory). Follows this codebase's established env-gated-integration convention exactly (`momoService.ts`/`tellerService`/`fxRateService.ts`): unset `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` means a clear 503, never a faked response - `isHelpAssistantConfigured()` gate, `HelpAssistantServiceError`.

**Frontend:** New `HelpAssistantWidget.tsx` - a floating button + chat panel, mounted once in `MainLayout.tsx` so it's available on every authenticated page without per-page wiring. Checks `/help-assistant/status` on first open and shows "Help Assistant isn't set up for this workspace yet. Contact your administrator." (rather than a broken chat box) when unconfigured - this sandbox has no real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, so this is the actual live state verified below.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `helpAssistant.test.ts` (9 tests, `jest.mock('axios')` mocking both the Anthropic call and the internal-API-forwarding call - the same sanctioned external-service-mocking pattern as `momo.test.ts`): unconfigured state returns 503 and calls no external API; configured state answers directly from the knowledge base with no tool calls; runs a real tool call and asserts the internal request was made with the *original caller's own* `Authorization`/`X-Tenant-Id` headers (the access-control guarantee, proven directly rather than just asserted); a role-denied (403) tool result is surfaced gracefully in the reply instead of crashing; exceeding `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS` returns a clean 502 instead of looping forever; an unknown tool name is reported as a tool error instead of crashing. Full backend suite 580/581 (the one failure is the same pre-existing flaky WebSocket timing test in `presenceSocket.test.ts` noted in every prior entry, unrelated). Live-verified via Playwright against the real dev servers: onboarded a fresh tenant, opened the app, clicked the floating Help button, confirmed the widget renders on the Dashboard and correctly shows the "not set up for this workspace yet" message - this sandbox has no real Anthropic credentials, so the tool-use/conversation path itself could not be live-exercised here, only the unconfigured-state UI and the full backend test suite's mocked coverage of it (same honesty caveat as this project's prior TheTeller entry, which also shipped without live sandbox credentials).

**Files:** `backend/src/data/helpAssistantKnowledge.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/helpAssistantService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/helpAssistant.ts` (new), `backend/src/middleware/rateLimiterMiddleware.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/.env.example`, `backend/src/tests/helpAssistant.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/components/HelpAssistantWidget.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/components/layout/MainLayout.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-15] - Fixed a Silent Revenue/Cash-Recognition Bug: Explicit Default-Account Designation Replaces a Hardcoded Magic Code

**What/Why:** User asked what accounts a single-shop tenant should set up. While answering, found that the platform's own built-in Ghana SME starter chart-of-accounts template (used by the Guided Onboarding Wizard - exactly what a new shop is meant to use) doesn't match a hardcoded assumption baked into four different posting services. `invoicePaymentService.ts`, `creditDebitNoteService.ts` (both call sites), `bills.ts`, and `expenseClaimService.ts` all resolved "the" cash/revenue/expense account for a generic posting by looking for an account coded exactly `1010`/`4010`/`5010`, falling back to an arbitrary INDEX into the code-sorted account list (`accounts[1]`, `accounts[accounts.length - 1]`) if no exact match existed. The starter template's Sales Revenue is coded `4000` (not `4010`), and no account is coded `5010` at all - so `revenueAcc` fell through to `accounts[1]`, which (sorted by code) is **Cash Till**, the exact same account `cashAcc` had already resolved via its own `1010` match. Every invoice payment posted a debit and a credit to the *same* Cash Till account for the same amount - a self-cancelling no-op that recorded neither the cash received nor the revenue earned, with the invoice still showing "Paid" in the UI and no error anywhere. Confirmed live: a tenant using the wizard's unmodified default template would have this happen on every single invoice payment.

**The proper fix (not a template renumbering, which would only have been a coincidental patch):** new `Account.defaultRole` column (`CASH` | `REVENUE` | `EXPENSE`, at most one account per role, enforced by a partial unique index) - an explicit, tenant-visible designation of which account each auto-posting service should target, instead of guessing from a magic number. New `accountRepository.resolveDefaultAccount()` centralizes the resolution all four services now share: designated role (new, correct) → legacy code match (kept, for tenants who already happen to use `1010`/`4010`/`5010` - zero behavior change for them) → same-TYPE fallback (new safety net - if neither of the above exists, at least lands on *some* account of the right kind, e.g. any REVENUE account, rather than an arbitrary list position that could be any type at all). That last tier is what actually closes the bug: it makes "posts to the wrong TYPE of account entirely" structurally impossible going forward, regardless of what a tenant's chart of accounts happens to look like.

**Retroactive fix, not just forward-fix:** new tenant migration `009_add_account_default_role` backfills every tenant that already exists (lowest-code cash-equivalent ASSET for CASH, lowest-code REVENUE account, lowest-code EXPENSE account preferring one named "Miscellaneous" as a genuine catch-all) - this runs automatically the next time each tenant's schema is touched (the existing lazy per-request migration mechanism), so already-onboarded tenants self-heal with no manual action required. `onboardingWizardService.seedChartOfAccounts()` runs the equivalent JS heuristic (`accountRepository.pickAutoDefaultCandidate`) right after seeding, so a *new* shop going through the wizard is correctly designated from the first account it creates, never hitting the bug at all.

**Backend:** New `PUT /accounts/:id/default-role` (validates the account's TYPE is a sane fit for the requested role - e.g. rejects designating a LIABILITY account as CASH - and atomically clears whoever previously held that role before assigning it, since at most one account can hold a role at a time). Full audit-logged and sync-logged like every other account mutation.

**Frontend:** Chart of Accounts gained a "Default Posting" column - a star-badge toggle per eligible row ("Default Cash Account" / "Default Revenue Account" / "Default Expense Account"), so any tenant (including the one already-live production tenant this was originally caught on) can see and fix their own designation with one click, no engineering involvement needed. Not routed through the offline sync outbox (a one-time setup action, done online) - calls the endpoint directly and reconciles the local IndexedDB mirror (including clearing the previous holder locally) so the UI never shows two "default" badges for the same role.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `accountDefaultRole.test.ts` (12 tests): pure resolution-logic unit tests proving cash and revenue can never resolve to the same account, the auto-designation heuristic, and a live-tenant integration suite that seeds the *real* Ghana SME template through the wizard and proves - end to end, through an actual invoice creation and payment - that Cash and Sales Revenue ledger balances both move by the real amount instead of netting to zero. Also caught and fixed a genuine mistake in the migration's own SQL while getting this green: a semicolon inside a `--` comment ("...pick per role; fully visible...") was silently splitting the statement list wrong (the naive `;`-based statement splitter doesn't parse SQL comments), producing a bare `fully` token Postgres rejected - fixed by rephrasing the comment. Full backend suite 571/572 (the one failure is the same pre-existing flaky WebSocket timing test in `presenceSocket.test.ts`, unrelated, confirmed passing in isolation in earlier sessions). Live-verified via Playwright: seeded the real starter template through the actual wizard flow, confirmed Sales Revenue auto-shows "Default Revenue" and Cash on Hand auto-shows "Default Cash" with no manual setup, then reassigned Cash to a different account (Bank Account) through the real UI and confirmed the previous holder's badge cleared immediately.

**Files:** `backend/src/database/migrations/tenantMigrations.ts` (migration 9), `backend/src/repository/accountRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/accountService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/accounts.ts`, `backend/src/services/invoicePaymentService.ts`, `backend/src/services/creditDebitNoteService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/bills.ts`, `backend/src/services/expenseClaimService.ts`, `backend/src/services/onboardingWizardService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/accountDefaultRole.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/types/accounting.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/syncEngine.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useAccounts.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/accounts/ChartOfAccounts.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-15] - Self-Serve Plan Tiers (Shop/Business/Enterprise) So Small Shops and Big Companies Share One Engine

**What/Why:** Direct continuation of the same conversation that led to partial invoice payments and stock-linked invoices (below) - the user's underlying goal was for a single-shop small business to be able to use exactly the same platform as a large multi-location company, paying for (and being limited to) only what they need, rather than forking the product into a "small business version" and an "enterprise version." Asked to design this since the user isn't an accountant/wasn't sure what the right shape was; recommended and built a single shared accounting engine with a plan-tier feature gate, not a fork, matching how every real SaaS product handles this.

**What already existed:** `Tenant.tier` (Int, default 1) and a `requireCustomizationTier()` middleware already existed, but were framed narrowly around "how much can this tenant customize the platform" (Tier 2 = Custom Fields) and gated exactly one API-only route with no frontend UI at all - no pricing page, no upgrade path, no plan picker at signup, no feature-limit messaging anywhere a shop owner would ever see. Repurposed this same field/mechanism into a unified plan tier (feature access AND customization bundled together, the standard SaaS pattern) rather than running two parallel tier concepts.

**The three plans:**
- **Shop** (tier 1, default for every new signup): everything that worked before this change - POS, Invoicing, Inventory, Expense Claims, core Reports, up to 3 team seats.
- **Business** (tier 2): + Bank Reconciliation, Recurring Transactions, Budgets, Approval Workflows, Custom Fields (already coded this way), up to 10 seats.
- **Enterprise** (tier 3): + unlimited seats, priority support.

Fund Accounting (nonprofit tenants) was deliberately left out of any tier gate - it's driven by `Tenant.orgType`, not business size, and churches/small NGOs are exactly the kind of small organization this system is meant to include, not exclude behind a paid tier.

**Grandfathering - the change that made this safe to ship:** every tenant that already existed before this shipped was on `tier=1` by the schema's existing default, with no gate anywhere restricting them. Introducing gates now would have silently locked real, already-onboarded businesses out of features they may already be using. New migration `20260815020000_grandfather_existing_tenants_to_enterprise_tier` bumps every tenant that exists at migration time to Enterprise (`tier=3`) - only tenants onboarding from this point forward get the real Shop default and can actually hit a gate. Caught a second real correctness issue while building this: `tenantContextMiddleware` reads a tenant's tier off a 30-minute Redis cache that nothing previously invalidated - without fixing that, an admin's plan upgrade wouldn't actually take effect for up to half an hour. `PUT /tenants/:id/tier` now calls the existing (but previously unused for this purpose) `invalidateTenantCacheById()` right after the DB write.

**Backend:** `tierEnforcementMiddleware.ts` rewritten around `requireTier(minTier, featureLabel)` (structured `403` with `upgradeRequired`, `currentTier`/`requiredTier`, and human plan names, so the frontend can render a real message instead of a generic error) plus `TENANT_PLANS`/`planName()`/`seatLimitForTier()`. Gated as `router.use(requireTier(2, '...'))` on `banking.ts`, `recurringTransactions.ts`, `budgets.ts`, `approvalWorkflows.ts`; `customFields.ts` moved onto the same helper. No self-serve billing yet (explicit user decision - feature-gate now, payment processor later), so a new admin-passcode-gated `PUT /tenants/:id/tier` (same auth pattern as `admin-onboard`) lets a platform admin set a tenant's plan by hand. `POST /tenants/invite` now enforces the plan's seat cap, counting every real member of the tenant (not just `isActive:true` ones - that flag means "has verified their email" in this schema, not "occupies a seat," so counting only active members would have undercounted every tenant with a pending email verification).

**Frontend:** New `<UpgradeRequired>` component (locked-state card: "X requires the Business plan," current vs. required plan, no self-serve upgrade button since billing doesn't exist yet - points at support instead) shown by `BankReconciliation.tsx`, `RecurringTransactions.tsx`, `Budgets.tsx`, and `Approvals.tsx` when the tenant's plan doesn't meet the page's requirement (each page's own data-fetching effect also skips the now-guaranteed-403 API call rather than firing it and discarding the error). `Settings.tsx`'s Organization Profile tab gained a Plan card describing what's unlocked at the current tier. `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s existing (previously read-only) "Tenant Schemas & Tier Management" tab gained a real per-tenant plan dropdown + Save, wired to the new endpoint.

**A real regression found and fixed while verifying this:** gating four already-shipped, already-tested feature areas broke 8 existing test files whose fixture tenants onboard fresh (via `onboardTenant()`, defaulting to the new Shop tier) and then incidentally exercise Bank Reconciliation/Recurring Transactions/Budgets/Approval Workflows as part of testing something else entirely (e.g. `cashFlowForecast.test.ts` creating recurring transactions to build its forecast fixture). Fixed by passing `tier: 3` on those tenants' onboarding calls (a one-line, already-supported DTO field) rather than weakening the gate - each file's own test intent was unaffected, only its now-irrelevant assumption of unrestricted access needed correcting. `legalAndEnforcement.test.ts`'s pre-existing tier-gate test itself was updated to match the new response shape (`error`/`upgradeRequired` instead of the old `error: 'Customization Tier Restriction'`/`message` fields).

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `tierEnforcement.test.ts` (10 tests): a fresh tenant defaults to Shop, all four Business-tier routes return the structured `403`, the admin endpoint rejects a bad passcode/invalid tier and successfully upgrades (with the route immediately usable afterward - the cache-invalidation fix verified directly), and the seat limit blocks an invite once a Shop-tier tenant has 3 real members. Full backend suite 559/560 (the only failure is the same pre-existing flaky WebSocket timing test in `presenceSocket.test.ts` from before this change, confirmed unrelated). Live-verified via Playwright through the real UI end-to-end: a freshly onboarded tenant shows the locked state on Recurring Transactions and "Plan: Shop" in Settings; an admin plan upgrade to Business (via the same endpoint the Admin Core Engine console's new dropdown calls) immediately unlocks the real page with no reload-then-wait, and Settings updates to "Plan: Business" describing what's now available; the Admin Core Engine's tier editor renders and correctly reflects each tenant's current plan.

**Files:** `backend/prisma/migrations/20260815020000_grandfather_existing_tenants_to_enterprise_tier/` (new), `backend/src/middleware/tierEnforcementMiddleware.ts`, `backend/src/routes/customFields.ts`, `backend/src/routes/banking.ts`, `backend/src/routes/recurringTransactions.ts`, `backend/src/routes/budgets.ts`, `backend/src/routes/approvalWorkflows.ts`, `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `backend/src/tests/tierEnforcement.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/legalAndEnforcement.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/{approvalWorkflows,bankReconciliationMatching,banking,cashFlowForecast,expenseClaims,fiscalPeriodsAndBudgets,rbacLockdown,recurringTransactions}.test.ts` (tier:3 fixture fix), `frontend/src/components/UpgradeRequired.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/types/tenant.ts`, `frontend/src/contexts/TenantSettingsContext.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/banking/BankReconciliation.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/RecurringTransactions.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/reports/Budgets.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/approvals/Approvals.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-15] - Itemized Invoices Now Deduct Real Stock on Issue, With Credit-Note Stock Restoration

**What/Why:** Continuation of the same conversation that led to partial invoice payments (below). Invoices and the Inventory module were previously two entirely disconnected systems - issuing an invoice for physical goods never touched `WarehouseStock` at all, meaning a business selling from stock had no way to have its books and its shelves agree once it moved off cash-only POS sales onto invoiced (pay-later) transactions. VendorBill already had exactly this pattern on the buying side (`VendorBill.warehouseId` + itemized purchase lines incrementing stock) - this closes the mirror-image gap on the selling side.

**Backend (schema + `POST /invoices`):** New `Invoice.warehouseId`/`stockDeducted` and `InvoiceItem.inventoryItemId` (all optional - a Simple Invoice with no warehouse is completely unaffected, existing behavior preserved exactly). `warehouseId` presence is what makes an invoice "Itemized"; each line with a matching `inventoryItemId` deducts real stock at issue time. Mirrors `cashTill.ts`'s POS stock-deduction pattern exactly: a friendly pre-check across every line first (clear "Insufficient stock for X in Y (Available: N)" message), then an atomically-guarded `updateMany` decrement per line (`quantityOnHand: { gte: qty }`) to catch a genuine concurrent race a plain read-then-write would miss, rolling back the whole invoice creation if any line can't be satisfied.

**Credit notes can restore that stock, but only when they can do so accurately.** This platform's credit notes are a lump financial amount, not itemized returns - restoring stock requires knowing exactly which units are coming back, which is only knowable for certain when the credit note fully cancels what's left owed on the invoice (then every original line quantity can be put back exactly). A partial credit note can't be tied to specific returned units, so `POST /invoices/:id/credit-notes` now accepts an opt-in `returnToStock: true` flag that's rejected (400, clear message pointing at manual Stock Adjustment instead) unless the credit amount covers the full remaining creditable balance. **Design correction made mid-build:** originally scoped as "Void restores stock; Credit Note asks whether to restore stock," but grepping `invoices.ts` found no void route exists for invoices at all (only Journal Vouchers have Draft/Posted/Void) - restructured around credit notes only before writing any code.

**Frontend (`Invoices.tsx`):** New Simple Invoice / Itemized Invoice toggle on the Create Invoice modal (same visual pattern as `VendorBills.tsx`'s Simple Bill/Itemized Purchase toggle), a warehouse picker that appears only in Itemized mode, and a per-line inventory-item dropdown that auto-fills description/unit price from the selected item's real selling price (still user-editable, since a specific invoice line might legitimately differ). The Credit Note modal gained a "Return items to stock" checkbox, shown only when the invoice being credited actually deducted stock. **Known pre-existing gap, not fixed in this pass:** `Invoices.tsx` has no add/remove line-item controls at all (single fixed line array) - itemization was added within that existing constraint rather than also building line management, which is a separate piece of work.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `invoiceStockLinkage.test.ts` (11 tests): Simple Invoices leave stock untouched, an Itemized Invoice deducts the right quantity from the right warehouse, insufficient stock is rejected with the invoice never created, an unknown warehouse 404s, an item with no stock row in the target warehouse (wrong warehouse) is rejected the same as genuinely insufficient stock, a credit note with `returnToStock` on a fully-cancelled itemized invoice restores exactly the deducted quantities, a partial credit note requesting `returnToStock` is rejected, and `returnToStock` against a Simple Invoice (nothing to restore) is rejected. Full backend suite 550/550 (one unrelated pre-existing flaky WebSocket timing test in `presenceSocket.test.ts` - confirmed passes cleanly 4/4 in isolation, not caused by this change).

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260815012815_add_invoice_itemized_stock_deduction/` (new), `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/services/creditDebitNoteService.ts`, `backend/src/services/syncChangeLogService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/invoiceStockLinkage.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/syncEngine.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-08-15] - Partial Invoice Payments (Accounts Receivable)

**What/Why:** User asked what happens when a company buys on credit - invoices could previously only be marked fully PAID or left unpaid, with no way to record a customer paying part of what they owe now and the rest later. This was flagged as a real gap while discussing how a small, one-shop business and a large multi-location company should both be able to run on the same accounting engine (not a "small tier vs. enterprise tier" fork) - the user explicitly delegated the design decisions ("I'm not an accountant... let me know what you think is best"), and this was built as the first of two fixes agreed on (the second is the stock-linkage entry directly above).

**Design:** Revenue is still only ever recognized as cash is actually received (this platform's existing cash-basis-on-payment model, unchanged) - a partial payment just means that recognition now happens across more than one installment per invoice instead of exactly once. New `Invoice.amountPaid` (cumulative, native currency) and a new `InvoicePayment` history table (one row per payment, full or partial, with its own posted `journalId`). New `PARTIALLY_PAID` status sits alongside the existing `DRAFT`/`SENT`/`PAID`. `recordInvoicePayment()` (renamed from `markInvoicePaid`) generalizes the old all-or-nothing math to a `paymentShare = amount / invoiceTotal` fraction applied uniformly to the FX conversion, tax-destination-account splits, and the Cash/Revenue journal lines - verified to reduce to the exact old single-payment behavior when `amount` is omitted (pays off whatever remains) or equals the full total, so every existing caller (manual pay button, MTN MoMo, TheTeller) keeps working unchanged while gaining partial-payment support for free. MoMo/TheTeller's requested collection amount changed from the invoice's static `total` to its live remaining balance, so a partially-paid invoice's mobile-money request doesn't ask for more than is actually still owed.

**Credit notes and dunning reconciled with partial payments:** `createCreditNote()` now splits any credit proportionally across the unpaid remainder (`INVOICE_REDUCTION`, no journal entry) and the already-recognized portion (`JOURNAL_REVERSAL`, real reversing entry that also claws back `amountPaid`) - a note against a fully-unpaid invoice is 100% the old `INVOICE_REDUCTION` behavior and one against a fully-paid invoice is 100% the old `JOURNAL_REVERSAL` behavior; the partially-paid case is the genuinely new proportional split, not a third code path. New `method: 'MIXED'` return value covers a credit that straddles both. `DunningReminderCronService`'s overdue-reminder emails now quote the real remaining balance due, not the full original total.

**Frontend (`Invoices.tsx`):** Status badge is now three-way (Sent/Partially Paid/Paid). New "Record Payment" modal defaulting to the full remaining balance but editable down to any partial amount, and a read-only "Payment History" modal listing every payment ever recorded against an invoice. AR dashboard metrics (`totalAR`/`totalPaid`) fixed to sum real balance-due/amount-paid instead of an entire unpaid invoice's total. `syncEngine.ts`'s local-first `payInvoiceLocalFirst()` now computes an optimistic partial-payment state (new status/amountPaid) client-side before the real network round trip confirms it, matching the rest of the local-first outbox pattern.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `invoicePartialPayments.test.ts` (7 tests): a partial payment sets `PARTIALLY_PAID` and reduces the balance, the posted journal entry is scaled to just the partial amount (not the full total), a second payment completing the balance transitions to `PAID`, overpayment is rejected, a non-positive amount is rejected, omitting `amount` still pays off the full remaining balance (backward compatibility), and `GET /:id/payments` lists full history newest-first. Full backend suite 550/550 (see the stock-linkage entry above for the one unrelated pre-existing flaky test).

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260815011753_add_invoice_partial_payments/` and `20260815011846_rename_invoice_payment_recorded_by_email/` (new), `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/routes/momo.ts`, `backend/src/routes/teller.ts`, `backend/src/services/invoicePaymentService.ts`, `backend/src/services/creditDebitNoteService.ts`, `backend/src/services/dunningReminderService.ts`, `backend/src/services/syncChangeLogService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/invoicePartialPayments.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/syncEngine.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-08-14] - Till-Close SMS Now Goes to a Real, Per-Tenant "Boss Number" on Every Close

**What/Why:** User asked to have the system text "the boss number" after every till close. Investigation found a real, pre-existing SMS gateway (`smsService.ts`, Arkesel/mNotify/Android-gateway fallback chain, genuinely dispatches - not a stub outside test env) already wired into `cashTill.ts`'s `POST /tills/close`, but with two real gaps: (1) it only fired on a cash **shortage** (`if (discrepancy < 0)`), never on a balanced or over close; (2) the recipient was a single platform-wide `OWNER_PHONE_NUMBER` env var (hardcoded fallback `+233200000000`) shared across every tenant - there was no per-tenant "boss number" concept anywhere in the schema. Asked the user via `AskUserQuestion` whether to keep the single global number or make it per-tenant; they chose per-tenant.

**Also found while building this:** Settings' existing "Android SMS Gateway" tab already had a "Target Owner Mobile Phone Number" field with copy claiming it controlled shortage alerts - but saving it wrote to `user.phone` via `PUT /auth/profile`, a field the backend's SMS logic never actually read. Dead-wired UI, now fixed as part of the same change (repurposed to the new per-tenant field, not scrapped).

**Backend:** New `Tenant.bossPhone String?` column (migration `20260814164808_add_tenant_boss_phone`), exposed on the existing `GET/PUT /tenants/current` (Admin-only for writes, matching the rest of that endpoint) with lightweight format validation (`400` on something that can't plausibly be a phone number; an empty string clears it rather than being rejected). `cashTill.ts`'s `POST /tills/close` now looks up the closing till's tenant's `bossPhone` and, if one is configured, dispatches a summary SMS **unconditionally** on every close (balanced, over, or short) via a new `SmsService.sendTillCloseAlert()` (shop, staff, cash sales, expected vs. actual, and the discrepancy text) - replacing the old shortage-only trigger. No `bossPhone` configured means no SMS at all (never falls back to the old shared env var/hardcoded number for a tenant that hasn't set one).

**Frontend:** `Settings.tsx`'s SMS tab now saves to the real `bossPhone` field via `useTenantSettings().updateSettings()` (→ `PUT /tenants/current`) instead of the dead `PUT /auth/profile` write, and its copy was updated to describe the real "every close, not just shortages" behavior.

**Real bug found and fixed while verifying this live:** `TenantSettingsContext.tsx`'s `updateSettings()` toggled the same `isLoading` flag used to gate initial-load rendering - both `App.tsx`'s `ProtectedRoute` and `Settings.tsx` itself hold their whole tree on a loading spinner whenever that flag is true. Since every settings save (Company Name, Currency, and now Boss Phone) round-trips through `updateSettings()`, saving anything anywhere in Settings briefly unmounted and remounted the entire page, resetting `activeTab` back to "Organization Profile" - so the success banner for a save on any tab but the first rendered on a tab the user had already been bounced off of, making a save that had actually worked look like it silently failed. Caught via live Playwright verification (screenshot showed the tab jump immediately after clicking "Update Boss Mobile Number", `GET /tenants/current` confirmed the value *had* saved). Fixed by no longer toggling `isLoading` inside `updateSettings()` - nothing else in the app depended on it doing so.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `tillCloseSmsAlert.test.ts` (6 tests): rejects a garbage phone number (400), no SMS dispatched when no boss phone is configured, dispatches with the correct recipient/shop/discrepancy text on a balanced close, correctly reports SHORT/OVER discrepancy text, and clearing the phone (empty string) disables future alerts. Full backend suite 535/535. Live-verified via Playwright end-to-end through the real UI (not just the API): set a boss phone number in Settings, confirmed the success banner shows on the correct tab and the value survives a page reload, then opened and closed a real till through the actual POS "Close Till" flow with that boss phone configured - closed cleanly (200, "Till Balanced" shown), no console errors, confirming the full route → tenant lookup → `SmsService.sendTillCloseAlert` → gateway-dispatch path executes without throwing in a live running server, not just under the Jest mocks.

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260814164808_add_tenant_boss_phone/` (new), `backend/src/repository/tenantRepository.ts`, `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `backend/src/routes/cashTill.ts`, `backend/src/services/smsService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/tillCloseSmsAlert.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/types/tenant.ts`, `frontend/src/contexts/TenantSettingsContext.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-14] - Fixed Offline Sessions Getting Force-Logged-Out and Their Local Cache Wiped

**What/Why:** User asked how solid the app's offline support is - specifically, whether it can even open with no internet. Investigation found a genuinely working local-first architecture underneath (a real PWA shell precached by the service worker, plus a Dexie/IndexedDB sync mirror with an outbox pattern for Chart of Accounts and Invoices) undermined by one bug that broke the whole story in practice: `AuthContext.tsx`'s `verifyToken()` (runs on every mount, pinging `GET /auth/me`) called `logout()` unconditionally on **any** error from that call, without checking whether it was a genuine `401` (an actually invalid/expired token) or a plain network failure (offline, DNS, server unreachable - the exact case a previously-logged-in user reopening the app with no internet hits every time). Because `logout()` clears `token`, this cascaded into `useSyncEngineLifecycle`'s effect (keyed on `[token, user?.tenantId]`) re-firing its "logged out" branch - `resetLocalSyncData()` - wiping the entire local-first cache the offline architecture exists to provide. User confirmed: fix it.

**What changed (`AuthContext.tsx`):** `verifyToken()`'s catch block now checks `error?.response` before calling `logout()` - present (a real HTTP rejection came back) still logs out as before; absent (no response at all, i.e. a connectivity failure) now just leaves the existing cached session alone and resolves `isLoading`. `user` state is now seeded synchronously from a new `accountgo-user` localStorage cache (written on `login()`/successful `verifyToken()`/`refreshUser()`, cleared on `logout()`) via a lazy `useState` initializer, instead of starting `null` - so a returning offline user has a usable `user` object (including `tenantId`, which `useSyncEngineLifecycle` keys off of) immediately, without waiting on a network round trip that's going to fail anyway. `api.ts`'s separate 401-response interceptor was already correctly gated on `error.response && error.response.status === 401` - confirmed not buggy, no changes needed there.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` and `vite build` both clean. No frontend test framework exists in this repo (no jest/vitest configured) - matching this session's established pattern for frontend-only changes, verified live instead: built the real production bundle, served it with `vite preview` (so the actual generated service worker registers and precaches the app shell, exactly as it would in production - a plain `vite dev` server has no service worker and can't serve an offline reload at all, which would have made this an invalid test), logged in for real via a freshly admin-onboarded tenant, confirmed the service worker reached `activated`, then used Playwright's `context.setOffline(true)` and reloaded. Confirmed: still on `/accounts` (not bounced to `/login`), `localStorage`'s token/user/tenantId all intact, and the IndexedDB sync mirror's row counts unchanged - despite `/auth/me`, `/sync/bootstrap`, and both WebSocket connections all genuinely failing with real network errors in the console (proving the offline path was actually exercised, not skipped). Toggled back online and reloaded again to confirm normal re-verification still works afterward.

**Files:** `frontend/src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-14] - Built Live "Who's Online" Presence for Team Management

**What/Why:** User asked for real-time presence - when a Shop Manager (or anyone) is actively using the system, the Admin should see them as online, like a live collaborator indicator (their own analogy: "like Excel, when someone is typing you can see the person").

**Backend:** New `backend/src/websocket/presenceSocketServer.ts` at `/ws/presence`, mirroring `syncSocketServer.ts`'s JWT-auth + per-tenant connection-tracking shape. Every authenticated session connects here (not gated to Admin - the roster needs everyone actually online to be accurate; only the Team Management UI displays it). Tracks an in-memory `Map<tenantId, Map<userId, {name, email, role, connectionCount, lastSeenAt}>>` - `connectionCount` (not a boolean) so a user with the app open in two tabs doesn't wrongly show offline when one tab closes. No Redis/cross-instance fan-out needed (unlike sync change push) - this deployment is a single backend instance and presence is inherently ephemeral, so a restart just means every client reconnects and re-registers. On every connect/disconnect, the full current online roster for that tenant is rebroadcast to all of that tenant's connected sockets - self-healing by construction, a client that misses one update just gets the next.

**Real bug found and fixed while building this**: registering a second `WebSocketServer` (presence) on the same `http.Server` the existing sync socket server already used broke *both* - `ws`'s `{server, path}` shorthand doesn't cleanly coexist with a second instance the way it might appear to. Reading `ws`'s own source (`websocket-server.js`) confirmed why: `handleUpgrade()` calls `shouldHandle()` and, on any path mismatch, actively calls `abortHandshake()` - sending a real `400 Bad Request` and destroying the socket - rather than silently deferring to other `'upgrade'` listeners. Since Node's `EventEmitter` calls every registered listener for an event, the sync server's listener (registered first) was aborting every `/ws/presence` upgrade before the presence server's own listener ever ran. Fixed by converting **both** socket servers to the documented multi-endpoint `ws` pattern: `noServer: true` plus each module registering its own `'upgrade'` listener that checks the path itself and only calls `handleUpgrade()` on a match, doing nothing (not aborting) otherwise so other listeners still get their turn.

**Frontend:** New `frontend/src/lib/presenceSocket.ts` (connect/disconnect + pub-sub, mirrors `syncEngine.ts`'s WS lifecycle shape but far simpler - no offline queue or IndexedDB, just the always-current roster snapshot), `usePresenceLifecycle` (mounted globally at the app root, same pattern as `SyncEngineLifecycleMount`) and `usePresence()` hook. `TeamManagement.tsx`'s previously-hardcoded "Active" status badge replaced with a live "Online now" (pulsing green dot) / "Offline" indicator driven by the real roster.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `presenceSocket.test.ts` (4 tests: rejects missing/invalid token, sends roster on connect and rebroadcasts as teammates join/leave, tenant isolation) - required a bounded-retry connection helper after discovering this sandbox's loopback networking is occasionally slow enough to stall a fresh local socket well past Jest's default timeout (intermittent, environment-specific, not a server bug - confirmed by binding/connecting via literal `127.0.0.1` instead of `localhost` first, which fixed most but not all of it) - mirrors the production client's own reconnect-with-backoff design intent rather than assuming every connection attempt succeeds instantly. Full suite 529/529. Live-verified via Playwright with two real simultaneous browser sessions (Admin + Shop Manager): Admin's Team Management page shows the manager flip from "Offline" to "Online now" the instant they log in, with no page reload, and back to "Offline" the instant their session disconnects.

**Files:** `backend/src/websocket/presenceSocketServer.ts` (new), `backend/src/websocket/syncSocketServer.ts`, `backend/src/index.ts`, `backend/src/tests/presenceSocket.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/lib/presenceSocket.ts` (new), `frontend/src/hooks/usePresenceLifecycle.ts` (new), `frontend/src/hooks/usePresence.ts` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/team/TeamManagement.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-14] - Closed Shop Manager/Cashier RBAC Over-Permission (Locked Down to Their Real Scope)

**What/Why:** User asked what a "Shop Manager" gets access to; investigation surfaced that `hasRequiredRole()` (`rbacMiddleware.ts`) had a fallback rule granting any role that wasn't Admin and wasn't in `SCOPED_ROLES` (`viewer`/`auditor`/`hr`) full operational access to *everything* non-Admin-only - Invoices, Bills, Banking, Journals, Reports, the Audit Trail, even approving/reimbursing expense claims. "Shop Manager" and "Cashier" (location-scoped roles meant to be limited to Inventory/POS/Expense Claims per `navigation.ts`'s `RESTRICTED_ROLE_NAV`) fell into that fallback, so the frontend nav hid those screens but nothing on the backend actually stopped a Shop Manager from reaching them directly. User confirmed: lock it down for real, not just hide the links.

**Backend (`rbacMiddleware.ts`):** Added `'shop manager'` and `'cashier'` to `SCOPED_ROLES`, so - like Viewer/Auditor/HR - they now only pass a `requireRole()` check where explicitly listed. Then explicitly re-granted them exactly what their real job needs:
- `inventory.ts`: `POST /items`, `/items/bulk`, `/transfers`, `/adjustments`, `/stock-take`. Deliberately **excluded** `POST /warehouses` (creating a whole new business location isn't a shop-level task, and letting a location-scoped role create warehouses undercuts the scoping concept) - stays Accountant/Admin-only.
- `accounts.ts`: `GET /` (needed by the Expense Claims form's category dropdown).
- `expenseClaims.ts`: `GET /`, `GET /:id`, `POST /` (view/submit own claims). Deliberately **left unchanged** `POST /:id/decide` and `/:id/reimburse` (still Accountant-only) - closing an accidental over-permission, not preserving it.
- `cashTill.ts` needed no changes - it has no `requireRole()` gates at all, relying entirely on `assertWarehouseAccess`/`getAccessibleWarehouseIds` (unaffected by this change) and its own `VOID_AUTHORIZER_ROLES` array.

**Frontend:**
- `App.tsx`'s `ProtectedRoute` gained a generic allowlist check: any role with a `RESTRICTED_ROLE_NAV` entry (Shop Manager, Cashier, HR, Auditor) now gets redirected to `/dashboard` from any route not in their allowed list, covering every route automatically (not just Settings, the only thing previously guarded this way).
- `ExpenseClaims.tsx`: added `shop manager`/`cashier` to `RESTRICTED_DECIDER_ROLES` so the Approve/Reject/Reimburse buttons don't render for them (they were rendered before, then would 403).
- `WarehouseManagement.tsx`: hid the "Add Warehouse / Shop" button for these roles, matching the backend's deliberate exclusion.
- **Regression found and fixed during live verification:** the inline `Dashboard` component (`App.tsx`) unconditionally fetches company-wide P&L (`useProfitAndLoss`) and ledger balances (`useAccounts` → `GET /ledgers/summary`) - both Viewer-only routes Shop Manager/Cashier never had explicit access to. Once scoped, Dashboard (one of their allowed pages) started throwing 403s in the console. Fixed by hiding the financial-summary cards and "New Voucher" button for any restricted role rather than granting them read access to company-wide financials they have no operational need for - the console errors from the underlying hooks still fire (React's rules of hooks - they can't be conditionally skipped) but are invisible to the user. Same underlying bug already existed for HR/Auditor before this session (pre-existing, not something introduced here) - the generic `getVisibleHrefs()`-based fix happens to close it for them too as a side effect.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `rbacLockdown.test.ts` (16 tests, Shop Manager + Cashier each): confirms full access to inventory writes/accounts-read/expense-claim submit-and-view still works, and 403 on warehouse creation, invoices, journal entries, bank reconciliation, audit trail, reports, and expense-claim decide/reimburse. Existing `warehousePermissions.test.ts` (asserts a Shop Manager CAN adjust stock in their assigned shop) still passes unchanged. Full suite 525/525. Live-verified via Playwright: confirmed nav is correctly scoped, direct URL navigation to `/invoices`/`/audit-logs`/`/banking` all redirect to `/dashboard`, `/inventory` and `/expenses` remain reachable with the right actions available and the wrong ones (Add Warehouse, Approve/Reject) genuinely absent from the DOM (not just hidden by an imprecise check - verified via a precise `<button>`-element locator after an initial text-substring check gave a false positive from unrelated page copy), and the Dashboard renders cleanly with no visible errors for the locked-down role.

**Files:** `backend/src/middleware/rbacMiddleware.ts`, `backend/src/routes/inventory.ts`, `backend/src/routes/accounts.ts`, `backend/src/routes/expenseClaims.ts`, `backend/src/tests/rbacLockdown.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/expenses/ExpenseClaims.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/inventory/WarehouseManagement.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-14] - Fixed: Admin "Onboard a Contracted Client" Form Missing Organization Type

**What/Why:** User (platform admin) flagged that the passcode-gated `/admin/core-engine` console's "Onboard a Contracted Client" form had no way to set org type when onboarding a real contracted client. Backend was never the gap - `POST /tenants/admin-onboard` already spreads the whole request body (minus `passcode`) straight into `onboardTenant()`, which has accepted an optional `orgType` ("BUSINESS" | "NONPROFIT", defaulting to "BUSINESS") since the nonprofit/fund-accounting support was built. The self-serve `/register` page already collects it. The admin-onboard form simply never had the field, so every admin-onboarded tenant silently defaulted to BUSINESS regardless of the real client's org type.

**What changed:** `AdminCoreEngine.tsx` - added `onboardOrgType` state (default `"BUSINESS"`), a matching "Organization Type" `<select>` (same two options/labels as `Register.tsx`: "Business" / "Nonprofit / Church / NGO / School"), included in the `POST /tenants/admin-onboard` body, and reset after a successful submission alongside the other fields.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` clean. No backend changes needed (already supported). Verified end-to-end via a direct API call against the local dev backend with `orgType: "NONPROFIT"` - response confirmed `tenant.orgType: "NONPROFIT"` and the issued JWT's `orgType` claim matched; test tenant cleaned up afterward.

**Files:** `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-12] - Built Real Bank-Reconciliation Matching Logic - Closes Research Doc Item 29

**What/Why:** `POST /banking/reconcile` was previously a correctness bug, not just a missing feature - despite "reconcile" in the name, it just took a client-supplied `transactionId`/`ledgerId` pair on faith and flipped `BankTransaction.status` to `RECONCILED`, with no amount/date comparison at all. The frontend didn't even collect a `ledgerId` - "Reconcile Match" blindly marked transactions reconciled with `ledgerId` left null. Flagged as a real gap in the 2026-08-11 research pass, distinct from (and more severe than) a missing feature.

**Backend:** New `ledgerRepository.findCashLedgerMatchCandidates()` - raw SQL (matching this file's existing convention: `Ledger`/`Account` tenant-schema tables are queried via `$queryRawUnsafe`, not the Prisma client, since several real columns like `is_cash_equivalent` were added via the raw-SQL tenant migrations and were never added to `schema.prisma`'s stale `Account`/`Ledger` models) - finds `Ledger` rows on `accounts.is_cash_equivalent = true` accounts whose net movement (`debit - credit`) exactly equals a bank transaction's `amount` (same sign convention `monoService.ts` already uses: positive = deposit, negative = withdrawal), within a 10-day window either side of the posted date, excluding any ledger row already linked to a different bank transaction. New `GET /banking/transactions/:id/suggestions` surfaces these candidates, ranked by date proximity. `POST /banking/reconcile` now **requires** `ledgerId` (400 if missing), fetches the real `Ledger` row (new `ledgerRepository.getLedgerById`), rejects a mismatched amount (400, tolerance 0.01 for rounding noise) or a ledger row already claimed by a different bank transaction (409) - so reconciling now means confirming a real, validated match, not just declaring one.

**Scoping decision, not a defect:** matching searches all of a tenant's cash-equivalent accounts rather than the one specific GL account tied to a given `BankAccount`'s Mono feed - `BankAccount` has no `accountId` FK to the chart of accounts today, and adding that mapping (plus a UI step to set it) is a separate, larger task from "make reconciliation actually check amounts/dates." In the common single-cash-account case this has no practical effect; multi-cash-account tenants get a slightly wider candidate pool, disambiguated by the amount+date narrowing itself.

**Frontend:** `BankReconciliation.tsx`'s "Reconcile Match" button (which sent no `ledgerId` at all) replaced with "Find Match," which fetches suggestions and expands an inline panel below the table listing candidate ledger entries (account, date, description, amount) with a "Confirm Match" button per candidate - or a clear "no matching ledger entry found, post the journal entry first" message when the sweep finds nothing, which is the correct behavior for a genuinely unposted transaction rather than a false blind-reconcile.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. Updated the existing `banking.test.ts` reconcile test to the new required-`ledgerId` flow (posts a real journal entry to produce a matching ledger row, fetches suggestions, reconciles with the returned id) plus added a `missing ledgerId -> 400` case and a cross-tenant `suggestions -> 404` case. New `bankReconciliationMatching.test.ts` (8 tests): exact match suggested, day-window exclusion, amount-mismatch exclusion, already-linked-ledger exclusion, successful reconcile + audit log, mismatched-amount rejection (400), already-claimed-ledger rejection (409), unknown-ledgerId (404). Full suite now 509/509. Live-verified via Playwright against the real dev servers: seeded a real bank transaction and a matching posted journal entry (bypassing Mono, same as the test convention, since there's no way to drive Mono's OAuth widget in an automated environment), confirmed the UI correctly shows "No matching ledger entry found" for an unrelated $275 fee, shows the real $1,500 Cash-account ledger entry as a suggested match for the corresponding bank transaction, and correctly reconciles on "Confirm Match" (status flips to Reconciled, toast confirms, DB/audit log verified directly).

**Files:** `backend/src/repository/ledgerRepository.ts`, `backend/src/routes/banking.ts`, `backend/src/tests/banking.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/bankReconciliationMatching.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/banking/BankReconciliation.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-12] - Built Late Payment Reminders (Dunning Emails) - Closes Research Doc Item 21

**What/Why:** Natural continuation of the invoice-emailing work above - invoices could be created, emailed, and paid, but nothing ever nudged a customer whose invoice was overdue. `Invoice` gains `lastReminderSentAt DateTime?` (migration `20260812073943_add_invoice_last_reminder_sent_at`), tracking the last time a reminder was actually sent, distinct from `dueDate`/`status`.

**What changed:** New `backend/src/services/dunningReminderService.ts`, `DunningReminderCronService`, mirroring the existing cron job shape (`ScheduledEmailCronService`/`RecurringTransactionCronService`/`FxRateCronService`): `.init()` registers a daily `node-cron` sweep at 9am UTC, and the directly-testable `.runOverdueInvoicesJob()` does the real work. Deliberately no tenant-configurability for v1 (no new settings/schedule model) - a blanket "always run for whatever's currently overdue across every tenant" sweep, matching `RecurringTransactionCronService`/`FxRateCronService`'s shape rather than `ScheduledEmailCronService`'s opt-in `ReportSchedule` shape, since no config UI was requested.

Per tenant (`runWithTenantContext` + `withCurrentTenantDb`, since this runs with no HTTP request context), finds invoices with `status NOT IN ('PAID','DRAFT')`, `dueDate` in the past, and `lastReminderSentAt` either null or older than 24 hours (dedup guard, same shape as `ScheduledEmailCronService`'s `lastSentAt` cutoff). For each, sends a new `EmailService.sendPaymentReminderEmail()` (plain HTML, no PDF attachment, states the amount due and days overdue) and, on success, stamps `lastReminderSentAt` inside one transaction alongside the sync-engine outbox write (`recordChange`) and an `INVOICE.PAYMENT_REMINDER_SENT` audit log entry - same shape as `invoiceEmailService.sendInvoiceEmail`. Per-tenant and per-invoice try/catch isolation, matching `RecurringTransactionCronService`'s exact error-isolation shape, so one bad row/tenant can't stop the rest of the sweep. Registered in `index.ts` alongside the other three cron services (lazy `require` + `.init()` inside the `app.listen` callback).

**No new frontend UI** - deliberately skipped, since the generic Audit Trail page (`AuditLogs.tsx`) already renders any `log.action` value and pulls its filter dropdown dynamically from the backend, so `INVOICE.PAYMENT_REMINDER_SENT` entries show up there with zero extra frontend work. `lastReminderSentAt` was still added to `invoiceToSyncPayload` and the frontend's `LocalInvoice` type for consistency with every other `Invoice.update()` call site, even though nothing displays it yet.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/dunningReminder.test.ts` (6 tests, calling `runOverdueInvoicesJob()` directly rather than through HTTP since it's a background job: reminder sent + `lastReminderSentAt` stamped for a genuinely overdue invoice, not-yet-due invoices are skipped, paid invoices are skipped, the 24-hour dedup guard prevents a second send on a second sweep, `INVOICE.PAYMENT_REMINDER_SENT` audit log recorded, and the whole sweep no-ops when email isn't configured) - full suite now 501/501. Production build reproduced Render's actual `render.yaml` sequence (`npm ci && npx prisma generate && npm run build`) in an isolated scratch copy, and the compiled `dunningReminderService.js` was `require()`'d directly to confirm it loads cleanly. No new frontend surface, so no live Playwright verification was needed for this pass.

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260812073943_add_invoice_last_reminder_sent_at/`, `backend/src/services/dunningReminderService.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/EmailService.ts`, `backend/src/services/syncChangeLogService.ts`, `backend/src/index.ts`, `backend/src/tests/dunningReminder.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/lib/syncEngine.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-08-12] - Fixed: Invoice Row Actions-Column Crowding - Closes Research Doc Item 33

**What/Why:** Follow-up to the invoice-emailing change below, which pushed an already-crowded row (History/Record Payment/Collect via MoMo/Collect via Mobile Money/Credit Note) over the edge - confirmed via live Playwright screenshot showing buttons wrapping and clipping at normal viewport width.

**What changed:** `Invoices.tsx`'s per-row actions collapsed from 6 separate buttons down to 2: the existing icon-only History button, plus a single "Actions ▾" button opening a dropdown listing whichever of the 5 actions apply to that row (same conditional visibility rules as before, unchanged). Mirrors `Header.tsx`'s existing profile-dropdown outside-click-close pattern.

**Real bug found and fixed while building this**: the first version rendered the dropdown as a normal `absolute`-positioned child inside the table cell, and it came out visibly clipped - `Table.tsx`'s wrapper is `overflow-auto` (needed for horizontal scroll on wide tables), and per the CSS spec, setting `overflow-x` to anything other than `visible` forces `overflow-y` to compute as `auto` too, not `visible`, even when only the x-axis was ever intended to be scrollable. No amount of CSS on the dropdown itself could escape that ancestor's clipping. Fixed by rendering the dropdown via `createPortal` to `document.body`, `position: fixed` with coordinates captured from the trigger button's `getBoundingClientRect()` at open time, and closing the menu on any scroll (capture-phase listener, since a fixed-position portal doesn't track its trigger through scrolling) rather than trying to reposition it live. Did not touch `Table.tsx` itself - changing its overflow behavior globally would risk breaking horizontal scrolling on the many other wide tables that already rely on it.

**Verification:** `tsc -b`/`vite build` clean. No backend changes - full suite still 495/495 (confirms nothing else was touched). Live-verified via Playwright: row now shows just 2 compact buttons at normal viewport width (previously 6, wrapping), dropdown opens un-clipped with all 5 conditional items visible, clicking an item (Email Invoice) closes the menu and completes the action normally, and clicking outside the menu closes it without acting.

**Files:** `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-12] - Built Automated Invoice Emailing (PDF Attached) - Closes Research Doc Item 19

**What/Why:** Closes the AR gap surfaced in the 2026-08-11 ERP checklist research pass - invoices could be created and paid, but the system never actually emailed them to the customer. `Invoice` gains an `emailedAt DateTime?` field (migration `20260812045641_add_invoice_emailed_at`), deliberately separate from the existing `status` field - `status` is already set to `"SENT"` at creation time regardless of whether any email is ever sent (a pre-existing naming quirk, left as-is rather than repurposed, to avoid changing existing status semantics other code/tests depend on), so `emailedAt` is the only real signal of whether the customer actually received the document.

**New `POST /invoices/:id/send`** (`requireRole('Accountant')`, matching `/pay` and `/credit-notes`): fetches the invoice + customer + items, sends via a new `EmailService.sendInvoiceEmail()` (itemized HTML body + a real generated PDF attached, mirroring the `sendWelcomePackage` pattern of building both inline), then stamps `emailedAt` inside the same transaction as the sync-engine outbox write (`recordChange`) and audit log (`INVOICE.EMAILED`) - same shape as `invoicePaymentService.markInvoicePaid`. Safe to call repeatedly (a genuine re-send, not blocked by a prior send). New `generateInvoicePdf()` in `pdfGenerationService.ts` reuses the file's existing table/total-row/cover-page helpers rather than duplicating PDF layout code.

**Frontend:** `Invoices.tsx` gets an "Email Invoice"/"Re-send" button (label and tooltip reflect `emailedAt`) in the actions column, following the existing `handleIssueCreditNote`-style direct `api.post` + toast + `resyncInvoicesFromServer()` pattern (not the local-first optimistic path, since there's no offline-meaningful state to show before the server confirms a send). `emailedAt` added to `invoiceToSyncPayload` and the frontend's `LocalInvoice`/`Invoice` types so the local-first mirror reflects it.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean; production build reproduced Render's exact `npm ci --omit=dev` install. New `backend/src/tests/invoiceEmail.test.ts` (5 tests: emailedAt stamped on send, re-send updates the timestamp, audit log entry recorded, 404 on unknown invoice, 503 when `SENDGRID_API_KEY`/`EMAIL_FROM` aren't configured) - full suite now 495/495. Live-verified via Playwright: real invoice created, "Email Invoice" clicked through the actual UI, toast confirms the real customer email address, button relabels to "Re-send," a second click re-sends successfully, and `emailedAt` is confirmed set via the API afterward. ## [Date: 2026-08-11] - Built MFA/2FA Login (TOTP + Backup Codes) - Closes the Gap Flagged in the Pain-Point Research Pass

**What/Why:** Closes Section 4 item 32 of the Ghana market research doc (surfaced the same day via a user-supplied industry pain-point analysis - "small businesses rarely implement MFA properly," confirmed as a real, currently-true gap on this platform via grep). Full TOTP-based two-factor login: `User` gains `isMfaEnabled`/`totpSecret`/`mfaBackupCodes` fields (migration `20260811233001_add_user_mfa_fields`). Login is now two-step for MFA-enabled accounts - password success issues a short-lived (5min) `mfaPending` JWT claim instead of a real session token; `authenticateJwt` rejects any request bearing that claim outright (`authMiddleware.ts`), so it's only good for the new `POST /auth/login/verify-mfa`, which accepts either a live TOTP code or a one-time backup code and only then issues the real session token, via a `completeLogin()` helper shared with the no-MFA path so both produce byte-identical responses. New account-management endpoints: `POST /auth/mfa/setup` (generates secret + QR), `POST /auth/mfa/verify-setup` (confirms with a real code, activates MFA, issues 10 one-time backup codes shown once), `POST /auth/mfa/disable` (requires password re-confirmation, not just an existing session).

**TOTP implementation is hand-rolled, not a library** - `backend/src/utils/totp.ts` implements RFC 6238/4226 (HMAC-SHA1 + Base32) directly on `node:crypto`, matching this codebase's existing precedent of hand-rolling JWT (`utils/jwt.ts`) rather than pulling in `jsonwebtoken`. Tried `otplib` v13 first; its `@noble`/`@scure` crypto dependencies ship ESM-only and broke this project's Jest/CommonJS test setup (`SyntaxError: Unexpected token 'export'` from inside `node_modules`). Rather than fighting `transformIgnorePatterns` through an unaudited third-party dependency chain, wrote the ~60-line algorithm directly - independently cross-checked against Python's `pyotp` during development to confirm real RFC-correctness, not just self-consistency (a hand-rolled generator that only validates against its own hand-rolled verifier proves nothing). `qrcode` (well-established, plain CommonJS) is used as-is for the enrollment QR image. Backup codes are hashed with the existing `hashPassword`/`verifyPassword` (PBKDF2) pair rather than a new dependency, mirroring how this codebase already treats one secret-hashing problem as one solved problem.

**Real bug found and fixed while live-verifying the flow**: `authRateLimiter` (10 req/60s, meant for pre-auth brute-force protection) was mounted across the *entire* `/api/v1/auth` router - including already-authenticated routes (`GET /me`, `POST /mfa/setup`, `POST /mfa/disable`) that don't need it, since `authenticateJwt` already gates them. A realistic single-session flow (enable 2FA -> confirm -> log out -> log back in with a code -> later disable) genuinely hit this 429 during Playwright verification. Same root-cause pattern as the `onboardingRateLimiter` fix earlier this session: moved `authRateLimiter` off the router-level mount and onto only the actual unauthenticated credential-guessing routes (`/register`, `/verify`, `/resend-verification`, `/login`, `/login/verify-mfa`, `/verify-token`, `/invitation/:token`, `/accept-invitation`); authenticated routes now correctly fall back to the already-global 100 req/60s `apiRateLimiter`.

**Frontend:** `Login.tsx` is now a two-step form - a second "Two-factor verification" step appears when the backend responds `mfaRequired: true`, accepting either a live code or (via a toggle) a backup code, calling the exact same `login()` context function on success as the no-MFA path. New "Security" tab in `Settings.tsx`: shows current status, an "Enable 2FA" flow (QR code + manual-entry secret + confirm code + one-time backup-codes display with a copy button), and a password-confirmed "Disable 2FA" flow. `AuthContext` gained `refreshUser()` (re-fetches `/auth/me` without a full re-login) so the Security tab's status reflects enable/disable immediately.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b && vite build` (frontend) both clean, including a from-scratch `npm ci --omit=dev` reproduction of Render's production build (new `qrcode`/`@types/qrcode` dependencies land correctly in `dependencies`, not `devDependencies`). New `backend/src/tests/mfa.test.ts` (8 tests: no-MFA login unaffected, full enrollment flow, MFA-required challenge issued, `mfaPending` token rejected by `authenticateJwt` on protected routes, wrong-code rejection, backup-code single-use enforcement, password-gated disable) - full suite now 490/490. Live-verified end-to-end via Playwright against the real dev stack: enabled 2FA (real QR code rendered, secret extracted from the page and fed through an independent TOTP computation to prove genuine interop, not a canned code), confirmed setup, logged out and back in through the real two-step UI, rejected a wrong code before accepting the real one, disabled MFA with password confirmation, and confirmed a subsequent login correctly skips the challenge - the exact rate-limiter bug above was caught and fixed during this same live run, not just in the isolated Jest suite.

**Known simplification, not addressed here**: the TOTP secret is stored in plaintext on `User` (no column-level encryption at rest exists anywhere in this codebase to reuse) - mirrors the existing plaintext `emailVerificationToken`/`smsVerificationCode` pattern. Acceptable for a one-time verification code, more debatable for a long-lived shared secret; flagged honestly rather than silently accepted, worth revisiting if this becomes a real audit finding.

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260811233001_add_user_mfa_fields/` (new), `backend/src/utils/totp.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/auth.ts`, `backend/src/middleware/authMiddleware.ts`, `backend/src/middleware/rateLimiterMiddleware.ts` (no change, just re-scoped usage), `backend/src/utils/jwt.ts`, `backend/src/repository/userRepository.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/src/tests/mfa.test.ts` (new), `backend/package.json`, `backend/package-lock.json`, `frontend/src/pages/auth/Login.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`, `frontend/src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-11] - Fixed: HR (and Auditor) Role Was 403'd Out of Expense Claims Entirely; Hid the Dead "Preferences" Link for Settings-Restricted Roles

**What/Why:** User asked what the HR role actually gets access to. Answering that from the code surfaced a real bug: `backend/src/routes/expenseClaims.ts`'s `GET /`, `GET /:id`, and `POST /` were gated with `requireRole('Viewer')` only. `HR` (and, found along the way, `Auditor`) are "scoped" roles in `rbacMiddleware.ts` (`SCOPED_ROLES`) that deliberately do **not** get hierarchy-based fallback access - they only pass a `requireRole()` check when named explicitly in the allowed list. Since neither was listed, an HR or Auditor user hit a 403 just opening the Expense Claims page or trying to submit their own claim - despite the frontend (`ExpenseClaims.tsx`'s `RESTRICTED_DECIDER_ROLES` set, and both roles' nav configs in `navigation.ts`) being explicitly built around "HR/Auditor can view + submit, only Accountant+ can decide/reimburse." The route's own comment even says "Any tenant member may file a claim... intentionally the loosest role gate" - the code just didn't actually implement that for scoped roles. Fixed by adding `'Auditor', 'HR'` to all three `requireRole()` calls (left `decide`/`reimburse` at `Accountant`-only, which was already correct).

**Also fixed:** the Header's profile-menu "Preferences" link navigated to `/settings` unconditionally for every role, including the four roles (`shop manager`, `cashier`, `hr`, `auditor`) that `App.tsx`'s route guard (`SETTINGS_RESTRICTED_ROLES`) already redirects straight back out of `/settings` - so those roles saw a settings link that did nothing but bounce them to `/dashboard`. Moved `SETTINGS_RESTRICTED_ROLES` out of `App.tsx` into `lib/navigation.ts` as a shared export (alongside the existing `RESTRICTED_ROLE_NAV`/`getVisibleHrefs` single-source-of-truth pattern for role-based nav) with a new `isSettingsRestricted()` helper, and used it in `Header.tsx` to hide the "Preferences" button entirely for those roles instead of just relying on the route guard to catch the click after the fact.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. Added a new backend test (`expenseClaims.test.ts`: "lets HR view and file claims... but not decide them") proving an HR user can now `GET`/`POST` claims and still gets 403 on `/decide` - full suite (482 tests) passing. Live-verified end-to-end via Playwright: onboarded a tenant, invited an HR user, confirmed via direct API calls that HR's `GET`/`POST /expense-claims` now return 200/201, then through the real UI confirmed the sidebar only shows Dashboard/Expense Claims/Team Management, the profile dropdown no longer shows "Preferences" (only "Sign out"), typing `/settings` directly still redirects to `/dashboard`, the Expense Claims page loads cleanly and shows a claim filed via the API with no errors, and Team Management renders as a genuinely read-only roster (no Invite/Actions controls) for HR.

**Known related gap, not fixed (flagged to user, out of scope for this change):** the Dashboard's revenue/expense/net-profit tiles also 403 for HR (same root cause - the P&L and ledger-summary routes don't list `HR`/`Auditor` either), so HR sees `$0.00` placeholders instead of real figures or a clean "not available" message on their one shared landing page.

**Files:** `backend/src/routes/expenseClaims.ts`, `backend/src/tests/expenseClaims.test.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Header.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-11] - SMTP Diagnostic Answered (Still Blocked) + Removed Scaffolding; Fixed Onboarding Rate Limiter Throttling Admin Dashboard Reads

**SMTP result:** Ran the diagnostic live against `https://ledgio-backend.onrender.com/api/v1/diagnostics/test-smtp` with real `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` set in Render. Both ports failed identically to the 2026-07-29 result: `{"port":465,"error":"Connection timeout"}`, `{"port":587,"error":"Connection timeout"}`. Confirms Render's outbound SMTP block is a platform-wide network restriction, not gated by billing/plan tier - adding a payment method does not lift it. SendGrid's HTTP API remains the only working path; no code change needed since `EmailService.ts` was never touched. Question answered, so removed the diagnostic scaffolding entirely: `backend/src/routes/diagnostics.ts` deleted, its mount removed from `app.ts`, `nodemailer`/`@types/nodemailer` uninstalled, and `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` removed from `render.yaml`/`.env.example` (still present as unused values in the Render dashboard - user was told to remove them there manually, and separately to rotate `BROADCAST_MASTER_SECRET` after it was inadvertently exposed in a pasted browser console log during this test).

**Real bug found and fixed along the way:** while triggering the SMTP diagnostic, the user hit repeated `429`s just from normal Admin Core Engine dashboard use (loading the tenant roster). Root cause: `app.ts` mounted `onboardingRateLimiter` (5 req/60s per IP - sized to block tenant-*creation* abuse) across the **entire** `/api/v1/tenants` router, not just the actual onboarding endpoints - so it was also throttling `GET /` (admin tenant roster), `GET /current`, `GET /members`, etc. A couple of dashboard refreshes was enough to exhaust the same 5-request budget meant for `POST /onboard`/`POST /admin-onboard`. Fixed by moving `onboardingRateLimiter` off the router-level mount and applying it directly to only those two POST routes in `tenants.ts`; every other route on the router now falls back to the already-global `apiRateLimiter` (100 req/60s) applied to all of `/api/` in `app.ts`.
