**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; full production build reproduced Render's exact `npm ci --omit=dev` install and compiled clean; full backend suite (481 tests) passing.

**Files:** `backend/src/routes/diagnostics.ts` (deleted), `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `backend/package.json`, `backend/package-lock.json`, `backend/.env.example`, `render.yaml`.

## [Date: 2026-08-11] - Temporary Diagnostic: Re-test Direct SMTP Now That Render Billing Is Active

**What/Why:** Prior history (2026-07-29 entries below) established that Render blocks outbound SMTP entirely - `ETIMEDOUT` on both port 465 and port 587/STARTTLS - which is why email moved off `nodemailer`/Gmail SMTP to SendGrid's HTTP API and `nodemailer` was removed from the codebase. User added a payment method to their Render account and asked to re-test whether that lifts the SMTP block, explicitly choosing to verify this empirically (flagged to them first that Render documents the SMTP restriction as applying across all plans, not just free tier, so this was very likely to reproduce the same failure - proceeded at their request to rule it out directly rather than relying on secondhand documentation).

**What was added:** `nodemailer`/`@types/nodemailer` reinstalled as real `dependencies` (not `devDependencies` - Render's `npm ci` omits dev deps at build time, same lesson as the `@types/ws` fix below). New passcode-gated `POST /api/v1/diagnostics/test-smtp` (`backend/src/routes/diagnostics.ts`, mounted in `app.ts`), gated by the same master broadcast passcode used elsewhere (`BroadcastService.verifyPasscode`) rather than a tenant JWT. Takes `{ passcode, to }`, attempts a direct Gmail SMTP send on both port 465 (implicit TLS) and port 587 (STARTTLS) using new diagnostic-only `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` env vars (Gmail address + App Password), and returns the exact success/error for each port. This is entirely separate from `EmailService.ts`/SendGrid, which remains the app's real send path and is unaffected either way. Added `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` to `render.yaml` (`sync: false`, both commented as diagnostic-only) and `.env.example`.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; full production build reproduced Render's exact `npm ci --omit=dev` install path and compiled clean. Full backend suite (481 tests) passing. Not yet live-tested against Render itself - needs `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` set in the Render dashboard and a real call to the endpoint once deployed; result to be recorded here as a follow-up entry. **This diagnostic route and its dependencies should be removed once the question is answered** - it is not meant to become a permanent part of the app.

**Files:** `backend/src/routes/diagnostics.ts` (new), `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/package.json`, `backend/package-lock.json`, `backend/.env.example`, `render.yaml`.

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - Extended Transactional Audit Log Writes (`recordAuditLogTx`) to the Remaining Call Sites

**What/Why:** follow-up to the same-day audit-trail hardening pass, which deliberately scoped `recordAuditLogTx` to the ledger/voucher/master flows the spec called out by name and left ~55 other call sites on the old best-effort `recordAuditLog()`. Went through every remaining site individually rather than blanket-converting: a site only gets `recordAuditLogTx` if it actually has a data mutation for the audit entry to describe and desync from. Migrated **40 call sites** across `expenseClaimService.ts`, `taxRateService.ts`, `creditDebitNoteService.ts`, `budgetService.ts`, `fiscalPeriodService.ts`, `recurringTransactionService.ts`, `approvalWorkflowService.ts`, and `routes/{auth,tenants,bills,banking,momo,teller,cashTill,inventory}.ts` - each site's DB write(s) and its audit entry now commit or roll back together, using the same pattern as the earlier pass (either an existing `withTenantDb`/`withCurrentTenantDb` block, or a new `prisma.$transaction()` wrapped around what was previously a bare sequential write for shared-schema tables with no existing transaction boundary).

**Left un-migrated, deliberately:** 16 remaining `recordAuditLog()` sites, all confirmed to have no associated data mutation to desync from - they're pure event logs, not mutation-adjacent audit entries: SMS gateway failures, email dispatch outcomes, admin broadcast sends, login success/failure/logout (no DB write happens on any of these paths - confirmed by reading each one), a cash-sale sync-failure log that explicitly creates no `CashSale` row (a client-side sale that was never actually completed server-side), the data-export-downloaded log (a read event, not a mutation), and the inventory bulk-import summary log (explained below). Also left as-is: `POST /tenants/admin-onboard`'s tenant-creation audit log, since `onboardTenant()` is a multi-step process involving dynamic Postgres schema provisioning and DDL migrations that doesn't cleanly fit inside one DML transaction - flagged as further follow-up, not silently skipped.

**Real bug found and fixed along the way:** making `POST /inventory/items/bulk`'s summary audit log transactional with its row-creation loop broke a real, previously-passing test (`reports a per-row failure without discarding the other valid rows`) with a 500. Root cause: all rows in a bulk-import batch shared **one** DB transaction with a per-row `try/catch` around each row's insert - but a Postgres constraint violation (e.g. a duplicate SKU) aborts the *entire* enclosing transaction, not just the one statement. The existing code only "worked" because nothing else queried that transaction after a caught row failure - a bare `COMMIT` on an already-aborted Postgres transaction is a **silent no-op** (behaves like `ROLLBACK` with no error raised), so every already-"succeeded" row in the same batch was actually being silently discarded whenever a later row failed, with the JSON response's `created` array never reflecting real persisted state. Adding one more query (the audit write) after the loop was the first thing to ever actually touch that poisoned transaction and surface the failure. Fixed properly, not routed around: each row in `/items/bulk` now runs in its own `withCurrentTenantDb` transaction, so one row's constraint violation can no longer roll back its siblings' already-committed inserts. The summary audit log for this endpoint stays on best-effort `recordAuditLog()` (not `recordAuditLogTx`) since it now summarizes N independently-committed transactions, not one atomic change.

**Files:** `backend/src/services/{expenseClaimService,taxRateService,creditDebitNoteService,budgetService,fiscalPeriodService,recurringTransactionService,approvalWorkflowService}.ts`, `backend/src/routes/{auth,tenants,bills,banking,momo,teller,cashTill,inventory}.ts`. Full backend suite (481 tests) and the backend build verified clean, twice in a row.

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - Ledgio Trust-First Features: Audit Trail Hardening, Full Data Export, Compliance Tracking, Guided Onboarding Wizard

**What/Why:** a spec review of Ledgio's "trust-first onboarding & audit" positioning (transparent audit trails, easy data export, guided onboarding with a hard trial-balance gate) against the actual codebase found the audit trail was real but had two spec-critical gaps, data export/compliance tracking didn't exist as dedicated features, and the guided onboarding wizard didn't exist at all. All four gaps closed this pass.

**1. Audit log writes made transactional with the data change (security-critical, per spec).** `recordAuditLog()` was previously called *after* the DB transaction for journal entries/accounts/invoice payments/stock adjustments committed, in a try/catch that explicitly swallowed failures - a crash between commit and audit write could leave a change unaudited. Added `recordAuditLogTx(client, input)` (`auditLogService.ts`) which writes inside the caller's own `withTenantDb` transaction and throws on failure (rolling the whole transaction back), and moved the audit write for `journalEntryService.ts` (create/post/void/reversal, including the previously-unaudited DRAFT-void path), `accountService.ts` (create/update/delete), `invoicePaymentService.ts` (markInvoicePaid), and `routes/inventory.ts` (stock adjustments) into their existing transactions.

**2. `audit_logs` made DB-level append-only.** New migration (`20260810150000_enforce_audit_log_append_only`) adds `BEFORE UPDATE`/`BEFORE DELETE` triggers that unconditionally `RAISE EXCEPTION` - blocks even the table owner (plain `REVOKE` wouldn't, since owners bypass grant checks), matching the spec's "enforced at the DB level, not just app logic." Verified directly via psql (`UPDATE`/`DELETE` both rejected) and via the full test suite (test cleanup hooks that delete audit rows now expect and swallow the rejection, same as pre-existing hooks already did for other reasons).

**3. Per-row "history" links added** on Journal Vouchers, Chart of Accounts, Invoices, and Vendor Bills (deep-link to `/audit-logs?entityId=X&entity=Y`) - previously the only tenant-facing audit view was the generic filterable `/audit-logs` page with no entry point from an actual transaction row.

**4. Full-tenant data export** (`GET /api/v1/data-export/{manifest,json,csv}`, `dataExportService.ts`) - 37 tables (every per-tenant-schema table via raw SQL matching how `accountRepository`/`journalEntryRepository`/`ledgerRepository` already have to read them - Prisma's model delegate silently returns zero rows against a non-default schema even under a correct `SET LOCAL search_path`, a real bug found and worked around while building this - plus every shared-schema table), CSV export as a ZIP with a generated `README.txt` describing each file, or a single JSON dump. No pricing-tier gate, no cooldown, restricted to tenant Admin/Owner (a role check, not a paywall) since it dumps the whole company's financials and team roster in one request. `team_members.csv` never includes password hashes or verification tokens. New "Data Export" tab in Settings.

**5. Provable "last compliance update" timestamp.** New platform-wide `ComplianceUpdate` model (no `tenantId` - the same GRA rules apply to every tenant), seeded with the real, already-documented 2026-08-01 gra.gov.gh VAT/NHIL/GETFund verification (see the 2026-08-01 entry below). `GET /api/v1/compliance/last-update` is visible to any authenticated user; writes (`POST /api/v1/compliance/updates`) are gated by the platform admin passcode (`BroadcastService.verifyPasscode`), not a per-tenant Admin role - a single tenant's admin must never be able to fabricate a compliance claim every other tenant would also see. Shown as a banner on the Tax Rates settings page.

**6. Guided onboarding wizard** (`/onboarding`, `OnboardingWizard.tsx` + `onboardingWizardService.ts`) - Business Profile (type, VAT/GRA TIN) -> Chart of Accounts (a new 29-account Ghana SME default template, `ghanaSmeChartOfAccountsTemplate.ts`, fully editable/deletable before or after seeding) -> Opening Balances -> completion checklist, computed live from real tenant data (never a set-once flag). **The trial-balance gate is a hard gate in code**: `postOpeningBalances()` posts the entered balances through the *existing* `journalEntryService.createJournalEntry()` rather than duplicating its double-entry validation, so an unbalanced entry gets the exact same "Total Debits (X) must equal Total Credits (Y)" rejection every other journal entry gets, with no soft-warning path around it - `Tenant.isLive` only flips true on that call's success. Live-verified end-to-end via Playwright: seeded 29 accounts, entered a genuinely unbalanced opening-balance set (client-side Post button correctly stays disabled), then a balanced set (posts successfully, "You're live" banner appears, checklist persists across a full page reload).

**Files:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma` (+`ComplianceUpdate` model, +`Tenant.businessType`/`vatRegistered`/`graTin`/`isLive`), 3 new migrations, `backend/src/services/{auditLogService,accountService,journalEntryService,invoicePaymentService,dataExportService,onboardingWizardService}.ts`, `backend/src/routes/{inventory,dataExport,compliance,onboardingWizard}.ts`, `backend/src/data/ghanaSmeChartOfAccountsTemplate.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, new backend tests (`dataExport.test.ts`, `compliance.test.ts`, `onboardingWizard.test.ts`), `frontend/src/pages/{journals/JournalList,accounts/ChartOfAccounts,invoices/Invoices,bills/VendorBills,settings/Settings,settings/TaxRates}.tsx`, new `frontend/src/pages/onboarding/OnboardingWizard.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`. Full backend suite (481 tests) and both frontend/backend production builds verified clean.

**Explicitly deferred, not fabricated as done:** the transactional-audit-log fix (item 1) covers the ledger/voucher/master flows the spec calls out by name; the other ~55 of 63 `recordAuditLog` call sites (logins, SMS gateway failures, broadcasts, tax rates, fiscal periods, budgets, and similar lower-stakes settings) still use the original best-effort write and would need the same `recordAuditLogTx` treatment to be fully consistent - left as follow-up work, not silently skipped.

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - Local-First Sync Pilot: IndexedDB Mirror + Optimistic Writes + Live Push (Chart of Accounts + Invoices)

**What:** the user asked for the app to feel instant - pull the tenant's data into a local database on login and keep it live-synced in the background, with full read+write (not just a read cache) and event-driven push rather than polling. Given the scale (this touches nearly every module, and the backend has two genuinely different tenant-data storage models under the hood), the approach was scoped deliberately: build the full architecture, then prove it end-to-end on two pilot modules chosen specifically to exercise both storage models, before rolling out further. See the approved plan for the full research and design writeup (three research passes covering existing offline patterns, the data model/API surface, and write-path invariants).

**Architecture:**
- **Backend transactional outbox** (`SyncChangeLog` + `SyncSequenceCounter` models, new migration): every syncable write appends one row to `sync_change_log` *in the same transaction* as the mutation (`syncChangeLogService.recordChange()`), with a per-tenant monotonic `sequence`. This gives a single authoritative change feed instead of relying on inconsistent per-table `updatedAt`/tombstone conventions, and makes a hard DELETE (which otherwise leaves no trace anywhere in this schema) visible to a syncing client for the first time.
- **`GET /sync/bootstrap`** (full current-state snapshot + current sequence) and **`GET /sync/changes?since=<sequence>`** (everything after that cursor) - new `backend/src/routes/sync.ts`.
- **Real-time push**: a new WebSocket endpoint (`backend/src/websocket/syncSocketServer.ts`, `/ws/sync`, JWT-authenticated via a query param) relays new change-log entries to connected clients; `recordChange`'s writes are additionally published to a per-tenant Redis channel (`syncChangeLogService.notifyChange()`, called *after* the transaction commits, never from inside it - a Redis publish can't be rolled back) so this works correctly even if the backend later scales beyond one instance. A reconnecting/catching-up client always falls back to `/sync/changes`, so a missed push is never a correctness issue, only a slightly-less-instant one.
- **Idempotency generalized**: every syncable write now accepts a client-generated `clientTxnId`, following `CashSale`'s already-proven pattern exactly (fast-path existence check + a real DB unique constraint + a two-phase "throw a sentinel to cleanly roll back the poisoned transaction on a race, then look the winner up in a fresh one" recovery - a bug found and fixed during this work, see below). Added to `Account` (raw-SQL tenant-schema table - new tenant migration `008_add_account_client_txn_id`) and `Invoice` (shared-schema Prisma model).
- **Frontend local mirror**: `frontend/src/lib/syncEngine.ts` - a Dexie (chosen over RxDB/PouchDB/ElectricSQL, which all assume a sync protocol/backend shape this bespoke REST+Prisma API doesn't have) IndexedDB database with `accounts`/`invoices`/`outbox`/`meta` tables. `useSyncEngineLifecycle` (mounted once at the app root, mirroring how `TenantSettingsContext` already reacts to `token`) bootstraps on login, opens the WebSocket, and tears everything down + wipes local data on logout (shared-device safety).
- **Optimistic writes**: `createAccountLocalFirst`/`updateAccountLocalFirst`/`createInvoiceLocalFirst`/`payInvoiceLocalFirst` write an optimistic record to IndexedDB *instantly* (a temp client-generated ID for creates, reconciled to the real server ID once the request lands) and queue the real request in an outbox, flushed sequentially (matching `saleSyncQueue.ts`'s established pattern) on: immediate enqueue, `online` event, WebSocket reconnect, and a 15s fallback interval. A genuine server rejection (e.g. a duplicate account code the client can't validate locally) marks the record `_failed` with a visible "Needs attention" badge rather than silently retrying forever or silently vanishing - the record is never lost, only ever reconciled or flagged.
- **`ChartOfAccounts.tsx`/`useAccounts.ts` and `Invoices.tsx`** now render straight from the local IndexedDB mirror via `useLiveQuery` (`dexie-react-hooks`) instead of a network fetch on every mount - pages render instantly from cache and update reactively as the outbox/push pipeline reconciles. Both pages needed only their data-fetching internals swapped, not their JSX. Credit-note issuance and MTN MoMo/TheTeller payment confirmation (write paths that mutate an invoice's status but aren't part of this pilot's outbox) get a `resyncInvoicesFromServer()` call instead, since there's no change-log entry for those paths yet.

**Bugs found and fixed during this work:**
- The two new P2002/23505-recovery blocks (`invoices.ts`, `accountService.ts`) initially tried to look up the winning record *inside the same transaction* that had just failed - but a failed statement poisons the rest of that Postgres transaction ("current transaction is aborted"), so the recovery query itself failed. Fixed by adopting the exact two-phase pattern `cashTill.ts`'s `DuplicateSaleReplayError` already uses: throw a sentinel to unwind the poisoned transaction cleanly, then run the lookup in a fresh one. Caught by the new `syncChangeLog.test.ts`'s concurrent-duplicate-create tests, not just a POS-only concern.
- The WebSocket→Redis pub/sub bridge silently never delivered a single push: the subscriber connection (`redis.duplicate()`) inherits the main client's `lazyConnect: true` + `enableOfflineQueue: false` config, and `psubscribe()` was called without first awaiting an explicit `.connect()` - the command was rejected outright ("Stream isn't writeable...") before the lazy connection had actually opened, caught only by a `.catch()` that logged a warning nobody was watching. Found via a raw-WebSocket diagnostic script that isolated the backend push path from the frontend client, confirming create-account succeeded but zero push arrived; fixed by explicitly awaiting `subscriber.connect()` before subscribing.

**Deliberately out of scope this pass** (see the plan for the full phase-2+ order): Vendor Bills/Customers/Vendors next (same shape as Invoices), then Journal Entries (needs the fiscal-period-closed/approval-gate "needs attention" handling built out), then Inventory (needs offline-stock-reservation care, the same class of problem already flagged for POS), then the rest. Reports/analytics stay server-computed. `AuditLog`, `User`/`Invitation`/`WarehouseAccess`, `Tenant` config, and third-party payment-gateway polling state are never candidates for local sync. Also not addressed: a brief empty-list flash is still possible on `Invoices.tsx`/`ChartOfAccounts.tsx` if a page is opened before the very first bootstrap after login finishes (unlike the currency fix earlier this session, `ProtectedRoute`'s loading gate wasn't extended to also wait on sync bootstrap - a deliberate, minor, accepted scope boundary for this pass, not a regression).

**Verification:** `tsc -b`/`tsc --noEmit` clean both sides. Full backend suite (now including the new `syncChangeLog.test.ts`) passes 466/466, twice in a row. `npx vite build` (PWA/service-worker layer unaffected) clean. Live-verified end-to-end against the real dev stack via Playwright and a raw-WebSocket diagnostic script: confirmed bootstrap populates IndexedDB on login; created an account while the browser context was genuinely offline (`context.setOffline(true)`) and confirmed it rendered instantly with a "Syncing..." badge; reconnected and confirmed the badge cleared once the outbox flushed; confirmed a *second, separate logged-in session* for the same tenant received the new account via the WebSocket push with no manual refresh; confirmed a genuinely-rejected duplicate-code create surfaced "Needs attention" instead of silently disappearing or silently succeeding.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, new migration `20260810145007_add_sync_change_log_and_invoice_client_txn_id`, `backend/src/database/migrations/tenantMigrations.ts` (v8), `backend/src/services/syncChangeLogService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/sync.ts` (new), `backend/src/websocket/syncSocketServer.ts` (new), `backend/src/index.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/src/repository/accountRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/accountService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/accounts.ts`, `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/services/invoicePaymentService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/syncChangeLog.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/accountingSchema.test.ts`; `frontend/src/lib/syncEngine.ts` (new), `frontend/src/hooks/useSyncEngineLifecycle.ts` (new), `frontend/src/hooks/useAccounts.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/accounts/ChartOfAccounts.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/api.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - Fixed Two Frontend Session/Perf Bugs: Silent 401 Loop on Session Expiry + Currency Flash on Every Navigation

**What (bug 1 - silent 401 loop):** the user pasted a browser console dump from the live production app showing a wall of repeated `401 (Unauthorized)` errors across many endpoints (`/notifications`, `/accounts`, `/tenants/current`, `/fiscal-periods`, `/ledgers/summary`, `/analytics/export/pdf`) with the page just sitting there broken - no login redirect, no explanation. Root cause: `api.ts`'s axios response interceptor, on any 401, cleared the `accountgo-token` localStorage key directly but never touched `AuthContext`'s in-memory `token`/`user` React state (it can't - it's a plain module, not a component). `ProtectedRoute` gates purely on that in-memory state, so once a session's JWT went stale (24h TTL) mid-session, the interceptor's silent localStorage wipe left `ProtectedRoute` still rendering the full authenticated shell while every subsequent request now had no Authorization header at all - every widget on the page 401ing forever with zero user-facing indication, exactly reproducing the pasted symptom.

**What (bug 2 - currency flash + redundant fetches):** the user separately observed the PWA briefly showing "$" before flipping to "GH₵" when navigating to Invoices, and described the app as generally slow. Root cause: `useTenantSettings()` was a plain hook, and every one of its 15 call sites (`Dashboard`, `useAccounts.ts`, `ChartOfAccounts.tsx`, `Invoices.tsx`, `VendorBills.tsx`, `ExpenseClaims.tsx`, `JournalBuilder.tsx`, `GeneralLedger.tsx`, `BalanceSheet.tsx`, `ProfitAndLoss.tsx`, `CashFlowStatement.tsx`, `CashFlowForecast.tsx`, `KpiDashboard.tsx`, `Settings.tsx`) mounted its own independent `useState(DEFAULT_TENANT)` (`baseCurrency: 'USD'`) and fired its own fresh `GET /tenants/current` on every single mount - so every client-side navigation re-rendered starting from the hardcoded USD default before flipping to the real currency a moment later once that page's own fetch resolved, and fired a fully redundant network round-trip per page visit (some pages, like Chart of Accounts, doubled up since `useAccounts()` also calls it internally).

**Fix (bug 1):** `api.ts`'s 401 handler now also clears the previously-leaked `accountgo-tenant-id` key, and (skipping `/auth/login` itself, which legitimately 401s on bad credentials and has its own inline error handling in `Login.tsx`) dispatches a `window` `'ledgio:session-expired'` DOM event. `AuthContext.tsx` listens for it and calls its existing `logout()` (properly clears React state, which `ProtectedRoute` already reacts to correctly) plus a one-time toast ("Your session has expired. Please log in again.") - deduped via a ref so a burst of parallel 401s (e.g. the dashboard's several simultaneous calls) only shows one toast, not five.

**Fix (bug 2):** Converted `useTenantSettings` from a per-component hook into a shared `TenantSettingsContext` (new `frontend/src/contexts/TenantSettingsContext.tsx`, mounted once in `App.tsx` inside `AuthProvider`), fetched exactly once per login instead of once per page visit - `frontend/src/hooks/useTenantSettings.ts` is now a one-line re-export, so all 15 existing call sites needed zero changes. `ProtectedRoute`'s existing `isLoading` gate (already used for the `/auth/me` check) was extended to also wait on the new context's `isLoading`, so pages never paint the USD default at all rather than merely reducing how often they do.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` and `npx vite build` (PWA/service-worker layer unaffected) both clean. Live-verified against the real dev stack via Playwright:
- Bug 1: logged in, corrupted the token mid-session (no page reload, matching the real 24h-expiry scenario), triggered a client-side nav - confirmed exactly one "session has expired" toast, redirect to `/login`, and both localStorage keys cleared; separately confirmed a genuine wrong-password login attempt still shows its own inline error and does *not* fire the session-expired toast.
- Bug 2: onboarded a fresh GHS tenant, logged in, and clicked through 5 real sidebar navigations (Invoices, Vendor Bills, Journal Vouchers, P&L, back to Dashboard) - confirmed zero `$` sightings at any point (checked both immediately on click and after settling) and confirmed via network-request instrumentation that `GET /tenants/current` fired exactly once total across the whole session, not once per navigation.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/lib/api.ts`, `frontend/src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx`, `frontend/src/contexts/TenantSettingsContext.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/hooks/useTenantSettings.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - Fixed: Remaining "$" Instead of Tenant's Real Base Currency (GLR, Vendor Bills, Invoices, Journal Builder, Expense Claims)

**What:** the user reported still seeing "$" on the General Ledger Report, Vendor Bills, and Invoices pages, despite the same class of bug already being fixed once for Chart of Accounts (2026-08-09) - a relapse report. Investigating the exact root-cause pattern from that earlier fix (a component reading a stale per-record `currency` field, or a `formatCurrency` helper hardcoded/defaulted to `"USD"`, instead of the tenant's real `baseCurrency` from `useTenantSettings()`) turned up the same bug in five places, not just the three the user named - the General Ledger Report and four more pages the user hadn't mentioned. Also swept the rest of the frontend for any other offline-capable feature besides the Hybrid-offline POS (already hardened in the two prior 2026-08-10 entries above) - confirmed the PWA/Workbox app-shell caching layer is the only other one, and a real `vite build` confirms it still generates a correct service worker with no code changes needed there.

**Fix:**
- `GeneralLedger.tsx`: `formatCurrency` read the selected account's own stored `currency` field (`account?.currency || "USD"`) - but per `useAccounts.ts`'s own comment, every account's `currency` always just mirrors the tenant's base currency at creation time (the ledger is single-currency by design, no per-account currency picker exists in the UI). Now reads `useTenantSettings().settings.baseCurrency` directly, the same source Chart of Accounts/P&L/Balance Sheet already use.
- `VendorBills.tsx` / `Invoices.tsx`: their `formatCurrency(amt, curr = "USD")` helpers defaulted to `"USD"` for the AP/AR summary tiles that call it with no explicit currency - now default to `settings.baseCurrency`. Individual bill/invoice rows already pass their own real per-document `currency` explicitly (legitimate multi-currency data) and were left untouched. `VendorBills.tsx`'s `resetBillForm()` (called both after a successful bill create and every time the "New Bill" modal opens) had `setCurrency("USD")` hardcoded - now seeds it with `settings.baseCurrency`. `Invoices.tsx`'s new-invoice currency picker had no equivalent reset point, so it gets a `useEffect` that snaps `currency` to `settings.baseCurrency` only while it's still at the initial `"USD"` default, so it never clobbers a currency the user already picked.
- `JournalBuilder.tsx`: its running debit/credit-total `formatCurrency` had no parameter at all - always hardcoded `currency: "USD"`. Now reads `settings.baseCurrency`.
- `ExpenseClaims.tsx`: the new-claim form's currency `<select>` initializes to `"USD"` before real settings load; added the same "snap-once-if-still-default" `useEffect` pattern as `Invoices.tsx`.
- Also found and fixed a related-but-distinct bug while live-verifying: `VendorBills.tsx`'s non-itemized "New Bill" form had a static `"Bill Amount ($)"` label sitting right next to its own currency picker - misleading whenever a non-USD currency was selected. Changed to just `"Bill Amount"`.
- Deliberately left unchanged: `BankReconciliation.tsx`'s `formatCurrency` default (only reachable if a real Mono-connected account's `currency` is null, which shouldn't happen), `Register.tsx`'s pre-signup `baseCurrency: "USD"` form default (no tenant exists yet to read), and every `<option value="USD">USD ($)</option>` entry in currency-picker dropdowns (legitimate option label for the USD choice itself, not a display bug).

**Verification:** `tsc -b` (frontend) clean both before and after the label fix. Live-verified end-to-end against the real dev stack via Playwright: onboarded a fresh tenant with `baseCurrency: 'GHS'`, posted a real journal entry, and confirmed every one of the five pages now renders `GHS`/`GH₵` instead of `$` - General Ledger Report's opening/closing balance and debit/credit columns, Invoices' AR/Paid summary tiles and new-invoice currency picker default, Vendor Bills' AP/Paid summary tiles, Journal Builder's running totals, and the Expense Claims new-claim currency picker default - screenshotted each. Confirmed via a direct `GET /tenants/current` call that `baseCurrency` is correctly persisted and retrievable (the `admin-onboard` response itself omitting `baseCurrency` from its returned `tenant` object is a separate, pre-existing, minor response-shape gap - not a data-storage bug - left out of scope since the user's report was about display, not onboarding).
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/pages/reports/GeneralLedger.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/bills/VendorBills.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/journals/JournalBuilder.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/expenses/ExpenseClaims.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - Fixed: Hybrid-Offline POS Could Oversell the Same Item Across Two Local Carts

**What:** the previous fix's own writeup flagged a real, separate gap: the cart's "remaining stock" check only ever subtracted the *current* in-memory cart from raw `stockQty` - it had no idea about *other* sales this same device had already queued locally but not yet synced. A cashier who sold the last unit of an item, had it queue offline, then started a second cart during the same outage would see the item still showing as available and could sell it again - two locally-queued sales both claiming the same one unit of real stock, with the conflict only surfacing (as a "needs attention" failure) once the second sale finally reached the server at sync time.

**Fix:** `PointOfSale.tsx` now computes a `pendingReservedByItemId` map from the sync queue's own `pendingSales` (summing quantities per item across every sale not in `"failed"` status - a failed sale was explicitly rejected by the server, often *because of* a real stock conflict, so its items are no longer assumed reserved once failed) and combines it with the current cart via a new `unavailableQtyFor(itemId)` helper, replacing the old cart-only `cartQtyFor` in every place stock availability is checked or displayed: `addToCart`'s guard, `adjustCartQty`'s "+" cap, and the search-list's "N unit(s) left" display and disabled state. When an item is fully claimed by a pending sale, the list now also shows "(N held by pending offline sale(s))" so the cashier understands *why*, rather than just seeing it silently disabled. This is a same-device, client-side-only soft reservation - it still doesn't (and can't, without a bigger schema change) coordinate across two different devices selling from the same warehouse; updated the pre-existing "no offline stock reservation" comments in `saleSyncQueue.ts`/`cashTill.ts`/`PointOfSale.tsx` to say "no *server-side*" reservation, so a future reader doesn't read them as still fully true after this change.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` (frontend) clean. Live-verified against the real dev stack via Playwright: created an item with exactly 1 unit of stock, went offline, sold that 1 unit (queues locally), then tried to start a second cart for the same item in the same session - confirmed it now shows "0 unit left (1 held by pending offline sale)" with the Add button disabled, where it previously would have shown "1 unit left" and allowed a second, conflicting sale to queue.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/pages/pos/PointOfSale.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/saleSyncQueue.ts`, `backend/src/routes/cashTill.ts` (comment-only).

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - Fixed: Hybrid-Offline POS Stranded a Cashier on a Page Reload During an Outage

**What:** a self-review of the Hybrid-offline POS feature (Phases 1-2, 2026-08-08) found a real gap: `offlineDb.ts` already saved a catalog snapshot to IndexedDB on every successful load ("the only way a cashier can still search/add products to a cart once connectivity drops," per its own code comment) but `getCatalogSnapshot()` was never actually called anywhere - dead code. Worse, `till` state and the warehouse roster had no offline persistence at all. The result: connectivity dropping *mid-session* worked fine (React state just stayed put), but a page reload or reopen *during* an outage reset `till` to `null` and `items`/`warehouses` to empty, landing the cashier on the "Open Till" form - unable to ring up a single sale - even though a till was genuinely still open server-side. This directly contradicted the feature's stated purpose and wasn't documented anywhere as a known limitation.

**Fix:**
- `offlineDb.ts` gained two new IndexedDB stores (`tillSnapshot` keyed by warehouseId, `warehousesSnapshot` as a singleton) alongside the existing `catalogSnapshot`/`pendingSales`, with safe `objectStoreNames.contains()`-guarded upgrades (DB version 1 → 2).
- `PointOfSale.tsx`'s `fetchWarehouses`/`fetchTillAndItems` now persist a snapshot on every successful live fetch, and on a genuine network failure (`!err.response`, the same check already used to detect offline-vs-real-rejection elsewhere in this file) fall back to the cached warehouse roster, till, and catalog instead of resetting to empty/null. The till snapshot is explicitly cleared on close (and whenever a live fetch confirms no till is open), so a closed till never gets resurrected from a stale cache.
- A new "Showing till and catalog data saved from before this outage" banner makes clear this is cached, not live, data - stock counts in particular can't reflect sales made since the snapshot (including this device's own still-unsynced queue - there's still no offline stock reservation anywhere in this schema, an existing, separate, and still-open limitation).
- Added a `window` `online` listener in `PointOfSale.tsx` itself (the existing one in `saleSyncQueue.ts`'s `useSaleSyncQueue` only refetches when a pending sale actually syncs) so a session that was showing offline-cached data with nothing queued - e.g. just browsing, or everything already synced - refreshes to live truth promptly once connectivity returns, rather than staying stale indefinitely.

**Not fixed, flagged as a separate pre-existing gap:** the cart's "remaining stock" check only accounts for the *current* in-memory cart, not other sales already queued-but-unsynced in the same offline session - a cashier could oversell the last unit of an item twice across two separate carts during one outage before either syncs. Would need a bigger design call (optimistic local stock decrement across the pending queue, and what happens to it if a queued sale later fails/is cancelled) - out of scope for this fix.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` (frontend) clean. Live-verified against the real dev stack via Playwright, reproducing the exact bug scenario: opened a till online, confirmed the till/catalog/warehouse snapshots landed in IndexedDB, went offline and navigated away and back (remounting the page, the same effect as a reload) - confirmed the till, "Close Till" button, and full searchable catalog all survived instead of showing "Open Till" as they did before the fix, with the new offline-data banner correctly shown; added an item to the cart while offline; went back online and confirmed the banner cleared and the catalog refreshed to live stock automatically within seconds, with no user action needed.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/lib/offlineDb.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/pos/PointOfSale.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - Advance the Audit Trail: Better Filters + Closing Coverage Gaps

**What:** the user asked to advance the Audit Trail beyond the filtering/CSV export shipped 2026-07-28, specifically calling out better filters. After a no-code brainstorm (per the user's explicit "don't code, let's suggest to ourselves first" instruction) covering both filter/UX gaps and coverage gaps, the user approved doing both in the priority order proposed. Two workstreams shipped together:

**Workstream A - filters/UX:**
- `AuditLog` gains a `@@index([tenantId, createdAt])` composite index (new migration `20260810063600_add_audit_log_tenant_created_at_index`) - the existing list/export queries already filter by `tenantId` and sort by `createdAt desc`.
- New `entityId` (exact match) and `ipAddress` (substring) filters added to both `buildAuditLogWhere` (`routes/auditLogs.ts`) and `buildAdminAuditLogWhere` (`routes/adminAuditLogs.ts`) - the shared where-builder pattern that keeps the list and CSV export from drifting apart.
- New `GET /audit-logs/meta/values` (tenant-scoped) and `GET /admin/audit-logs/meta/values` (platform-wide, passcode-gated) endpoints return the real distinct `action`/`entity` values actually present, feeding `<datalist>` autocomplete on the existing Action/Entity `<Input>`s in both `AuditLogs.tsx` and `AdminCoreEngine.tsx` - typo-prevention without losing freeform search or a hardcoded action-name enum that would drift from reality.
- `AuditLogs.tsx` (tenant view only) gained URL query-param sync via `useSearchParams` (an already-established pattern in this codebase - `AcceptInvitation.tsx`, `Verification.tsx`) so a filtered view survives a refresh or can be shared as a link, three quick-filter preset buttons ("My Activity", "Today", "Failed Logins Today" - no "Deletions" preset since deletion actions don't follow one consistent `*.DELETED` name pattern across every module), and a clickable Entity ID badge on each row that jumps straight to that one record's full history.
- CSV export's `Changes` column was raw `JSON.stringify(changes)` - now rendered via a new `formatAuditChanges()` helper (`utils/csvExport.ts`) as the same "field: from → to" text the in-app table already shows, used by both the tenant and admin export routes.

**Workstream B - coverage gaps:** 7 modules previously made no `recordAuditLog` call at all, so real changes to them were invisible in the trail. All now audited following the established `MODULE.PAST_TENSE_VERB` convention, reusing already-fetched before/after state wherever the surrounding code already had it (no redundant extra DB fetch):
- **Tax Rates** (`taxRateService.ts`): `TAX_RATE.CREATED` / `.UPDATED` / `.DELETED`.
- **Fiscal Periods** (`fiscalPeriodService.ts`): `FISCAL_PERIOD.CREATED` / `.CLOSED` / `.LOCKED`.
- **Budgets** (`budgetService.ts`): `BUDGET.CREATED` / `.UPDATED` / `.DELETED` - `updateBudget` already fetched the pre-update record for its own validation, reused directly rather than adding a redundant fetch.
- **Recurring Transactions** (`recurringTransactionService.ts`): `RECURRING_TXN.CREATED` / `.UPDATED` / `.DELETED` - `updateRecurringTransaction` didn't previously fetch a before-state at all; added one.
- **Approval Workflows** (`approvalWorkflowService.ts`): `APPROVAL_WORKFLOW.CREATED` / `.DECIDED` - `decideApprovalStep` already fetches both the workflow and the step before deciding, reused directly.
- **Banking** (`routes/banking.ts`): `BANK_ACCOUNT.CONNECTED` (pure create, `details`-only) / `BANK_TRANSACTION.RECONCILED` (reuses the existing inline `existing`/`updatedTx` fetch inside the reconcile handler's `withCurrentTenantDb` callback).
- **Tenant Settings** (`routes/tenants.ts`): `TENANT_SETTINGS.UPDATED` on `PUT /current` - previously had no before-fetch at all; added one via `tenantRepository.findTenantById`.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) / `tsc -b` (frontend) clean. Full backend suite run twice in a row (59 suites / 439 tests, both green) - including a pre-existing test (`auditLogFiltersAndExport.test.ts`) whose CSV-format assertion was updated to match the new human-readable Changes column rather than the old raw-JSON one. New `auditLogFilters.test.ts` (tenant-scoped entityId/ipAddress/meta-values/CSV-format coverage) and new admin-side tests appended to the existing `adminAuditLogs.test.ts`; each of the 7 newly-audited modules' existing test file gained a dedicated audit-log assertion test (`taxRates.test.ts`, `fiscalPeriodsAndBudgets.test.ts`, `recurringTransactions.test.ts`, `approvalWorkflows.test.ts`, `banking.test.ts`, `allEndpoints.test.ts` for tenant settings). Live-verified against the real dev stack via Playwright: onboarded a real tenant, created/updated an account and a tax rate, confirmed the Entity ID/IP Address filter fields and quick-filter presets render, clicked an Entity ID badge and confirmed it filters to that exact record and syncs to the URL (survives a page reload), confirmed the Action datalist populates with real distinct values (`ACCOUNT.CREATED`, `TAX_RATE.CREATED`, etc.), and confirmed the CSV export's Changes column renders as human-readable text instead of raw JSON.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/src/routes/auditLogs.ts`, `backend/src/routes/adminAuditLogs.ts`, `backend/src/utils/csvExport.ts`, `backend/src/services/taxRateService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/taxRates.ts`, `backend/src/services/fiscalPeriodService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/fiscalPeriods.ts`, `backend/src/services/budgetService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/budgets.ts`, `backend/src/services/recurringTransactionService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/recurringTransactions.ts`, `backend/src/services/approvalWorkflowService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/approvalWorkflows.ts`, `backend/src/routes/banking.ts`, `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/audit/AuditLogs.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`, plus the test files listed above.

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - Fund Accounting for Nonprofit Tenants (Churches/NGOs/Schools)

**What:** closes the #1 flagged pain point in the (still commercially unvalidated) NGO/church/school research segment - inability to separate restricted donor funds (e.g. a "Building Fund" grant) from unrestricted general operating money. Per the user's explicit decisions: (1) a new `Tenant.orgType` (`BUSINESS`/`NONPROFIT`), chosen once at onboarding, drives which nav sections a tenant sees; (2) scope is core fund tracking + per-fund reports only - no donor CRM, receipting, or grant-report templates this pass.

**Architecture note carried through the whole design:** this codebase is a hybrid multi-tenancy model - core accounting tables (`accounts`, `journal_entries`, `journal_entry_lines`, `ledgers`) live in a literal per-tenant Postgres schema (hand-migrated via `tenantMigrations.ts`), while everything else (`Invoice`, `TaxRate`, `Tenant`, etc.) is an ordinary shared-schema Prisma model filtered by `tenantId`. `Fund` is a new shared-schema model (mirrors `TaxRate`); `fund_id` columns added to `journal_entry_lines`/`ledgers` (tenantMigrations v7) are NOT real FKs across that schema boundary - validated at write time via `fundService.validateFundId`, the same cross-schema pattern `TaxRate.accountId` already established in the opposite direction.

- **Schema:** new `Fund` model (`name`, `code`, `description`, `isRestricted`, `isActive`); `Tenant.orgType` (default `BUSINESS`, immutable after onboarding); `Invoice.fundId`/`VendorBill.fundId` (real FKs, `onDelete: SetNull`).
- **Posting flow:** every dollar in this app funnels through exactly one function, `journalEntryService.createJournalEntry`/`postJournalEntry` → `ledgerRepository.postJournalEntryToLedger` - so `fundId` only needed threading through the line shape once, not reimplemented per feature. The account-level running balance in `postJournalEntryToLedger` is untouched - fund is a pure tag layered on top for filtering/reporting, never a second balance-partitioning key.
- **Critical fix required by design, not optional:** the void/reversal line-mapper in `journalEntryService.voidJournalEntry` previously dropped every field except `accountId`/`debit`/`credit`/`description` when building a reversal's lines. Without carrying `fundId` through here, voiding any fund-tagged POSTED entry would produce a reversal with no fund tag - the original stays counted against the fund in a fund-filtered report, but its reversal never cancels it out, permanently overstating that fund's balance. Fixed and covered by a dedicated regression test. The exact same bug class existed in `creditDebitNoteService.ts`'s two `JOURNAL_REVERSAL` paths (credit/debit notes against an already-paid, fund-tagged invoice/bill) - fixed there too, in this same pass rather than deferred, since leaving it unfixed would mean fund reports go quietly wrong the first time someone issues a credit note. Also found and fixed a second, easy-to-miss version of the same bug: `journalEntryRepository.ts`'s 3 read paths (`getJournalEntryById`/`getJournalEntryByEntryNumber`/`listJournalEntries`) weren't including `fund_id` in their SQL column lists, which would have silently returned `fundId: undefined` even though it was written correctly.
- **Payment posting:** `invoicePaymentService.markInvoicePaid` and `routes/bills.ts`'s `POST /:id/pay` attach the invoice's/bill's `fundId` uniformly to every line they build (Cash + tax credits + Revenue for invoices; Expense + Cash for bills) - keeps the whole payment transaction inside one fund by construction, so no separate "guard against cross-fund posting" logic was needed anywhere.
- **Reports:** `getProfitAndLoss`/`getBalanceSheet` in `reportRepository.ts` gained an optional `fundId` filter, threaded through the exact same `conditions[]`/`params[]` pattern already used for date filters (additive, not a restructuring) - a per-fund Balance Sheet/P&L is just the existing report function with one more filter, reusing 100% of the existing math/PDF/Word export. `GET /ledgers` (`listLedgerTransactions`) also gained the filter cheaply, though no frontend page currently calls that specific endpoint (General Ledger's page uses a different per-account-statement endpoint not extended this pass - noted as a trim, not a gap, since wiring it would have needed more repository work than the "cheap addition" scope justified).
- **Org type + nav:** `Register.tsx` gained an "Organization Type" selector; `orgType` rides on the JWT (no extra DB query needed on `/auth/me`, since it's immutable after onboarding); `navigation.ts`'s `getVisibleNavGroups(role, orgType)` hides "Point of Sale" and the entire "Inventory & Godowns" section and adds a "Funds" item under "Finance & Banking" for `NONPROFIT` tenants, applied before the existing role-based filtering.
- **Frontend:** new `/settings/funds` page (mirrors `TaxRates.tsx`'s page-owns-its-data pattern); Fund selector added to `Invoices.tsx`/`VendorBills.tsx` create forms and per-line to `JournalBuilder.tsx` (with a real bug caught and fixed here too: `useJournals.ts`'s `postJournal` explicitly reconstructed each line's payload without `fundId`, which would have silently dropped it exactly like the backend void-mapper bug, just client-side).

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) / `tsc -b` (frontend) clean. Full backend suite run twice in a row, both green (61 suites / 446 tests - up from 59/434 by the new `funds.test.ts` suite plus new cases added to `journalEntries.test.ts`, `paymentPosting.test.ts`, `creditDebitNotes.test.ts`, and a new `fundReports.test.ts`). New tests cover: Funds CRUD tenant isolation (404s not 403s cross-tenant, duplicate-code handling, delete-guard against invoice/bill/journal-line references with deactivate-instead succeeding); voiding a fund-tagged POSTED entry carries `fundId` through to the reversal and the fund-filtered ledger nets to exactly zero afterward; paying a fund-tagged invoice/bill posts every line with that fund's ID; a fund-tagged credit note's reversal carries the fund tag too; a fund-filtered Balance Sheet/P&L only sums that fund's rows while the unfiltered call still sums everything (regression check the new param is genuinely additive).
**Live verification:** a real bug was found and fixed mid-migration-testing - a stray semicolon inside a SQL comment in the new `tenantMigrations.ts` v7 entry (`unchanged; NULL simply means...`) confused the naive statement-splitter used by `tenantMigrationRunner.ts` (which doesn't understand `--` comments), corrupting the DDL and breaking tenant onboarding entirely until traced down and fixed (rewrote the comment to avoid the semicolon). Live-checked end-to-end against the real dev stack via Playwright + direct API calls: onboarded a real `NONPROFIT` tenant, confirmed the sidebar correctly hides Point of Sale/Inventory and shows Funds; created two real funds (restricted "Building Fund", unrestricted "General Fund") via the actual `/settings/funds` UI; confirmed the invoice-creation modal's Fund dropdown renders and defaults to the selected fund; created and paid a Building-Fund-tagged invoice (fell back to a direct API call to complete the round-trip after the UI script's submit-button selector didn't match the real "Issue Invoice" label - a scripting issue, not a product one, confirmed by the screenshot showing the modal correctly populated and ready to submit); confirmed the fund-filtered Balance Sheet/P&L showed exactly that fund's numbers; voided the payment's journal entry and confirmed the fund-filtered Balance Sheet returned to exactly 0 (proving the void-mapper fix works live, not just in the test suite); posted a manual Journal Voucher with two lines tagged to two different funds and confirmed it was accepted.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260810023000_add_fund_accounting/`, `backend/src/database/migrations/tenantMigrations.ts`, `backend/src/repository/fundRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/fundService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/funds.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/src/repository/journalEntryRepository.ts`, `backend/src/repository/ledgerRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/journalEntryService.ts`, `backend/src/services/invoicePaymentService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/bills.ts`, `backend/src/services/creditDebitNoteService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/repository/reportRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/reportingService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/reports.ts`, `backend/src/routes/ledgers.ts`, `backend/src/repository/tenantRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/tenantService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/auth.ts`, `backend/src/utils/jwt.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useProfitAndLoss.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useBalanceSheet.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useJournals.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/reports/ProfitAndLoss.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/reports/BalanceSheet.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/Funds.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/auth/Register.tsx`, `frontend/src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/ui/CommandMenu.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/bills/VendorBills.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/journals/JournalBuilder.tsx`, `frontend/src/types/accounting.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/funds.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/fundReports.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/journalEntries.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/paymentPosting.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/creditDebitNotes.test.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-08-10] - TheTeller (PaySwitch): Second, Parallel Mobile Money Provider for Telecel Cash / AirtelTigo / Zeepay / G-Money

**What:** closes the remaining gap flagged in `docs/GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`'s Mobile Money coverage - MTN MoMo collections already worked end-to-end, but Telecel Cash, AirtelTigo Money, Zeepay, and G-Money had no collection path at all. Rather than hand-building a second bespoke telco integration, primary-source research (real docs fetched from `theteller.net/documentation`) found **TheTeller (PaySwitch)**, one unified self-serve-sandbox API that covers all four of those networks (network selected via an `r-switch` field) plus MTN. Per the user's explicit decision, this ships as a fully **separate, parallel** provider next to the existing MTN-direct integration - `momoService.ts`/`momo.ts`/`MomoPaymentRequest`/the existing "Collect via MoMo" UI are untouched, and MTN is deliberately excluded from the new provider's network choices so the same real-world network can never produce two differently-shaped payment-request rows across two tables. This mirrors the codebase's existing convention (Mono and MoMo are already two fully isolated integrations with zero shared abstraction) - TheTeller becomes a third.

- New `TellerPaymentRequest` Prisma model/table (own migration, `tenantId`/`invoiceId`/`network`/`phoneNumber`/`amount`/`transactionId` (unique, TheTeller's caller-generated 12-digit numeric format, not a UUID)/`status`/`responseCode`/`failureReason`), plus `Invoice.tellerRequests` reverse relation.
- New `tellerService.ts`: HTTP Basic auth (no OAuth/token-caching needed, unlike MTN) against `TELLER_BASE_URL` (defaults to the sandbox `https://test.theteller.net`); `isTellerConfigured()` gates everything on `TELLER_API_USERNAME`/`TELLER_API_KEY`/`TELLER_MERCHANT_ID`, 503 with no fake data when unset - same contract as every other optional integration in this codebase. `processTransaction()`/`checkTransactionStatus()` call TheTeller's `POST /v1.1/transaction/process` / `GET /v1.1/users/transactions/{id}/status`. **Real behavioral difference from MTN**: TheTeller's response can resolve synchronously (`approved`/`declined`) in the same HTTP call rather than MTN's always-202-then-poll pattern - anything else/absent maps defensively to `PENDING`. No balance function - TheTeller's docs don't expose a balance endpoint, so none was invented.
- New `routes/teller.ts`, mounted at `/api/v1/teller`: history list, send-request (`requireRole('Accountant')`, server-side `VDF/ATL/TGO/ZPY/GMY` allowlist rejecting MTN even if the frontend were bypassed, 503/400/404 mirroring `momo.ts`'s contract) - and, to handle the synchronous-resolution case, the send-request handler itself calls the shared `invoicePaymentService.markInvoicePaid` immediately when TheTeller's response already came back `approved`, instead of forcing a redundant "Check Status" click. check-status endpoint mirrors MoMo's idempotent-already-resolved handling and posts through the same shared `markInvoicePaid` MoMo/manual payments already use, so correct Cash/Revenue(+tax) posting comes free with no new accounting logic.
- Frontend (`Invoices.tsx`): new "Collect via Mobile Money (Other Networks)" button and modal next to (never replacing) the existing "Collect via MoMo" one - distinct `Wallet` icon, network `<select>` (Telecel Cash / AirtelTigo Money (Airtel) / AirtelTigo Money (Tigo) / Zeepay / G-Money), and explicit copy ("Use Collect via MoMo above for MTN numbers") to prevent confusion between the two buttons. `handleSendTellerRequest` also handles the synchronous-success case (toast + close + refresh straight from the send response, not only from check-status).
- `backend/.env.example` and `render.yaml` both gained `TELLER_API_USERNAME`/`TELLER_API_KEY`/`TELLER_MERCHANT_ID`(/`TELLER_BASE_URL` in `.env.example` only, defaulted in code). **Pre-existing gap noticed, not backfilled here**: `render.yaml` had zero `MOMO_*` entries at all despite `.env.example` documenting them - MTN MoMo collections are effectively unconfigurable on the current Render deploy; flagging this for a separate small follow-up.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) / `tsc -b` (frontend) clean. Full backend suite run twice in a row, both green (59 suites / 434 tests - up from 58/427 by exactly the new `teller.test.ts` file). New `teller.test.ts` (own tenant, not shared with `momo.test.ts`) covers: unconfigured→503+zero rows; missing phone→400; invalid/MTN network→400; async happy path (process→pending, status-check→approved→invoice PAID→real ledger deltas); **synchronous happy path** (process response itself returns `approved`→invoice PAID immediately, no check-status call needed - the one real behavioral divergence from MoMo); declined path (`failureReason` set, invoice stays unpaid); already-PAID invoice→400 on a second request.
**Live verification note:** no real TheTeller sandbox credentials were available this session (would need signing up at `theteller.net/signup`), so only the unconfigured/503 path could be live-verified end-to-end rather than a real send→approve→PAID cycle against TheTeller's actual sandbox - explicitly flagging this rather than silently skipping it, per the plan. Live-checked via Playwright against the real dev stack: the new button/modal render correctly, the network dropdown correctly excludes MTN (`['Telecel Cash', 'AirtelTigo Money (Airtel)', 'AirtelTigo Money (Tigo)', 'Zeepay', 'G-Money']`), the disambiguation copy is correct, and submitting with no credentials configured surfaces a real "not configured" error toast rather than faking success. Real end-to-end verification against TheTeller's actual sandbox (approve/decline/synchronous-resolution behavior against the live API) remains a follow-up once real credentials are obtained.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260810013208_add_teller_payment_requests/`, `backend/src/services/tellerService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/teller.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/src/tests/teller.test.ts`, `backend/.env.example`, `render.yaml`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-09] - Chart of Accounts Ignored the Tenant's Configured Currency

**What:** user report - "chart accounts is showing dollars even if I set cedis in my settings." Confirmed: `ChartOfAccounts.tsx` formatted each row using that account's own `currency` field (`formatCurrency(account.balance, account.currency)`), and `useAccounts.ts`'s `createAccount()` hardcoded `currency: "USD"` into every account creation payload, completely ignoring whatever the tenant had actually set under Settings > Currency & Regional. No UI anywhere (create or edit) ever exposed a per-account currency picker, so this stored value was pure accident-of-creation-time, not a deliberate choice - confirmed via a repo-wide search that `account.currency` is read in exactly one place in the entire frontend (that same display line), so it carries no other functional meaning anywhere.

**Fix:** `ChartOfAccounts.tsx` now formats every balance using `useTenantSettings().settings.baseCurrency` - the same tenant-currency source every other report (P&L, Balance Sheet, Cash Flow, etc.) already correctly reads - instead of trusting the stored per-account field at all. `useAccounts.ts`'s `createAccount()` now sends the tenant's real `baseCurrency` instead of a hardcoded literal, so newly-created accounts stop writing the wrong value in the first place. Because the display fix no longer depends on the stored column, this is also correct immediately for every account created before this fix, with no data backfill needed.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` clean. Live-verified against the real dev stack: onboarded a fresh tenant with `baseCurrency: 'GHS'`, created an account and posted a journal entry against it, confirmed the Chart of Accounts page renders `GHS 1,500.00` / `-GHS 1,500.00` (not `$`), screenshotted for the record.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/pages/accounts/ChartOfAccounts.tsx`, `frontend/src/hooks/useAccounts.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-08-09] - Real Per-Levy Tax Accounts: Invoice Tax Posts to Its Own GL Account(s), Not Revenue

**What:** an accountant collaborating with the user flagged a real accounting-correctness gap: paying a customer invoice credited the *entire* total - subtotal **and** tax - straight to Revenue. Tax collected on behalf of the government (VAT/NHIL/GETFund, in the Ghana case this platform already models) isn't the business's income - it's a liability pending remittance. Lumping it into Revenue overstated income and left no ledger balance anywhere showing how much tax was currently owed. Confirmed with the user this should be a **per-levy** account (VAT/NHIL/GETFund each get their own destination, not one shared account) and scoped to **invoices only** (output tax) - vendor bills have no tax fields today, so input tax/recoverable VAT is a separate future addition.

**The fix builds on schema that already existed but was never wired up** - `TaxRate.accountId` (whole-rate) and `TaxRate.components`/`Invoice.taxBreakdown` (`{name, rate}[]`/`{name, rate, amount}[]`, from the earlier layered Ghana-levy feature) were both already real `Json?` columns. This pass adds an optional `accountId` to each component/breakdown-line and actually reads it at payment time - no new migration needed anywhere.

- `taxRateRepository.ts`: `TaxRateComponent` gains `accountId?: string` - conditionally spread in, never written as an explicit `null`, so a component without one still serializes to exactly `{name, rate}` (protects an existing exact-shape test assertion).
- `taxRateService.ts`: new `validateAccountIds()` confirms every referenced account (the whole-rate one and/or each component's) actually exists in the tenant's own Chart of Accounts before a tax rate is created or updated - reuses the same `accountRepository.getAccountById` lookup `journalEntryService` already uses for journal lines, so a nonexistent or cross-tenant account ID is rejected with a clear 400 rather than silently stored.
- `routes/invoices.ts`: `buildBreakdown()` now snapshots each component's `accountId` into `Invoice.taxBreakdown` at issue time (not just re-read off the `TaxRate` at payment time) - a later edit to which account a levy posts to never rewrites the accounting history of what was actually charged on an already-issued invoice.
- `invoicePaymentService.ts`'s `markInvoicePaid()` - the actual bug fix: now builds one credit line per distinct tax-destination account (merging levies that share a destination), scales each native-currency tax amount by the same `postingAmount / invoice.total` factor already used for the whole invoice (so multi-currency invoices split correctly, and single-currency tenants get byte-identical output to before), and lets Revenue absorb the subtotal plus any tax with no configured destination plus any rounding remainder - credits always sum to exactly the Cash debit. A tax-destination account deleted after the invoice was created (not a real FK - cross-schema references never are in this codebase) is silently folded into Revenue rather than hard-blocking payment of an otherwise-unrelated invoice.
- Frontend `TaxRates.tsx`: added an account `<select>` next to each layered component row and one for the simple/non-layered rate, wired to `useAccounts()` with Liability-type accounts sorted first as a visual hint (not a hard filter - any account can be picked, matching every other account picker in this codebase). The tax-rate list table now resolves and shows each component's/rate's mapped account name, so the setting is actually verifiable after creation instead of write-only.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) / `tsc -b` (frontend) clean. Full backend suite run twice in a row (58 suites / 427 tests, both green). New tests: `taxRates.test.ts` gains a "Per-levy GL account posting configuration" block (valid tenant-owned account accepted on both the whole-rate and per-component paths; nonexistent and cross-tenant account IDs rejected on both create and update; confirmed the existing `toEqual({name: 'VAT', rate: 0.15})` exact-shape assertion still passes, proving no accidental `accountId: null` was written for unconfigured components). `paymentPosting.test.ts` gains a "Per-levy tax account posting" block: a 3-levy split posts a 5-line entry (Cash + VAT + NHIL + GETFund + Revenue) that sums back to the Cash debit; a mixed case (only some levies have an account) correctly folds the rest into Revenue; a levy whose destination account was deleted after invoice creation but before payment falls back to Revenue without blocking payment. `multiCurrency.test.ts` gains a case creating an EUR invoice against a layered tax rate with one component's account set, confirming the Liability account's and Revenue's real ledger balances both reflect the correctly FX-scaled split after payment.
**Live verification note:** the existing local dev/test Postgres was actually 5 migrations behind (`prisma migrate status` showed `20260801023734_add_tax_rate_components` and later as unapplied) - applied via `prisma migrate deploy` against the local database before any of the above tests could pass; this was a pre-existing local environment gap, unrelated to this feature. A full manual Playwright/browser check against a running dev server was not performed this pass (no dev server was already running) - relied instead on the full backend integration suite above, which already exercises every step of this flow (account creation, tax rate creation with account validation, invoice creation with breakdown snapshotting, payment posting, and real ledger balance assertions) against a real Postgres database with no mocking beyond the FX-rate HTTP call.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/repository/taxRateRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/taxRateService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/services/invoicePaymentService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/taxRates.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/paymentPosting.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/multiCurrency.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/TaxRates.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-09] - Chart of Accounts: Account Type Filter + Real Cost of Sales Account Type (Gross Profit)

**What:** while reviewing the Journal Voucher plan, the user asked for three more things on the Chart of Accounts screen: (1) an Account Type filter dropdown, (2) a new "Cost of Sales" account type so the platform can support a normal trading P&L (Revenue − Cost of Sales = Gross Profit, then − Operating Expenses = Net Profit), and (3) confirmation that the Balance column reflects the real cumulative balance of everything actually posted.

**#3 required no code change** - `useAccounts.ts`'s `fetchAccounts()` already calls `GET /ledgers/summary` (real `ledgers`-table sums) and maps it onto each account's `balance`; confirmed via reading the code that this was already correct, just contingent on the Journal Voucher Post-workflow fix (above) actually getting entries into the ledger.

**#1 and #2 - what shipped:**
- New tenant migration (v6, `006_add_cost_of_sales_account_type`) swaps the `accounts.type` CHECK constraint to allow `'COST_OF_SALES'` alongside the existing 5 values - a plain `VARCHAR` + `CHECK`, not a global Postgres enum like `journal_entries.status`, so this was a simple additive constraint change with no heavier migration needed.
- `reportRepository.ts`'s 4 report functions (`getProfitAndLoss`, `getCashFlowStatement`, `getKpiDashboard`, `getBalanceSheet`) all now recognize `COST_OF_SALES`: P&L gets a real separate `costOfSales`/`totalCostOfSales` bucket and a genuine `grossProfit` field (`netProfit` now correctly nets `grossProfit - totalExpenses` instead of the old flat `totalRevenue - totalExpenses`); the other three fold it in as debit-normal like Expense (for `retainedEarnings`/`netIncome`) with no double-counting. KPI Dashboard also gains a real `grossProfitMarginPct` - closing a gap the code itself used to flag as "would be fabricated" since no COGS existed anywhere; it now returns a real number once a tenant posts to a Cost of Sales account, `null` otherwise (same convention as every other ratio here).
- `GET /reports/profit-loss/export` (PDF/Word) folds Cost of Sales into the exported "Expenses" list so the downloaded document's Total Revenue − Total Expenses still reconciles to Net Profit, exactly like the on-screen report - a real gap that would have silently dropped Cost of Sales lines from the export otherwise.
- Frontend: `ChartOfAccounts.tsx` gets a real Account Type filter dropdown (AND-combined with the existing text search); a bidirectional `COST_OF_SALES` ↔ `"Cost of Sales"` mapping was added to `useAccounts.ts` (the previous naive `charAt(0).toUpperCase() + slice(1).toLowerCase()` transform would have mangled a multi-word type like this into `"Cost_of_sales"`/`"COST OF SALES"`); `ProfitAndLoss.tsx` gets a real Cost of Sales section and Gross Profit subtotal row (only shown once a tenant actually uses it, to avoid a redundant duplicate-of-Total-Income section for everyone else); `KpiDashboard.tsx` gets a Gross Profit Margin tile.

**Honest scope boundary:** this does not make POS sales auto-post a COGS entry - that needs real inventory costing (FIFO/weighted-average), a materially larger separate project already documented as a known gap in TASKS.md. Tenants get a real account type they (or future automation) can post to; Gross Profit/Gross Margin become real numbers only once something actually posts to it.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit`/`tsc -b` clean. Full backend suite run twice in a row (58 suites / 416 tests, both green) - `accounts.test.ts` and `reports.test.ts` extended with real coverage: creating a `COST_OF_SALES` account, and a full posting scenario asserting `grossProfit`/`totalCostOfSales` on the P&L, `retainedEarnings` correctly folding it in on the Balance Sheet with the accounting equation still balancing, and a real (non-null) `grossProfitMarginPct` on the KPI dashboard. Live-verified against the real dev stack: confirmed the filter dropdown's options and correct filtering behavior, created a real Cost of Sales account and posted a real journal voucher against it, and confirmed the P&L page showed Revenue $1,554 − Cost of Sales $300 = Gross Profit $1,254 and the KPI dashboard showed a real 80.7% Gross Profit Margin tile.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/database/migrations/tenantMigrations.ts`, `backend/src/repository/accountRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/accountService.ts`, `backend/src/repository/reportRepository.ts`, `backend/src/routes/reports.ts`, `backend/src/tests/accounts.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/reports.test.ts`, `frontend/src/types/accounting.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useAccounts.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useProfitAndLoss.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useKpiDashboard.ts`, `frontend/src/components/accounts/AccountForm.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/accounts/ChartOfAccounts.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/reports/ProfitAndLoss.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/reports/KpiDashboard.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-09] - Journal Voucher → Journal Entry Lifecycle: Real Post Action + Void-with-Reversal

**What:** the user reported "some errors" with journal entries, then described the fix: rename "Journal Entry" to "Journal Voucher"; a Voucher becomes a Journal Entry once Posted; a posted entry is not editable but is voidable; voiding a posted entry must create a real reversal that offsets it; the (reversal) entry must show up in the G/L and financial reports.

**The real bug behind "some errors":** `JournalBuilder.tsx`'s "Create Entry" flow always saved a **DRAFT** (the backend defaults to DRAFT when `status` is omitted), but the frontend had **no Post action anywhere** - `JournalList.tsx` only ever rendered a Void button, and only for Draft rows. So every manually-created journal entry silently stayed a Draft forever, never touched the `ledgers` table, and never appeared in the G/L or any financial report - despite the builder's submit button being misleadingly labeled "Post Entry". Confirmed with the user this is the bug they meant before implementing.

**Fix - backend:**
- New tenant-schema migration (v5, `005_add_journal_entry_reversal_linkage`) adds `reversal_of_entry_id`/`reversed_by_entry_id` nullable UUID columns to `journal_entries`, applied automatically per-tenant via the existing lazy `ensureTenantSchemaMigrated` mechanism.
- `journalEntryService.voidJournalEntry()` rewritten: a DRAFT void is unchanged (plain status flip, no ledger effect since it never posted). A **POSTED** void now builds a real reversing entry inside the same transaction - swaps debit/credit on every line of the original, dates it *today* (the void date, not the original's date), re-checks the fiscal period is open for today, generates a `REV-`-prefixed entry number, creates it directly as POSTED, and posts it to the ledger via the existing `ledgerRepository.postJournalEntryToLedger` - the same path every other posting uses, so it automatically shows up in every ledger view and financial report (`reportRepository.ts`/`ledgerRepository.ts` aggregate straight from `ledgers` with no status filter, confirmed via code read - no report-layer changes were needed). The original is flipped to `VOID` (reusing the existing status value - adding a 4th enum value would have needed a heavier public-schema Prisma migration) with `reversedByEntryId` set; the reversal carries `reversalOfEntryId` pointing back. Voiding a reversal itself is allowed (produces a second reversal - the correct double-entry way to "undo an undo"). `POST /:id/void` now returns `{ journalEntry, reversalEntry }`.
- `entryNumber` never changes on Post - the same string identifies the record for its whole life; status + UI copy communicate the stage, not a renumbering.

**Fix - frontend:** `/journals` (nav + page title) renamed "Journal Vouchers"; `JournalBuilder.tsx` → "Create Journal Voucher" / "Save Voucher" (fixing the actual mislabel); `JournalList.tsx` Draft rows now get a real **Post** button in addition to Void; Posted rows' Void button warns a reversal will be posted; a small caption under the entry number shows "Reversal of JE-..." / "Reversed by REV-..." when linked (resolved client-side against the already-fetched list, no extra API calls).

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit`/`tsc -b` clean. Full backend suite run twice in a row (58 suites / 414 tests, both green). `journalEntries.test.ts` extended with 4 new tests: a POSTED void creates a `REV-` entry with correctly swapped lines, correct forward/back linkage, and ledger balances that net exactly back to their pre-original values; voiding a reversal produces a second reversal; voiding into a closed fiscal period is rejected with no state change; both `JOURNAL_ENTRY.VOIDED` and `JOURNAL_ENTRY.REVERSED` audit rows are written. Live-verified against the real dev stack (fresh tenant via `admin-onboard`): confirmed the sidebar/list/builder all read "Journal Voucher(s)", created a voucher, posted it and watched the Chart of Accounts' Cash balance move by the real amount, then voided it and confirmed a real `REV-...` entry appeared with the correct linkage captions on both rows.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/database/migrations/tenantMigrations.ts`, `backend/src/repository/journalEntryRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/journalEntryService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/journalEntries.ts`, `backend/src/tests/journalEntries.test.ts`, `frontend/src/types/accounting.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useJournals.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/journals/JournalList.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/journals/JournalBuilder.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`.

**What:** TASKS.md's "Implement 'Go To' feature for enhanced navigation" - confirmed with the user this means expanding the existing Cmd+K menu (`CommandMenu.tsx`), not building real cross-app record search (a separate, materially larger feature - jumping to a specific Invoice/Journal Entry/etc. by name isn't possible today since no business record has a URL-addressable single-record view anywhere in the app, confirmed via investigation before scoping).

**The actual gap:** `CommandMenu.tsx` had only 5 hardcoded navigation shortcuts (Dashboard, Chart of Accounts, Journal Entries, General Ledger, P&L) out of ~25 real protected routes - Invoices, Bills, Inventory, POS, Tax Rates, Fiscal Periods, Recurring Transactions, Approvals, Expense Claims, Team, Audit Trail, Bulk Import, Banking, Balance Sheet, Cash Flow Statement, Cash Flow Forecast, KPI Dashboard, Budgets, Stock Intelligence, and Executive Reports were all missing.

**Fix:** rather than hand-copying a second, inevitably-drifting list into `CommandMenu.tsx`, extracted `Sidebar.tsx`'s existing `navigationGroups` (grouped, icon-labeled, already covers every real route) and its role-based `RESTRICTED_ROLE_NAV`/`getVisibleHrefs` filtering into a new shared `frontend/src/lib/navigation.ts` - `getVisibleNavGroups(role)` is now the single source of truth both `Sidebar.tsx` and `CommandMenu.tsx` render from. `CommandMenu.tsx` now renders one `Command.Group` per section, filtered by the current user's role exactly like the sidebar already is - a Cashier's Cmd+K never offers a page their own sidebar (and the backend) would block them from anyway. The "Preferences" settings shortcut is similarly hidden for the same 4 roles `/settings` itself is blocked for at the route level (`App.tsx`'s `SETTINGS_RESTRICTED_ROLES`), avoiding a dead-end navigation.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` clean, production build succeeds (PWA manifest/service-worker unaffected). Live-verified against the real dev stack: confirmed the sidebar renders identically after the extraction (no regression), opened Cmd+K and confirmed all ~25 routes now appear grouped exactly as the sidebar groups them, typed a filter ("tax") and confirmed `cmdk`'s built-in search still narrows correctly across the full list, and confirmed selecting a result actually navigates (`/settings/tax-rates`).
