## [Date: 2026-08-08] - Hybrid-Offline POS, Phase 2: Frontend Local Queue + Sync Loop

**What:** Completes item 16 on the Ghana market research roadmap ("Hybrid-offline POS architecture") - the roadmap doc flagged this as the single highest-leverage infrastructure change surfaced by the market research (a pure cloud POS freezing checkout during Ghana's routine internet/power drops is a top-ranked pain point across nearly every retail segment researched). Builds on Phase 1's backend idempotency work (same day) to add the actual local-first queue and sync loop, so a cashier can keep ringing up sales during a real outage.

**Scope, confirmed with the user up front:** sales only. Till open/close and voids still require a live connection; close-till is hard-blocked with a clear message while any sale is pending sync, deliberately sidestepping the harder problem of reconciling stragglers into an already-closed till.

**Implementation:**
- New `frontend/src/lib/offlineDb.ts` - a thin `idb` (IndexedDB) wrapper with two object stores: a per-warehouse catalog snapshot (refreshed opportunistically on every successful live fetch, so a cashier can still search/add products to a cart once connectivity drops) and the pending-sale queue itself.
- New `frontend/src/lib/saleSyncQueue.ts` (`useSaleSyncQueue` hook) - drives the sync loop: triggers on the browser's `online` event, a 15s poll, immediately after a new sale is queued, and via a manual "Sync Now" button. Deliberately **not** Workbox Background Sync - it wouldn't replace any of this loop's actual machinery (the poll, the manual trigger, the rich pending/needs-attention UI all still need the same React-level queue regardless), has zero Safari/iOS support, and would have forced switching the PWA build off its current `generateSW` strategy for no real gain given the already-accepted "must stay open for the shift" tradeoff. Only network-layer failures (no HTTP response at all) get silently retried; a genuine rejection from the server (e.g. insufficient stock discovered only at sync time - there's no offline stock reservation anywhere in this schema) stops that sale from auto-retrying, calls the new `sync-failures` audit endpoint, and surfaces it as "Needs Attention" for manual reconciliation - a completed cash sale with real money already collected is never silently dropped.
- `PointOfSale.tsx`: `handleRecordSale` now always generates a `clientTxnId` (a general retry-safety improvement, not only an offline one - it also covers a double-tap submit or a client-side timeout on an actually-successful request) and branches on whether the request actually reached the server; a genuine network failure queues the sale locally instead of losing it. The till status banner and "Today's Receipts" list both show pending/needs-attention sales distinctly (amber/red, with a live "+ GH₵X pending sync" note on the cash-sales total), and a cashier can cancel a sale that never synced (trivial - it's just removed from the local queue, no backend call needed since nothing was ever recorded server-side).

**Verification:** `tsc -b` clean, production build succeeds with the PWA manifest/service-worker still generating correctly (`/api/**` still excluded from all caching, unchanged). Live end-to-end check against the real dev stack (this repo has no Playwright test suite - verified manually, matching this session's established live-verification convention): registered a real test tenant, opened a till, recorded one normal online sale (confirmed the new `clientTxnId` doesn't change existing behavior), then intercepted `POST /tills/sales` to simulate a real network failure and recorded a second sale - confirmed it queued locally with the correct "Pending Sync" UI, the till total correctly showed both the confirmed and pending amounts, and Close Till was genuinely blocked. Removed the interception and clicked "Sync Now" - confirmed via the backend's own request log that a real second `POST /tills/sales` (201) fired and created a genuine second `CashSale` row with real stock deduction (19 → 18 units), not just an optimistic UI update.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/lib/offlineDb.ts` (new), `frontend/src/lib/saleSyncQueue.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/pos/PointOfSale.tsx`, `frontend/package.json` (added `idb`).
**Roadmap:** marks item 16 in `docs/GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md` `[DONE]`.

## [Date: 2026-08-08] - Hybrid-Offline POS, Phase 1: Backend Idempotency (`clientTxnId`)

**What:** First phase of item 16 on the Ghana market research roadmap ("Hybrid-offline POS architecture") - user chose this from the remaining unblocked candidate features, then explicitly scoped this phase to sales-only (till open/close and voids stay online-only). This phase ships the backend groundwork alone, backward-compatible, before any frontend offline-queue work begins.

**Root problem this unblocks:** to let a cashier queue sales locally while offline and replay them once connectivity returns, `POST /tills/sales` needed to become safely retry-idempotent - replaying the same queued sale twice (a real possibility: a flaky reconnect, a duplicate sync attempt) must never double-process it (double-deduct stock, double-charge the till).

**Implementation:**
- `CashSale` gained two nullable columns: `clientTxnId` (client-generated dedup key, `@@unique([tenantId, clientTxnId])`) and `clientOccurredAt` (when the sale actually happened on the device, distinct from `createdAt`'s true row-insert time). Both optional on the request - fully backward compatible.
- `POST /tills/sales`: a `findFirst` fast-path check skips redundant stock-deduction work on a simple sequential retry, but **the real safety net is the DB unique constraint itself, not the pre-check** - two concurrent requests carrying the same `clientTxnId` can both pass a `findFirst` before either commits (Read Committed isolation). Postgres blocks the losing `INSERT` until the winner resolves; catching that `P2002` (inspecting `error.meta?.target` to distinguish it from the pre-existing, unrelated `receiptNo` collision risk) throws a sentinel so the whole transaction rolls back cleanly - including the loser's own already-applied stock decrement - then a fresh transaction re-fetches the now-guaranteed-committed winner and returns it (`200`, `replayed: true`, vs. `201` for a genuine first-time create). Getting the rollback ordering right here was the one real correctness bug caught during a design-review pass before implementation - swallowing the duplicate inside the original transaction and returning 200 from there would have let Prisma commit that request's stock decrement anyway, silently double-deducting stock with no duplicate row to show for it.
- `GET /void-stats` now buckets its `from`/`to` filter by `clientOccurredAt` when present, falling back to `createdAt` otherwise - so a sale synced hours after it actually happened still attributes to the shift it occurred in, while every pre-existing sale (always `clientOccurredAt: null`) behaves identically to before.
- New `POST /tills/sales/sync-failures`: records a sync-failure via the existing `recordAuditLog` (reusing the established non-throwing single-write-path, same one `ExpenseClaim` reuses the Approval Workflow engine for) rather than a new table. Deliberately best-effort/fire-and-forget from the frontend once its sync loop gives up on a sale - creates no `CashSale` row (correctly, since none was ever completed), but gives a manager on *any* device visibility into a failed sync, not just the terminal that queued it - closing a real cross-terminal gap that pure-client-side (IndexedDB-only) tracking would leave open for this app's existing multi-branch/`WarehouseAccess` model.
- New `backend/src/tests/cashSaleIdempotency.test.ts`: concurrent same-`clientTxnId` dedup (exactly one `CashSale` row, one stock deduction, not two), sequential-retry replay, backward-compat path with no `clientTxnId`, `void-stats` `clientOccurredAt` bucketing, and the sync-failures audit-log write.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full backend suite run twice in a row against real Postgres/Redis: 412/412 passing both times (was 407 before this phase's 5 new tests).
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260808172851_add_cash_sale_offline_sync_fields/`, `backend/src/routes/cashTill.ts`, `backend/src/tests/cashSaleIdempotency.test.ts`.
**Next:** Phase 2 (frontend IndexedDB local queue + sync loop + `PointOfSale.tsx` integration) - not yet built.

## [Date: 2026-08-04] - Widen Cramped Data-Entry Modals

**What:** User sent a photo of the "Add Product / Inventory Item" modal on their own screen, cramped into a small card, and asked to widen every modal that looks the same way, then use Bulk Add Products.

**Root cause:** every modal in the app (`Modal.tsx`) defaults to `max-w-lg` (512px) regardless of content. That's fine for a single-field form (Add Vendor, Add Customer, Invite Staff) but genuinely cramped for the 6 modals that pack a multi-column grid or an embedded data table into that width: `WarehouseManagement.tsx`'s "Add Product / Inventory Item" (3-column grid), "Bulk Add Products" (8-column quick-add table), "Stock Adjustment History" (6-column table), "Stock Take" (4-column count/review tables); `VendorBills.tsx`'s "Record Vendor Bill" itemized line-row; `Invoices.tsx`'s "Create New Invoice" line-item row.

**Fix:** widened each via the `Modal` component's existing `className` override (`twMerge` already resolves the conflicting `max-w-*` utility, so no change to `Modal.tsx` itself was needed) - `max-w-2xl` for the product/vendor-bill/invoice forms, `max-w-3xl` for the two history/stock-take tables, `max-w-5xl` for Bulk Add Products (the worst offender - a full 8-column editable table). Also widened the Bulk Add Products warehouse-select column (`w-32`→`w-40`) since shop names were truncating. Left the single-field modals (Add Vendor, Add Customer, Invite Staff, Change Role, Add Warehouse, Close Till, Void Sale, etc.) untouched - widening those would just add empty space, not fix a real cramped-field problem.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean on both frontend and backend, frontend production build clean. Live-verified in a browser (Playwright against the local dev stack): registered a real test tenant via the passcode-gated admin-onboard endpoint, logged in, screenshotted the widened "Add Product" and "Bulk Add Products" modals, then actually filled in and submitted 3 realistic test products (Samsung 24" Monitor, A4 Copy Paper, HP 63 Ink Cartridge) through the real Bulk Add Products flow end-to-end - "3 products created successfully", exercising the exact `/inventory/items/bulk` endpoint fixed for production timeouts earlier today. Backend suite unaffected (no backend files touched): 407/407 passing.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/pages/inventory/WarehouseManagement.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/bills/VendorBills.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-08-02] - Fix Production 500s on `/notifications` and `/inventory/items/bulk` (Prisma Transaction Timeout)

**What:** The user reported real production errors from `ledgio-backend.onrender.com` (browser console + server logs): `GET /api/v1/notifications` intermittently 500ing, and `POST /api/v1/inventory/items/bulk` 500ing consistently.

**Root cause:** every tenant-scoped query in this app runs inside a Prisma interactive `$transaction` (`withTenantDb` in `backend/src/database/tenantClient.ts`) so `SET LOCAL search_path` stays pinned to one connection. Prisma's interactive-transaction defaults are `maxWait: 2000ms` / `timeout: 5000ms`, which assume near-zero DB latency. The production server logs showed every trivial statement (`BEGIN`, `DEALLOCATE ALL`, `SET LOCAL search_path`, `COMMIT`) individually taking 300-500ms round-trip under Render's connection pooler - a single-query request like `/notifications` (BEGIN + DEALLOCATE ALL + SET LOCAL + SELECT + COMMIT) was already logging 1.2-2.2s total, dangerously close to the 5s ceiling, so it tipped over intermittently. `/inventory/items/bulk` runs 1-4 sequential queries **per item** inside that same single transaction - even a modest batch reliably exceeded 5s, hence its consistent (not intermittent) failure. Once the transaction timeout fires, Prisma throws past the route's own per-row `try/catch`, landing in the outer catch as an unhelpful generic 500.

**Fix:** `withTenantDb` now passes explicit `{ timeout, maxWait }` options to `$transaction`, sourced from new `PRISMA_TX_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 25000) / `PRISMA_TX_MAX_WAIT_MS` (default 10000) env vars - generous enough to absorb real production latency, still bounded (consistent with the `statement_timeout=30000` already declared on `DATABASE_URL`). No behavior change for fast local/CI Postgres, where this limit is never approached - purely a production-latency fix.

**Also fixed while investigating**: this session's sandbox environment had reverted to an earlier checkpoint (same class of issue logged 2026-08-01) - local Postgres/Redis weren't running, and the generated Prisma Client + database were stale relative to `schema.prisma` (missing 4 already-pushed migrations: credit/debit notes, tax rate components, MoMo payment requests, expense claims). Restarted both services, ran `prisma migrate deploy` + `prisma generate` to resync, confirmed via `prisma migrate status` before proceeding.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full backend suite run twice in a row against real Postgres/Redis: 407/407 passing both times (existing test mocks assert on `$transaction`'s callback-only signature and are unaffected by the added second options argument). Not independently load-tested against production-grade latency in this sandbox (no way to reproduce Render's actual pooler latency locally) - the fix is grounded directly in the real production log timings the user reported, not simulated.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/database/tenantClient.ts`, `backend/.env.example`.

## [Date: 2026-08-02] - Fix Monthly Executive Reports (Never Fired) + Add Period-over-Period Deltas to Weekly/Monthly Emails

**What:** User asked to update the weekly and monthly executive email reports sent to tenant companies, and to add a decision document. Investigation (see `docs/decisions/0001-weekly-monthly-executive-email-reports.md`) found `ReportSchedule.frequency` was persisted from Settings but never actually read by the cron dispatcher: `ScheduledEmailCronService.runDueSchedulesJob()` only ever checked `dayOfWeek`/`hourUtc`, so a tenant who picked "Monthly" silently kept getting weekly sends (or, depending on `dayOfWeek`, potentially none) - monthly delivery was effectively a no-op. The Settings UI made this worse: the frequency field was a disabled input hardcoded to `"Weekly (Every Monday at 8:00 AM)"`, so there was no way to actually select Monthly even if the backend had honored it. Separately, the email template itself was three flat numbers with no trend context (up vs. down vs. last period).

**Fix:** Added a `dayOfMonth` column to `ReportSchedule` (migration `20260802063000_add_day_of_month_to_report_schedules`, default `1`). `scheduledEmailService.ts` now has a shared `computePeriodReportData(tenantId, periodStart, now)` helper used by both `computeWeeklyReportData` (trailing 7 days) and the new `computeMonthlyReportData` (trailing 30 days) - each returns the current period's totals plus `salesChangePercent`/`itemsChangePercent` vs. the immediately preceding period of equal length (`null` when there's no prior data, e.g. a brand-new tenant, instead of a misleading 0%/∞). `runDueSchedulesJob()` now branches on `schedule.frequency`: `Weekly` matches `dayOfWeek`+`hourUtc` with the existing 6-day dedup guard; `Monthly` matches the new `dayOfMonth`+`hourUtc` with a 27-day guard. `EmailService` gained a shared `buildPeriodReportHtml()` template - a 2-column stat-tile grid with color-coded ▲/▼ deltas - plus `sendMonthlyExecutiveReport()` alongside the existing (now delta-aware) `sendWeeklyExecutiveReport()`. `POST /api/v1/reports/schedule` now accepts optional `dayOfWeek`/`dayOfMonth`/`hourUtc` overrides (previously ignored), and `POST /schedule/test-email` reads the tenant's saved `frequency` to preview the correct template instead of always sending the weekly one. Settings UI: the frequency field is now a real Weekly/Monthly `<select>` wired to the existing load/save endpoints, with copy that reflects the selected cadence.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean after a fresh `npm ci`/`prisma generate`. Updated `backend/src/tests/scheduledReports.test.ts` and `backend/src/tests/smsAndNodemailerReports.test.ts` for the `weeklySales` → `periodSales` field rename (the shared field name across both cadences) and the two new required delta fields. **Not run**: this sandbox has no reachable PostgreSQL instance (`pg_isready` fails, no `DATABASE_URL` configured), so the integration suite (which spins up real tenants/closeouts against a live DB) could not be executed here - flagging this explicitly rather than claiming a pass. Recommend running `npm test` in an environment with a live test DB before merging.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260802063000_add_day_of_month_to_report_schedules/migration.sql` (new), `backend/src/services/scheduledEmailService.ts`, `backend/src/services/EmailService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/scheduledReports.ts`, `backend/src/tests/scheduledReports.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/smsAndNodemailerReports.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`, `docs/decisions/0001-weekly-monthly-executive-email-reports.md` (new).

## [Date: 2026-08-01] - 180-Day Cash Flow Forecast (Recurring-Transaction + AR/AP-Aware)

**What:** Closes item 12 from `GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`'s Candidate System Features list - a forward-looking projection distinct from the historical Cash Flow Statement (item 4). The roadmap item itself posed the design question: trend-based, or recurring-transaction-aware? Chose recurring-transaction-aware, and went further: **the forecast is deliberately not a trend-based extrapolation at all**. Projecting forward from a historical average would be exactly the kind of fabricated number this codebase's "No Mock Data Ever" rule exists to prevent - a forecast built that way would show numbers with no real backing. Instead, every dollar in the forecast traces back to one of three genuinely real, dated sources:

- **RecurringTransaction occurrences due within the window** - their real future occurrence dates, computed by advancing `nextRun` with the exact same `advanceDate()` function the recurring-transactions cron itself uses (imported directly, not reimplemented), so the forecast can never disagree with what will actually post. Each occurrence's net cash impact is computed from its `templateData.lines`, summing debits/credits that hit an `isCashEquivalent` account (reusing the same flag built for the Cash Flow Statement).
- **Outstanding (non-PAID) Invoices due within the window** - assumed collected on their real `dueDate`.
- **Outstanding (non-PAID) VendorBills due within the window** - assumed paid on their real `dueDate`.

**Deliberately excluded**: approved-but-unreimbursed Expense Claims (built earlier today). Unlike invoices/bills, a claim has no due date - assuming a payment date for it would be fabricating data, not reading it. Excluded rather than guessed, the same principle already applied to the KPI dashboard's Gross Margin/Current Ratio omissions.

**Output shape**: starting cash balance (today's real `totalCashEquivalents`, reusing the KPI dashboard's repository query), weekly-bucketed inflows/outflows/running projected balance, and a full flat list of every contributing event (date, source, description, signed amount) so every number on the page is individually auditable back to a real record - nothing is a black-box aggregate.

New `/reports/cash-flow-forecast` page: a 30/90/180-day selector, summary cards (starting cash, projected inflows/outflows, ending projected balance - flagged red if negative), a lightweight hand-rolled bar visualization of the weekly running balance (no charting library added, consistent with the rest of the app's hand-built UI components), and the full contributing-events list.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/cashFlowForecast.test.ts` (3 tests): `days` parameter validated (7-365 range); a full 30-day scenario (real $5,000 capital injection, a real $100/week recurring rent transaction, a real $1,200 invoice due day 5, a real $300 bill due day 10) verified against hand-calculated totals - exactly 5 recurring occurrences at exactly -$100 each, exactly 1 invoice event at +$1,200, exactly 1 bill event at -$300, `totalProjectedInflow`/`totalProjectedOutflow`/`endingProjectedBalance` all matching by hand ($5,000 + $1,200 - $800 = $5,400), and the sum of every week's `netChange` independently reconciling to the same ending balance (proving the weekly bucketing doesn't drop or double-count events); a shorter 7-day window test confirming the window bound is actually applied (the day-10 bill correctly excluded, not just always returning everything). Full backend suite run twice in a row: 407/407 passing both times. **Live verification** against real dev servers via Playwright: seeded a real tenant ($8,000 capital, a $500/month recurring rent starting in 2 days, a $3,000 invoice), loaded the real `/reports/cash-flow-forecast` page at 180 days, and confirmed the on-screen numbers exactly matched hand-calculated expectations (Starting Cash $8,000.00, Projected Inflows $3,000.00, Projected Outflows $3,000.00 = 6 monthly rent occurrences over 180 days, Projected Balance in 180 Days $8,000.00) and that the weekly bar chart's step changes (constant $7,500 for several weeks, then a jump to $10,000.00 the week both the invoice landed and a second rent charge posted) were internally consistent with those totals.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/cashFlowForecastService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/reports.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/reports/CashFlowForecast.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/cashFlowForecast.test.ts` (new), `docs/GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-08-01] - Expense Claims (Submit / Approve / Reimburse)

**What:** Closes item 10 from `GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`'s Candidate System Features list. Employees previously had no way to record out-of-pocket spend (travel, supplies, meals) for reimbursement - `VendorBill` represents money owed to an external vendor, not a reimbursement owed to staff, so it was never the right fit. New `ExpenseClaim` covers: file → approve/reject → reimburse.

**Design:**
- **Reused the existing generic `ApprovalWorkflow` engine as-is** (`entityType: 'ExpenseClaim'`) rather than building a bespoke approval chain, exactly as speculated when this item was originally logged on the roadmap. `expenseClaimService.submitExpenseClaim()` creates the claim then immediately calls `approvalWorkflowService.createApprovalWorkflow()`; `decideExpenseClaim()` is a thin wrapper around `approvalWorkflowService.decideApprovalStep()` that mirrors the resulting workflow status back onto the claim so the Expense Claims page shows live status without needing a second visit to the generic Approvals inbox.
- **Filing is deliberately the loosest role gate** (`requireRole('Viewer')`, matching `GET /approval-workflows`'s existing convention for "any tenant member") - any employee can file a claim for their own spend. Deciding and reimbursing require `Accountant` or higher, matching every other payment-adjacent gate in this codebase (invoice/bill `/pay`, MoMo reimbursement confirmation).
- **The real journal entry (Debit Expense, Credit Cash) posts only on reimbursement**, after approval - not at filing time. This mirrors the same "recognize the accounting event only when money actually moves" principle already established for invoices/bills/credit notes (revenue/expense recognized at payment, not creation) - a filed-but-unapproved claim has zero ledger impact.
- `ExpenseClaim.expenseAccountId` is optional (an employee can specify which Expense account their spend should hit, e.g. "Travel & Entertainment" vs "Office Supplies"); omitted, it falls back to the same "find by conventional code (`5010`), else first account of that type" default pattern already used by invoices.ts/bills.ts for their cash/revenue account lookups. Validated as a real `EXPENSE`-type account at filing time, not just at reimbursement time, so a bad account reference is caught immediately.
- Frontend: new `/expenses` page (added to the sidebar's "Sales & Purchases" section, and to the Shop Manager/Cashier/HR/Auditor restricted-nav allowlists since filing an expense is a broadly useful action, not an admin-only one) - a "My Claims"/"All Claims" toggle (Accountant+ only), inline Approve/Reject/Reimburse buttons gated client-side by role (matching what the backend would actually allow, not just hiding a button that would 403 anyway for a legitimate reason - it's an honest reflection of the real permission, not security-by-obscurity).

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/expenseClaims.test.ts` (9 tests): validation (non-positive amount, non-EXPENSE account rejected), a Viewer can file but not decide/reimburse (403), full approve→reimburse flow verified against real ledger balance changes (not just journal existence - Cash down/Expense up by the exact claimed amount), reject leaves a claim un-reimbursable, double-decide rejected, reimbursing a non-APPROVED claim rejected, `mine=true` filtering. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 404/404 passing both times (up from 395). **Live verification** against real dev servers via Playwright: filed a real $85.50 Travel claim on the running `/expenses` page, approved it, reimbursed it, and confirmed the UI updates through PENDING_APPROVAL → APPROVED → REIMBURSED with toasts at each step; confirmed via a direct `GET /ledgers/summary` call that Cash & Bank dropped by exactly $85.50 and Travel & Entertainment rose by exactly $85.50 - an exact match to the claimed amount.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, new migration `20260801032422_add_expense_claims`, `backend/src/repository/expenseClaimRepository.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/expenseClaimService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/expenseClaims.ts` (new), `backend/src/app.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/expenses/ExpenseClaims.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/expenseClaims.test.ts` (new), `docs/GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-08-01] - Mobile Money (MTN MoMo Collections) Invoice Payment Collection

**What:** Closes item 3 from `GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`'s Candidate System Features list, following the 2026-08-01 research pass that confirmed MTN MoMo has a real, self-serve-sandbox developer API (`momodeveloper.mtn.com`). Verified the exact Collections API contract via multiple independent sources (MTN's own portal plus corroborating SDK/Postman references, since the developer portal's prose pages didn't expose full endpoint detail directly): token auth (`POST /collection/token/`, Basic Auth against a pre-provisioned API user/key), `POST /collection/v1_0/requesttopay` (sends a real USSD approval prompt to the customer's phone), `GET /collection/v1_0/requesttopay/{referenceId}` (poll the result), and `GET /collection/v1_0/account/balance`.

**A real, honestly-documented scope difference from the Mono integration**: MTN's Collections API has **no bulk transaction-history endpoint** - there is no "list my statement" call the way Mono has for bank feeds. Building a Mono-style "sync all transactions, reconcile against ledger" feature for MoMo would have meant fabricating an API capability that doesn't exist. Instead, this integration tracks each individual collection attempt (`MomoPaymentRequest`: phone number, amount, MTN's `X-Reference-Id`, status, MTN's own `financialTransactionId` once successful) and learns the result by polling per-reference, matching what the real API actually supports.

**Design:**
- New `MomoPaymentRequest` model (public-schema, tenantId-filtered, FK to Invoice) - one row per "Request to Pay" attempt against an invoice.
- `momoService.ts` mirrors `monoService.ts`'s established shape: `isMomoConfigured()` (true only with real `MOMO_SUBSCRIPTION_KEY`/`MOMO_API_USER`/`MOMO_API_KEY`), in-memory access-token caching (token calls are the one part of this API that would otherwise happen on every single request), `requestToPay`, `getTransactionStatus`, `getAccountBalance`. No fallback to fake data - unconfigured returns 503 everywhere, same discipline as the bank feed integration.
- Extracted the invoice-payment/journal-posting logic that used to live only inline in `invoices.ts`'s `/pay` handler into a new shared `invoicePaymentService.markInvoicePaid()`, so both the manual "Record Payment" button and a confirmed MoMo collection post through the exact same accounting logic (Cash/Revenue journal entry, audit log, approval-workflow gate) instead of duplicating it. Behavior-preserving refactor - verified via the full existing invoice/bill/credit-note test suite passing unchanged.
- New `momo.ts` route (`/api/v1/momo`): `POST /invoices/:id/request` (send the real prompt), `GET /invoices/:id/requests` (history), `POST /requests/:referenceId/check-status` (poll MTN, and on `SUCCESSFUL` call `markInvoicePaid`), `GET /balance` (merchant wallet balance). No webhook route was built - MTN's async callback delivery has no independently-verifiable signing/authenticity mechanism documented, so building an unauthenticated webhook endpoint would have meant guessing at a security model rather than confirming one; the manual "Check Status" poll is the honestly-scoped, fully-verified path (and is a reasonable UX anyway - "did the customer approve yet? check now").
- Frontend: `/invoices` gained a "Collect via MoMo" button (visible on any non-draft, unpaid invoice, alongside the existing Record Payment/Credit Note actions) opening a modal to send a request, see prior attempts with their status, and check a pending one's status.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/momo.test.ts` (7 tests, mocking MTN's API itself per CLAUDE.md's "mocking external services is allowed for unit testing"): 503 with zero fake data when unconfigured; missing phone number rejected; a real requesttopay → SUCCESSFUL status-check flow verified end-to-end against actual ledger balance changes (cash/revenue moved by the real invoice amount, invoice transitions to PAID) - not just journal existence; a FAILED status recorded with the real failure reason, invoice left untouched; requesting payment against an already-paid invoice rejected; real balance endpoint. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 395/395 passing both times (up from 388). **Live verification** against real dev servers via Playwright: seeded a real tenant and a real GHS 250 invoice, opened the "Collect via MoMo" modal on the actual running Invoices page (confirmed the modal correctly shows the invoice's real total), submitted a real request, and confirmed the live UI surfaces the honest "Mobile Money integration is not configured for this environment" error (no fake success) - this environment has no real MTN sandbox credentials, so the true SUCCESSFUL happy path is proven correct by the mocked automated tests, not live-tested end-to-end against MTN's actual servers, same caveat already established for the Mono bank-feed integration.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, new migration `20260801030330_add_momo_payment_requests`, `backend/src/services/momoService.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/invoicePaymentService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/routes/momo.ts` (new), `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/.env.example`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/momo.test.ts` (new), `docs/GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-08-01] - Layered Ghana Tax Levy Breakdown (VAT + NHIL + GETFund)

**What:** Closes item 2 from `GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`'s Candidate System Features list, following the 2026-08-01 primary-source research (verified directly on `gra.gov.gh`) that resolved Ghana's real VAT structure: a flat **20%** total invoice charge, composed of 15% VAT + 2.5% NHIL + 2.5% GETFund Levy, both now re-coupled into the VAT base under VAT Act 2025 (Act 1151). Previously, `TaxRate` only ever stored a single opaque percentage - a Ghanaian invoice could show "20% tax" but never the legally-expected per-levy breakdown competitors already ship.

**Design:**
- `TaxRate.components: Json?` - an optional array of `{name, rate}` objects. The parent `rate` field remains the single source of truth for actual tax calculation (unchanged behavior for every existing simple rate); `components` is purely a labeled, validated breakdown that must sum to the parent `rate` (checked server-side on both create and update, tolerance 0.05%). A tax rate with no `components` behaves exactly as before - fully backward compatible.
- Changing a layered rate's `rate` without also supplying new `components` is rejected (400) rather than silently leaving a breakdown that no longer adds up; clearing `components` to `null` first (or supplying a consistent new breakdown in the same request) is required.
- `Invoice.taxBreakdown: Json?` - a **snapshot** of the resolved `{name, rate, amount}[]` computed from the tax rate's components against the invoice's subtotal at creation time, stored immutably - same reasoning as `subtotal`/`tax`/`total` already being snapshotted rather than derived live, so a later edit to a `TaxRate`'s components never rewrites the history of what was actually charged on a past invoice.
- Frontend: `/settings/tax-rates` gained a "Use Ghana VAT Preset (20%)" button that pre-fills the real 15%/2.5%/2.5% split, plus a manual "layered" toggle for building any other multi-levy rate from scratch (add/remove levy rows, live running-total check). The rate list shows a muted breakdown subtitle (e.g. "VAT 15.00% + NHIL 2.50% + GETFund Levy 2.50%") under any layered rate's name. `/invoices` gained a new Tax column showing the total tax plus the same per-levy breakdown subtitle when `taxBreakdown` exists on that invoice.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New tests in `backend/src/tests/taxRates.test.ts` (6 tests, 12 total in the file): a layered rate's components sum-check (accept/reject), rejecting a component with a missing name or invalid rate, a real per-levy breakdown computed end-to-end on a $1,000 invoice (asserted `tax=200`, `total=1200`, breakdown VAT=150/NHIL=25/GETFund=25 - hand-calculated match), rejecting a rate change on a layered rate without updated components, and clearing a breakdown via `components: null`. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 388/388 passing both times. **Live verification** against real dev servers via Playwright + direct API calls: applied the Ghana preset on the real running Tax Rates page and confirmed the resulting row's breakdown subtitle; created a real $1,000 invoice against that rate via the API and confirmed the exact JSON response (`tax: "200"`, `taxBreakdown` with VAT/NHIL/GETFund at 150/25/25, `total: "1200"`); confirmed the Invoices list page renders the new Tax column with the correct breakdown subtitle alongside the existing Total/Status/Credit Note controls.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, new migration `20260801023734_add_tax_rate_components`, `backend/src/repository/taxRateRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/taxRateService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/TaxRates.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/taxRates.test.ts`, `docs/GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-31] - KPI & Financial Ratio Dashboard

**What:** Closes item 11 from `GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`'s Candidate System Features list. A new `/reports/kpis` page giving a business owner a quick-glance read on profitability and financial health, computed entirely from data already posted to the ledger - no new data entry required.

**Scope decision (deliberate, documented on the page itself, not just here):** two Explore passes confirmed this schema genuinely cannot compute a few textbook ratios honestly today:
- **No Gross Margin / Inventory Turnover** - POS sales (`cashTill.ts`) never post a Cost of Goods Sold journal entry at all; there is no COGS anywhere in the ledger to divide by.
- **No Current Ratio / Quick Ratio** - accounts have no current-vs-non-current classification (the same gap the Cash Flow Statement's missing Investing section already documented two days ago).

Rather than fabricate an approximation using the wrong denominator, the dashboard ships only ratios this data genuinely supports:
- **Net Profit Margin %** = Net Income / Revenue (period-scoped)
- **Return on Assets %** = Net Income / Total Assets
- **Equity Ratio %** = Total Equity / Total Assets
- **Debt-to-Equity Ratio** = Total Liabilities / Total Equity (flags negative equity explicitly rather than showing a confusing negative number silently)
- **Cash Ratio** = Cash & Cash Equivalents / Total Liabilities - reuses the `isCashEquivalent` flag built for the Cash Flow Statement

New `GET /api/v1/reports/kpis` (optional `startDate`/`endDate` - the period governs Net Income/margin, while Balance Sheet totals are always as of `endDate`, mirroring how the Balance Sheet and P&L reports already split "point in time" from "over a period"). No PDF/Word export was added for this one (unlike Balance Sheet/P&L/Cash Flow) - a deliberate scope call, since a ratio dashboard is read in-app or exported to CSV for further analysis, not typically submitted as a formal document the way a statement is; Print + CSV cover the real use case.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/kpiDashboard.test.ts` (4 tests): all 5 ratios verified by hand against a realistic 4-entry dataset (capital injection, cash sale, credit expense, partial payable payment); a second test confirms the period-vs-point-in-time date handling is correct (net income scoped to a date range while Balance Sheet totals stay pinned to `endDate`) - the trickiest part of this feature, verified with its own hand-checked numbers. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 382/382 passing both times. **Live verification** against real dev servers via Playwright: seeded a real tenant (owner capital $10,000, $4,000 cash sale, $1,500 credit expense) and confirmed all 5 cards on the real running dashboard (62.5% margin, 17.9% ROA, 89.3% equity ratio, 0.12 D/E, 9.33 cash ratio) match hand-calculated values exactly.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/repository/reportRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/reportingService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/reports.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useKpiDashboard.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/reports/KpiDashboard.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/kpiDashboard.test.ts` (new).

## [Date: 2026-07-31] - Credit Notes (AR) and Debit Notes (AP)

**What:** Closes item 6 from `GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`'s Candidate System Features list - a confirmed real gap (general invoice/bill correction and refund flow, not just a compliance nicety). Investigation found this platform recognizes revenue/expense only at payment time (`invoices.ts`'s and `bills.ts`'s `/pay` handlers post directly to Cash+Revenue or Cash+Expense - there's no separate Accounts Receivable/Payable ledger posting at creation time), so the textbook "always debit AR, credit Revenue" treatment doesn't actually apply here. The correct treatment genuinely depends on whether the invoice/bill has already been paid:

- **Unpaid invoice/bill**: nothing has been posted to the ledger yet, so the note simply reduces what will be charged on payment - no journal entry (`method: 'INVOICE_REDUCTION'` / `'BILL_REDUCTION'`).
- **Paid invoice/bill**: revenue/expense and cash were already recognized at the original total, so the note posts a real reversing journal entry (Credit Note: Debit Revenue, Credit Cash - a refund out; Debit Note: Debit Cash, Credit Expense - a refund in) and leaves the invoice/bill's own total/status untouched as the historical record of what was actually paid (`method: 'JOURNAL_REVERSAL'`).

New `CreditNote`/`DebitNote` models (public-schema, tenantId-filtered, unique note numbers `CN-`/`DN-`) record which path was taken explicitly - not left to be inferred later from whether `journalId` happens to be null. A cap check prevents over-crediting/over-debiting past the original amount, correctly accounting for the fact that `INVOICE_REDUCTION`/`BILL_REDUCTION` notes are already baked into the invoice/bill's live total while `JOURNAL_REVERSAL` notes are not.

New `backend/src/services/creditDebitNoteService.ts` (shared logic for both sides, mirroring the symmetry between `invoices.ts` and `bills.ts`), wired as `POST/GET /invoices/:id/credit-notes` and `POST/GET /bills/:id/debit-notes`. Frontend: a "Credit Note"/"Debit Note" button per row on `Invoices.tsx`/`VendorBills.tsx` opening a modal (amount, reason, prior-notes history) with context-aware copy explaining which treatment will apply.

**Scope note (documented, not silently omitted):** this is a financial correction only - it does not adjust inventory stock, even though a debit note commonly represents goods physically returned to a vendor. Tying stock reversal to a financial correction is a distinct concern from what this pass covers.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/creditDebitNotes.test.ts` (11 tests): unpaid-path reduction verified against the invoice/bill list; paid-path reversal verified against real ledger balance changes (`GET /ledgers/summary`) before/after, not just journal existence; over-crediting/over-debiting rejected; missing reason/non-positive amount rejected; cross-tenant 404; history listing. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 378/378 passing both times. **Live verification** against real dev servers via Playwright: issued a credit note against an unpaid $1,000 invoice (reduced to $700, Total Outstanding AR dropped accordingly) and against an already-paid $800 invoice (total stayed $800, real reversal posted); issued a debit note against an already-paid $300 vendor bill (amount stayed $300, real reversal posted) - all confirmed via the actual running UI, not just tests.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, new migration `20260731142337_add_credit_debit_notes`, `backend/src/services/creditDebitNoteService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/routes/bills.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/bills/VendorBills.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/creditDebitNotes.test.ts` (new).

## [Date: 2026-07-31] - Cash Flow Statement (Indirect Method)

**What:** Closes item 4 from `GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md`'s Candidate System Features list. The platform had Trial Balance, Profit & Loss, and Balance Sheet reports but no Cash Flow Statement - businesses could see profit and net worth but not where their cash actually came from or went.

**Design decision:** the schema has no way to distinguish "cash and bank" accounts from other assets, and no classification of accounts into Operating/Investing/Financing at all. Rather than guessing at a fragile name-only heuristic every time the report runs, `Account` gained a real, persisted `isCashEquivalent` boolean flag (tenant-schema migration `004_add_cash_equivalent_flag`, backfilled on existing accounts by the same Cash/Bank/Till naming convention already implicit elsewhere in this codebase, e.g. `invoices.ts`/`bills.ts`'s default `'1010'` payment account). New accounts default this automatically from their name (`ASSET` type + name containing "cash"/"bank"/"till") but the Chart of Accounts form lets a business override it explicitly with a labeled checkbox.

Given no further classification exists to separate fixed-asset/loan accounts from ordinary working capital, this ships as a real two-section **indirect-method** statement (Operating Activities, Financing Activities) rather than a fabricated three-section one: non-cash Asset and Liability balance changes feed Operating (standard working-capital adjustments to Net Income), Equity account changes (owner contributions/drawings) feed Financing. There is no Investing section - documented as a known simplification appropriate for the small-business bookkeeping this schema actually supports today, not silently glossed over.

**Correctness check:** because double-entry bookkeeping guarantees Assets = Liabilities + Equity at every point in time, `netCashFromOperating + netCashFromFinancing` is mathematically guaranteed to equal the actual change in the cash-equivalent accounts' ledger balance. The report exposes this as a `cashTies` boolean (mirroring the Balance Sheet's `isBalanced`) - a genuine reconciliation check, not a cosmetic one.

New `GET /api/v1/reports/cash-flow` (optional `startDate`/`endDate` - omitted means "since inception", matching the existing P&L default-to-all-time convention) and `GET /api/v1/reports/cash-flow/export?format=pdf|docx`, reusing the exact PDF/DOCX generation infrastructure and frontend export pattern built for Balance Sheet/P&L. New `frontend/src/pages/reports/CashFlowStatement.tsx` at `/reports/cash-flow`, added to the sidebar's Reports & Analytics section and the Auditor role's reduced nav set.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/cashFlow.test.ts` (9 tests): full-statement math verified by hand against a realistic 4-entry dataset (capital injection, cash sale, credit expense, partial payable payment) including the date-range-scoped variant; PDF/DOCX export produce real files with the seeded account names; unrecognized export format rejected; new accounts auto-flag/don't-auto-flag `isCashEquivalent` correctly by name, and an explicit override is respected. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 367/367 passing both times. **Live verification** against real dev servers via Playwright: seeded a real tenant (owner capital $10,000, a $4,000 cash sale, a $1,500 credit expense), loaded the real Cash Flow Statement page, and confirmed the on-screen numbers (Net Income $2,500, Operating $4,000, Financing $10,000, Net Change $14,000, ending cash $14,000, "Reconciles with actual cash account balances") - then downloaded the PDF export via the real endpoint and confirmed it's a genuine multi-section PDF.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/database/migrations/tenantMigrations.ts`, `backend/src/repository/accountRepository.ts`, `backend/src/repository/reportRepository.ts`, `backend/src/services/reportingService.ts`, `backend/src/services/accountService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/reports.ts`, `backend/src/services/pdfGenerationService.ts`, `backend/src/services/reportDocxService.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useCashFlow.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/reports/CashFlowStatement.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/components/accounts/AccountForm.tsx`, `frontend/src/hooks/useAccounts.ts`, `frontend/src/types/accounting.ts`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/cashFlow.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/accountingSchema.test.ts` (stale mock fix).

## [Date: 2026-07-31] - Itemized Vendor Bills (Real Goods Receipt) + Landed Cost Allocation

**What:** Follow-up to the `GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md` deep-research entry, which flagged accurate per-unit landed cost (freight/customs/duty spread across a shipment) as a top pain point for importers/wholesalers. Investigation found this had no foundation to build on: `VendorBill` was a pure lump-sum record with no line items at all, meaning **buying goods didn't update inventory** - stock only ever changed at item creation or via a manual restock adjustment, with no link between a purchase and what was actually received. This was flagged to the user directly (via `AskUserQuestion`) before starting, since it meant the real scope was two connected pieces, not the smaller "landed cost" feature alone.

**Fix 1 - Itemized purchase bills actually receive stock:** `VendorBill` gained an optional `warehouseId`, `billType` (`STANDARD`/`LANDED_COST`), and a new `VendorBillLine` (itemId/quantity/unitCost/lineTotal). `POST /bills` now accepts an optional `items: [{itemId, quantity, unitCost}]` + `warehouseId` - when present, the bill's `amount` is derived from the lines (not client-supplied), and a new `vendorBillReceivingService.ts` immediately increments `WarehouseStock` and recomputes each item's `costPrice` as a **moving (weighted) average** - `newCost = (existingQty×existingCost + receivedQty×receivedUnitCost) / (existingQty+receivedQty)`. Receiving happens at bill creation, not payment - goods arrive independent of when the invoice gets paid, matching how accounts payable actually works. The original lump-sum shape (no `items`) is fully preserved for backward compatibility - existing bills/tests are unaffected.

**Fix 2 - Landed cost allocation:** new `POST /bills/:id/landed-cost` records a secondary bill (freight/customs/duty, its own real vendor and AP payable) tied to a primary itemized bill via `landedCostForBillId`, then spreads its amount proportionally across the primary bill's lines (by each line's share of the purchase subtotal) and blends the resulting per-unit cost increase into each item's *current* moving-average cost. Explicitly documented as a simplification, not true lot/batch costing (this codebase has no per-shipment cost tracking infrastructure - `costPrice` is one blended figure per item): the blend uses whichever quantity from that shipment is still on hand, and skips (with a clear reason, not a silent no-op) a line whose entire shipment has already sold out. Guards: can't allocate against a bill with no line items, can't nest a landed cost against another landed-cost bill, can't apply to another tenant's bill.

Frontend (`VendorBills.tsx`): "Record Vendor Bill" modal gained a Simple/Itemized toggle - itemized mode adds a warehouse selector and a repeatable item/qty/unit-cost line builder with a live running total. Bills list shows a badge (item count, or "Landed Cost" for a secondary bill) and a "+ Landed Cost" action on any itemized bill, opening a modal that shows the resulting before→after cost per item once allocated.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/vendorBillReceiving.test.ts` (8 tests): backward-compatible lump-sum bill still works; itemized bill without a warehouse rejected; receiving computes weighted-average cost correctly for a first receipt and for a second receipt blended into existing stock; landed cost rejected against a lineless bill; landed cost allocation verified with exact expected numbers (two items with different value shares, cross-checked by hand); a sold-out line is skipped with a reason rather than silently changing nothing; nesting a landed cost against another landed-cost bill is rejected. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 358/358 passing both times. **Live verification** against real dev servers via Playwright: created a real vendor, recorded a 100-unit itemized purchase at $10/unit (confirmed "Purchase recorded and stock received" toast, $1,000 bill, "1 item" badge), then allocated a $200 landed cost against it and confirmed the UI showed the item's cost move from $10.00 → $12.00/unit - matching the hand-computed expected value exactly.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/src/services/vendorBillReceivingService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/bills.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/bills/VendorBills.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/vendorBillReceiving.test.ts` (new), new migration `20260731124114_add_vendor_bill_lines_and_landed_cost`.

## [Date: 2026-07-31] - Void Cash Sale with Manager Step-Up Authorization (POS Fraud Guard)

**What:** Follow-up to the `GHANA_MARKET_RESEARCH_AND_ROADMAP.md` deep-research entry, which flagged a specific, repeatedly-cited retail fraud pattern: a cashier completes a sale, collects cash, then voids the transaction and uses a "No Sale" to open the drawer - books balance perfectly while inventory quietly shrinks. Investigation found this codebase had **no void/cancel-sale capability at all** - `CashSale` rows were fully immutable once created, with no status field and no way to correct a mistaken sale short of manually adjusting stock and eating an unexplained till discrepancy. `POST /tills/sales` also wasn't role-restricted at all (any tenant member with warehouse access could ring up a sale). So this shipped as a real, new feature with the safeguard designed in from day one, not a patch to something already exposed.

**Design (confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion`):** a cashier can initiate a void, but it only takes effect once a Shop Manager/Admin/Accountant confirms it - either by the acting user already holding one of those roles (self-authorize) or, for a Cashier, by supplying a manager's own email + password as an inline step-up confirmation (the closest web-app equivalent to a physical "manager key" override at a till).

**Fix:** `CashSale` gained a `status` (`COMPLETED`/`VOIDED`) plus `createdByUserId`/`createdByName` (captured at sale time - didn't exist before, needed to attribute void ratios per cashier) and `voidedAt`/`voidedByUserId`/`voidedByName`/`voidReason`. New `POST /api/v1/tills/sales/:id/void` (`cashTill.ts`): validates a reason is given, blocks voiding an already-voided sale, blocks voiding once the sale's till has already closed (that day is reconciled - use a stock adjustment instead), self-authorizes Admin/Shop Manager/Accountant directly, otherwise requires `managerEmail`/`managerPassword` matching an active manager-tier account in the tenant (verified via `verifyPassword`). On success: restores every line's stock, reverses the till's running cash total, and writes a `CASH_SALE.VOIDED` audit log naming both the initiator and the authorizing manager. New `GET /api/v1/tills/void-stats` (Admin/Shop Manager/Accountant only) - per-cashier total/voided/ratio, flagging an `anomaly` when a cashier's void ratio crosses 15% over at least 5 sales (visibility for a manager to review, not a push alert per void).

Frontend (`PointOfSale.tsx`): a "Void this sale" action per receipt in "Today's Receipts" (hidden once voided, replaced with a "Voided by ... — reason" line), a Void Sale modal that only shows the manager-credentials fields when the logged-in user isn't already a self-authorizer role, and a "Void Activity by Cashier" panel (visible to managers/admins/accountants only) surfacing the same stats with anomalies highlighted in red.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/cashSaleVoid.test.ts` (8 tests): Admin self-authorized void with real stock/till-total reversal verified by direct before/after comparison; missing-reason rejected; double-void rejected; Cashier blocked without a manager step-up; wrong manager password rejected; correct Shop Manager credentials approve the void and record the manager (not the cashier) as authorizer; voiding blocked once the till is closed; void-stats endpoint 403s a Cashier and returns correct per-user ratios/anomaly flags for a manager. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 350/350 passing both times. **Live verification** against real dev servers via Playwright: opened a till, rang up a real sale, voided it as Admin through the actual UI - confirmed a success toast, the receipt showing struck-through with "Voided by ... — reason", stock genuinely restored (49→50 pcs), and Cash Sales So Far reset to GH₵0.00.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/src/routes/cashTill.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/pos/PointOfSale.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/cashSaleVoid.test.ts` (new), new migration `20260731121052_add_cash_sale_void_and_creator`.
## [Date: 2026-07-31] - Remove Team Member Feature + Passcode-Gated "Onboard Contracted Client" Admin Tool

**What:** Follow-up to the previous entry's "Change Role" feature. The user wanted a real way to take a team member off a workspace entirely (freeing their email for reuse), and separately, a way for the platform operator to onboard a business directly - skipping the normal email/SMS verification round-trip - for cases where the business has already been vetted through a signed contract, not a self-serve signup. (This request was originally phrased using the word "backdoor" across several turns; that literal ask was explicitly declined and clarified via `AskUserQuestion` into these two ordinary, auditable, Admin/passcode-gated features instead - no hidden bypass was built.)

**Fix 1 - Remove Team Member:** New `DELETE /api/v1/tenants/members/:id` (`requireRole('Admin')`) in `tenants.ts`. Blocks removing your own account (400, with a clear "have another Admin do it" message) and 404s for a member outside the caller's tenant. Guards against leaving a tenant with zero active Admins: if the target is an Admin, counts other `isActive: true` Admins and blocks the removal (400) if none remain - this guard is only reachable in a narrow edge case (self-removal is already unconditionally blocked earlier), specifically when the caller's own account was deactivated after their JWT was issued but the token is still valid. On success, hard-deletes the `User` row (freeing the email globally, since `User.email` is a platform-wide unique constraint) and writes a `TEAM_MEMBER.REMOVED` audit log entry with the removed member's name/email/role in `details`. Frontend: a "Remove" button (red, `UserMinus` icon) next to "Change Role" on every team member row in `TeamManagement.tsx`, gated behind a `window.confirm()` and wired to a real toast.

**Fix 2 - Onboard Contracted Client:** `onboardTenant()` (`tenantService.ts`) gained a server-side-only `options.skipVerification` parameter - deliberately a function parameter, never read from the request body (`dto`), so it can only be set by trusted internal server code, never smuggled in via a public signup request however it's shaped. When true: the created Admin user gets `isActive`/`isEmailVerified`/`isPhoneVerified` all `true` and no verification tokens, and receives the real welcome-package email immediately (skipping the verification email/SMS entirely). New `POST /api/v1/tenants/admin-onboard`, gated by the existing master-passcode pattern (`BroadcastService.verifyPasscode`, same mechanism as `GET /tenants`) rather than tenant JWT auth - calls `onboardTenant(..., {skipVerification: true})` and writes a `TENANT.ADMIN_ONBOARDED` audit log entry. Frontend: a 5th "Onboard Client" tab in the Admin Core Engine console (`AdminCoreEngine.tsx`), a form for company/admin details, using the already-unlocked session's passcode.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/teamMemberRemoval.test.ts` (5 tests) and `backend/src/tests/adminOnboard.test.ts` (4 tests, including a dedicated test confirming the public self-serve `/tenants/onboard` endpoint ignores a client-supplied `skipVerification: true` in the request body). Full backend suite run twice in a row: 342/342 passing both times. **Live verification** against real dev servers: onboarded a real tenant, created a second team member directly in the DB, logged into the real UI, clicked "Remove" on that member - confirmed via direct DB query the user row was actually deleted and a `TEAM_MEMBER.REMOVED` audit row was written with the correct actor (`userEmail`/`ipAddress` populated). Separately unlocked the real Admin Core Engine console with the local dev master passcode, filled out and submitted the "Onboard Client" tab - confirmed the success panel rendered, the new admin's DB row was `isActive`/`isEmailVerified`/`isPhoneVerified: true` immediately, a real login against `/auth/login` succeeded with no verification step, and a `TENANT.ADMIN_ONBOARDED` audit row was written. All test tenants/users cleaned up afterward.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `backend/src/services/tenantService.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/team/TeamManagement.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/teamMemberRemoval.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/adminOnboard.test.ts` (new).

## [Date: 2026-07-30] - Add Ability to Change an Existing Team Member's Role

**What:** The user asked to promote an existing team member (already a user in their tenant) to Admin. Investigation found no way to do this anywhere in the app - `POST /tenants/invite` only sets a role once, at invite time, and the only post-invite member-management endpoint that existed was `PUT /members/:id/warehouse-access`. There was no role-change endpoint at all, in the UI or the API - not a hidden/bypassable feature, a genuinely missing one. (Explicitly **not** built as any kind of backdoor or bypass - the request was clarified via `AskUserQuestion` first, and the fix is a normal Admin-gated feature following this session's established RBAC/audit patterns exactly.)

**Fix:** New `PUT /api/v1/tenants/members/:id/role` (`requireRole('Admin')`) in `tenants.ts`, mirroring the existing warehouse-access endpoint's shape: validates the new role against the same `CLOSED_ROLES` list the invite flow uses, 404s if the member isn't in the caller's tenant, no-ops (200) if the role is unchanged, and writes a `TEAM_MEMBER.ROLE_CHANGED` audit log entry with a structured `{role: {from, to}}` diff via the existing `recordAuditLog`/`actorFromRequest` helpers. Added one safety guard with no existing precedent to copy: **blocks demoting a tenant's only remaining active Admin**, to prevent a business accidentally locking itself out of its own Settings/Team Management - counts other `isActive: true` Admins only, since an unverified admin account can't actually log in yet to use that access.

Note surfaced to the user directly (and in the UI's modal copy): role is baked into a user's JWT at login, so a role change takes effect the next time that person logs in, not on their currently active session.

Frontend: new "Change Role" button + modal on every team member row in `TeamManagement.tsx` (Admin-only, same visibility gate as the existing "Manage Access" button), a role `<select>` reusing the same `CLOSED_ROLES` list as the invite modal, wired to the new endpoint with a real toast (success/error) instead of a silent no-feedback save.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/teamMemberRole.test.ts` (5 tests): non-Admin blocked (403), unrecognized role rejected (400), unknown member 404s, a real role change persists to the DB and writes the correct audit log row, and the last-active-Admin lockout guard is exercised both ways (a demotion that leaves another active Admin succeeds; one that wouldn't is blocked with a clear error). Full backend suite run twice in a row: 333/333 passing both times.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/team/TeamManagement.tsx`, `backend/src/tests/teamMemberRole.test.ts` (new).

## [Date: 2026-07-30] - Fix PDF Table Cells Wrapping/Overflowing Instead of Truncating

**What:** After PR #35 (real PDF/Word export) merged and Render redeployed, the user downloaded a real Executive Report PDF from production and found the "Code" column wrapped long warehouse codes onto a second line, overflowing the fixed row height and breaking the table's ruled borders. Root cause: `drawSimpleTable()` (`pdfGenerationService.ts`, shared by all 6 generated PDFs - Quick Start Guide, Stock Take Sheet, Balance Sheet, P&L, Executive Report, Stock Intelligence) passed pdfkit's `{ ellipsis: true }` text option without `lineBreak: false`, and pdfkit's automatic single-line ellipsis truncation turned out not to activate in that combination in the installed pdfkit version (0.19.1) - it just wrapped normally instead, silently discarding the width constraint the row height was sized for. A first fix attempt (adding `height`/`lineBreak: false` to the existing `ellipsis: true` option) still reproduced the identical wrapping bug when re-tested - pdfkit's ellipsis logic doesn't reliably respect `lineBreak: false` either.

**Fix:** Replaced reliance on pdfkit's built-in ellipsis with manual measurement: a new `truncateToWidth(doc, text, maxWidth)` helper binary-searches (via `doc.widthOfString()`, using whatever font/size is currently set) for the longest prefix of `text` that fits `maxWidth` alongside a `…` character, and `drawSimpleTable()` now truncates every header/cell string with it before calling `doc.text(..., { lineBreak: false })` - guaranteeing a single line that never overflows regardless of pdfkit's ellipsis quirks. Verified by regenerating the exact PDF from the user's report (`jamco` tenant, the same long `WH-...` warehouse codes) - confirmed the fix actually works this time (`WH-17852…` on one line) after the first attempt had silently failed the same manual check.

Also widened the Stock Take blind-count sheet's SKU column (`0.2` → `0.28` of table width, borrowing from Item Name and Unit) - unlike the Executive Report's "Code" column (a secondary reference field where ellipsis truncation is an acceptable tradeoff), a stock-take sheet's SKU is the primary key someone physically matches against shelf inventory, so truncating it would make the printed sheet unusable. `reportExports.test.ts`'s two PDF-content assertions (`stock-intelligence PDF contains the real seeded SKU`, `closeouts PDF contains the real seeded shop name`) also needed their synthetic test values shortened - they embedded a full millisecond timestamp (`REX-1785419438722`, `Report Export Shop 1785419438722`) which is far longer than realistic production SKUs/shop names and was legitimately triggering the newly-correct truncation, an artifact of the test data rather than a real regression.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 328/328 passing both times (including the corrected `reportExports.test.ts`/`stockTake.test.ts` assertions). Manually regenerated the user's exact failing PDF (same tenant name, same long warehouse codes) via a throwaway test script and visually confirmed the fix before/after.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/pdfGenerationService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/reportExports.test.ts`.

**Also confirmed not a bug (user-reported, investigated, found legitimate)**: a downloaded `profit_and_loss_....csv` showing all-zero balances for "Consulting Income"/"Office Rent" is real data - those are the exact account names from `BulkImportWizard.tsx`'s sample Chart-of-Accounts CSV template, meaning the tenant imported the sample template as-is and hasn't posted any real transactions yet. A separately-shared `Stock_Intelligence_Report_....csv` still showing the old fake hardcoded data ("Samsung 24 Inch Monitor" etc.) was downloaded before PR #35 merged and Render redeployed - `GET /analytics/export/csv` returning real data was part of that same already-merged fix, so a fresh download post-redeploy should be correct.

## [Date: 2026-07-30] - Replace Native `alert()` Popups with a Real Toast Notification System (App-Wide)

**What:** The user hit the "Failed to export PDF report." error via a native browser `alert()` popup on the Inventory Intelligence page (a jarring, blocking JS dialog: `ledgio-fawn.vercel.app says...`) and asked for it to be fixed. Investigation found `alert()` was used for error/validation messaging in **15 files, 43 call sites** across the app - exports, journal entries, invoices, vendor bills, team management, POS till open/close, bank reconciliation, bulk import, and warehouse management - none of the 15 files had any non-blocking notification alternative.

**Fix:** New `frontend/src/contexts/ToastContext.tsx` - a `ToastProvider` (mounted in `App.tsx` alongside `ThemeProvider`/`AuthProvider`, wrapping the whole app so it's available regardless of auth/route state) exposing a `useToast()` hook with `showToast(message, variant)` (`"success" | "error" | "info"`, defaults to `"error"` since that's what every existing call site needed). Renders a stacked, auto-dismissing (5s), individually-dismissable toast list fixed to the top-right of the viewport, styled consistently with the rest of the UI (Tailwind, dark-mode aware, lucide-react icons per variant).

Swept all 43 `alert(...)` call sites across the 15 files to `showToast(..., "error")`, preserving each call site's exact original message and success/failure semantics - a pure notification-mechanism swap, no behavior changes. Left two things alone, both intentionally: `window.confirm(...)` in `JournalList.tsx` (a blocking yes/no confirmation before voiding an entry - a toast can't replace that) and inline form-level error state (e.g. `PointOfSale.tsx`'s `setSaleError` shown directly in the sale form) which was never routed through `alert()` in the first place.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` and `npm run build` (frontend) both clean. **Live verification**: started real dev servers, seeded a real tenant, used Playwright to intercept and force-fail the `/analytics/export/pdf` request (reproducing the exact screenshot scenario) and click the real "Export PDF" button on Inventory Intelligence - confirmed **no native `dialog` event fired** (Playwright's `page.on('dialog')` listener, which would catch any `alert()`/`confirm()`, never triggered) and a real toast rendered with the correct message ("Failed to export PDF report."); also confirmed the dismiss button removes it. Repo-wide `grep` for `alert(` across `frontend/src` returns zero matches.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/contexts/ToastContext.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, and the 15 files with swept `alert()` calls: `frontend/src/pages/reports/{BalanceSheet,ProfitAndLoss,ExecutiveReports}.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/analytics/InventoryIntelligence.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/journals/JournalList.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/journals/JournalBuilder.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/team/TeamManagement.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/bills/VendorBills.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/audit/AuditLogs.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/banking/BankReconciliation.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/import/BulkImportWizard.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/inventory/WarehouseManagement.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/pos/PointOfSale.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-30] - Real PDF + Word (.docx) Export for Balance Sheet, P&L, Executive Reports, Inventory Intelligence (+ fix fake CSV data)

**What:** The user clicked "Export PDF" on the Executive Reports and Balance Sheet pages and got the browser's native print dialog instead of a downloaded file. Confirmed as a real, repeated bug: `window.print()` was wired up behind an "Export PDF" button on **4 pages** - `BalanceSheet.tsx`, `ProfitAndLoss.tsx`, `ExecutiveReports.tsx`, `InventoryIntelligence.tsx` - none of which produced an actual PDF file. While investigating, a second real bug surfaced in the same code path: `GET /analytics/export/csv` didn't query the database at all - it returned two hardcoded literal CSV strings (fake SKUs like "Samsung 24 Inch Monitor", fake shop names like "Osu Shop Store") regardless of `reportType` or the real tenant's data. Same "fake stub behind a real-looking button" anti-pattern already fixed once this session for the Quick Start Guide PDF (`CLAUDE.md`'s "No Mock Data Ever" rule).

**Fix:**
1. Extracted `backend/src/routes/analytics.ts`'s previously-inline `/stock-intelligence` and `/executive-summary` logic into a new `backend/src/services/analyticsService.ts` (`getStockIntelligence`, `getExecutiveSummary`, `getCloseoutsForExport`) so CSV/PDF/DOCX export and the on-screen view all share one data source instead of re-deriving it.
2. Added 4 real PDF generators to `pdfGenerationService.ts` (`generateBalanceSheetPdf`, `generateProfitAndLossPdf`, `generateExecutiveReportPdf`, `generateStockIntelligencePdf`), reusing its existing `addSectionHeading`/`drawSimpleTable`/brand-color helpers.
3. Added `docx` as a new backend dependency and a new `reportDocxService.ts` with 4 matching Word-document generators (same data, same 4 reports), for genuine `.docx` downloads alongside PDF - confirmed with the user this was wanted, not just a PDF fix.
4. New export routes: `GET /reports/balance-sheet/export?format=pdf|docx`, `GET /reports/profit-loss/export?format=pdf|docx` (`reports.ts`), and `GET /analytics/export/pdf?reportType=...`, `GET /analytics/export/docx?reportType=...` (`analytics.ts`, mirroring the existing `/export/csv?reportType=` convention).
5. Rewrote `GET /analytics/export/csv` to build real CSV via `analyticsService` + the existing `utils/csvExport.ts`'s `buildCsv()` helper instead of the two hardcoded literal strings - the actual fake-data bug fix, covering both `stock-intelligence` and the daily/monthly/yearly/closeouts report types. Added `getCloseoutsForExport` so the "closeouts" tab - which previously had no export beyond what's on screen - is now fully exportable.
6. Frontend: new shared `frontend/src/lib/downloadBlob.ts` helper (dedupes the blob-download boilerplate previously copy-pasted 3x). All 4 report pages' `window.print()`-based "Export PDF" buttons replaced with real downloads; each gained a new "Export Word" button. `BalanceSheet.tsx`/`ProfitAndLoss.tsx` keep their separate, honestly-labeled "Print" button using `window.print()` - that one wasn't part of the bug.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/reportExports.test.ts` (11 tests): real PDF/DOCX magic-byte + content-type checks for all 4 reports, plus the concrete fake-CSV-data regression test (seeds a real inventory item and a real till closeout, asserts the CSV contains the real seeded SKU/shop name and does **not** contain any of the old literal strings). Full backend suite run twice in a row: 328/328 passing both times. **Live manual verification**: started both dev servers, seeded a real tenant with a real posted journal entry, used Playwright to click the actual "Export PDF"/"Export Word" buttons on Balance Sheet, P&L, Executive Reports, and Inventory Intelligence in a real browser - confirmed 6 real files downloaded with correct `%PDF-`/`PK` zip magic bytes and multi-KB sizes, not the browser print dialog.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/analyticsService.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/pdfGenerationService.ts`, `backend/src/services/reportDocxService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/reports.ts`, `backend/src/routes/analytics.ts`, `backend/src/tests/reportExports.test.ts` (new), `backend/package.json` (added `docx`), `frontend/src/lib/downloadBlob.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/reports/BalanceSheet.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/reports/ProfitAndLoss.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/reports/ExecutiveReports.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/analytics/InventoryIntelligence.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-30] - Add Contra Voucher (Internal Fund Transfer) (Item #6b on the Ghana Market Research Roadmap)

**What:** Fourth item off the roadmap doc's quick-win list. TallyPrime's "Contra Voucher" concept - an internal transfer between the business's own cash/bank/till accounts (e.g. "till to bank") - was a confirmed real gap: no dedicated entity or UI existed, so recording one required hand-building a manual journal entry with no guardrails.

**Fix:** No schema migration needed - `createJournalEntry` (`journalEntryService.ts`) already accepted a caller-supplied `entryNumber`, so a Contra Voucher is implemented as a constrained wrapper (`createContraVoucher`) that generates its own `CV-` prefixed entry number (vs. the generic `JE-`) as the discriminator, rather than adding a new "type" column. The new function validates the source/destination accounts are different, both are `ASSET`-type (the closest available proxy for "cash/bank/till" - the schema has no dedicated `CASH`/`BANK` subtype), and the amount is positive, then builds the two-line entry (destination debited, source credited) and posts it immediately (it records a transfer that already happened, not a draft to review later). New route `POST /api/v1/journal-entries/contra` (`requireRole('Accountant')`, same as regular journal entry creation).

Frontend: new dedicated `ContraVoucher.tsx` page at `/journals/contra` (a "Transfer Funds" button added next to "Create Entry" on the Journal Entries list) - a constrained form (Date, From/To account dropdowns restricted to Asset-type accounts, Amount, optional Memo) rather than the generic multi-line Journal Builder. Also surfaced `entryNumber` in `JournalList.tsx`'s "Journal ID" column for the first time (it previously always showed the raw UUID, since `useJournals.ts`'s mapper never copied it over) - needed to make the `CV-` discriminator actually visible/searchable in the list, not just present in the database.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `describe('8. Contra Voucher ...')` block added to `backend/src/tests/journalEntries.test.ts` (6 new tests: valid transfer produces a correctly-balanced `CV-` numbered POSTED entry; same source/destination rejected; non-positive amount rejected; non-Asset account rejected; Viewer role blocked; the entry surfaces in the regular list). Full backend suite run twice in a row: 317/317 passing both times. **Live manual verification**: started both dev servers, onboarded a real tenant with real Cash/Bank accounts, used Playwright to fill out and submit the actual Contra Voucher form in a real browser, and confirmed the resulting Journal Entries list shows a real `CV-...` entry with the correct accounts, amount, description, and "Posted" status.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/journalEntryService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/journalEntries.ts`, `backend/src/tests/journalEntries.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/journals/ContraVoucher.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/pages/journals/JournalList.tsx`, `frontend/src/hooks/useJournals.ts`, `frontend/src/types/accounting.ts`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-29] - Ghana Market Research Follow-Through: Tax-Compliance Disclaimer + Honest Search-Bar Copy

(Cmd+K)") and `CommandMenu.tsx`'s ("Type a command or search...") both imply real full-text search across financial data. The actual `CommandMenu.tsx` is a static list of 8 navigation shortcuts (Dashboard/Chart of Accounts/Journal Entries/General Ledger/Profit & Loss/Preferences/Light/Dark Mode) with no data-search logic at all - `cmdk`'s built-in filtering only matches against those 8 static labels. Changed both placeholders to honestly describe quick navigation ("Quick navigation... (Cmd+K)" / "Jump to a page or setting...") rather than building real cross-app search right now (a separate, much larger feature, still on the roadmap list as its own item).
