## [Date: 2026-07-29] - Render Legal Documents as Real Markdown, Not Raw Text (react-markdown + remark-gfm)

**What:** Immediately after shipping the 4 new `/legal/:policyName` pages, the user flagged the actual rendering as unprofessional - `LegalDocumentPage.tsx` was rendering the fetched Markdown as raw text (`whitespace-pre-line`), so every document showed literal `#`/`##`/`**`/`|` characters straight from the source `.md` files instead of real headings, bold text, and tables. A real Terms of Service/Privacy Policy showing raw Markdown syntax to a business owner reads as broken, not just unstyled.

**Fix:** Added `react-markdown` + `remark-gfm` (GFM adds pipe-table support, which `PRIVACY_POLICY.md`'s subprocessor table needs) as frontend dependencies. `LegalDocumentPage.tsx` now renders the fetched content through `<ReactMarkdown>` with a full set of custom-styled component overrides (`h1`-`h3`, `p`, `ul`/`ol`/`li`, `strong`, `a`, `blockquote`, `code`, `hr`, and a real `<table>`) matching the site's existing dark theme palette - no `@tailwindcss/typography` plugin exists in this repo (confirmed via grep), so styling is applied directly via Tailwind utility classes on each element rather than relying on `prose` classes, which would have been a no-op. The Markdown-sourced placeholder callout (`> **Placeholder notice:** ...` in `PRIVACY_POLICY.md`) now renders as a distinct amber blockquote aside instead of blending into the body text.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` clean. **Live manual verification**: started both dev servers, used Playwright to load all 4 `/legal/:policyName` pages and confirmed via `document.body.innerText` that no raw Markdown syntax (`##`, `**`, `| ---`) leaks through on any of them; confirmed a real `<table>` DOM element renders for the Privacy Policy's subprocessor table (previously a run-on line of pipe characters); confirmed via computed-style inspection that paragraph styling is consistent across the document (ruling out an apparent color difference in one screenshot, which turned out to be a PNG compression artifact, not a real bug).
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/pages/legal/LegalDocumentPage.tsx`, `frontend/package.json`, `frontend/package-lock.json`.

## [Date: 2026-07-29] - Add an HR Role and Restrict Settings/Team-Management-Style Screens by Role

**What:** The user pointed out a real gap: every authenticated user, regardless of role, saw the exact same full sidebar - Shop Manager/Cashier (already warehouse-scoped on the backend for Inventory/Till writes) could still see and click into Workspace Settings, Tax Rates, Team Management, and every financial report screen, even though the backend would reject any actual write they attempted. Backend enforcement (`requireRole('Admin')` on `PUT /tenants/current`, invite, and warehouse-access endpoints) was already correct - the gap was entirely the frontend advertising screens these roles have no real use for, and no dedicated, reduced view existed for two roles the user specifically wants to onboard: HR (people/roster management, not full Admin power) and Auditor (financial-record review, not operational data entry).

**Fix:**
- **New "HR" role**, added to the closed role set (`backend/src/services/warehouseAccessService.ts`'s `CLOSED_ROLES`, `frontend/src/pages/team/TeamManagement.tsx`'s invite-role dropdown) - selectable at invite time like any other role, not location-scoped (sees the whole business's team roster, same as Accountant/Auditor/Viewer already do).
- `backend/src/middleware/rbacMiddleware.ts`: HR (and, unchanged, Viewer/Auditor) is now explicitly denied by default on any `requireRole(...)`-gated route unless that role is listed there explicitly - previously, any role string not recognized as Viewer/Auditor fell into the "custom worker job title" fallback bucket (built for titles like "Shop Manager"/"Store Clerk") and got full operational access to Inventory/Invoices/Bills/Journals by default, which would have been wrong for HR.
- **Role-scoped Sidebar** (`frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`): added a `RESTRICTED_ROLE_NAV` map - Shop Manager/Cashier see only Dashboard + Inventory + Point of Sale (their actual operational scope); HR sees only Dashboard + Team Management (their "own screen," a read-only roster view since `TeamManagement.tsx` already hides the Invite/Manage-Access controls for non-Admins); Auditor sees a read-focused financial-review set (Chart of Accounts, Journal Entries, Banking, Invoices, Bills, all Reports, Audit Trail) with no operational entry points (no POS, no Inventory writes, no Settings, no Team Management). Admin, Accountant, Viewer, and any legacy free-text worker title are unrestricted, matching pre-existing behavior - only the 4 roles above changed.
- **Real route-level guard**, not just a hidden link (`frontend/src/App.tsx`): `/settings`, `/settings/tax-rates`, `/settings/fiscal-periods`, `/settings/recurring-transactions` now redirect Shop Manager/Cashier/HR/Auditor straight to `/dashboard` if they navigate there directly by URL, rather than silently rendering a settings UI they can't actually use.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. Full backend suite (`npm test`) run three times in a row against real local Postgres/Redis: 311/311 passing (one unrelated pre-existing flake - `adminBroadcastEncrypted.test.ts`'s batch-send test failing under full-suite load - reproduced once, then passed clean on the next two full runs and in isolation, confirmed unrelated to this change). New `describe('HR role - team-roster visibility only, no admin/operational powers')` block added to `backend/src/tests/warehousePermissions.test.ts`: HR is a valid non-location-scoped invite role; HR can view the team roster; HR is blocked (403) from inviting staff, changing workspace settings, and unrelated operational writes (inventory adjustments) it has no explicit access to. **Live manual verification**: started both dev servers against a real local Postgres/Redis, onboarded a real tenant, invited real Admin/Shop Manager/HR/Auditor accounts via the actual API, and used Playwright to log in as each and inspect the real rendered sidebar - confirmed Admin sees the full unrestricted nav and can reach `/settings`; Shop Manager sees only Dashboard/Inventory/POS with no Settings link and is redirected to `/dashboard` on a direct `/settings` URL visit; HR sees only Dashboard/Team Management, same redirect behavior; Auditor sees the intended read-focused financial set with no Settings/POS/Inventory/Team-Management items, same redirect behavior.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/warehouseAccessService.ts`, `backend/src/middleware/rbacMiddleware.ts`, `backend/src/tests/warehousePermissions.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/team/TeamManagement.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-29] - Migrate Outbound Email from Resend to SendGrid (Single Sender Verification, No Domain Needed)

**What:** With `RESEND_API_KEY` actually set in production, real live logs confirmed the Resend integration itself works correctly - but every send to a real user failed with a 403: `"You can only send testing emails to your own email address ([the account owner's Resend signup email]). To send emails to other recipients, please verify a domain at resend.com/domains..."`. Resend's sandbox restriction requires a verified domain before it will deliver to anyone but the account owner - and the user doesn't currently own a domain. Asked what to do; the user chose to send from their own individual email address instead of buying a domain.

**Fix:** Migrated `EmailService.ts` from Resend to **SendGrid's v3 Mail Send API** (`POST https://api.sendgrid.com/v3/mail/send`), using SendGrid's **Single Sender Verification** rather than full domain authentication - this lets the user verify one individual email address (e.g. their own Gmail address) in the SendGrid dashboard and send to arbitrary recipients from it, with no domain purchase required. Key differences from the Resend implementation:
- `EmailService.isConfigured()` now requires **both** `SENDGRID_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_FROM` - unlike Resend, which had a sandbox fallback address, SendGrid's single-sender mode has no usable default; `EMAIL_FROM` must exactly match the address verified in SendGrid or the send is rejected.
- New `parseFromAddress()` splits `"Display Name <email@example.com>"` into SendGrid's separate `{email, name}` fields (SendGrid's API doesn't accept a combined string like Resend's did).
- Payload/response shape changed to SendGrid's format (`personalizations`/`content`/base64 `attachments` with `disposition: 'attachment'`); `sendMail()`'s public signature, retry-once-after-5-minutes behavior, and audit logging are otherwise unchanged.
- Updated `backend/.env.example` and `render.yaml` (`RESEND_API_KEY`/optional `EMAIL_FROM` → `SENDGRID_API_KEY`/required `EMAIL_FROM`), `backend/src/routes/health.ts`'s `integrations.email` check, `health.test.ts`'s credential-leak assertion, and `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s cosmetic copy ("Email Service (Resend)" → "Email Service (SendGrid)", etc.) to match.

**Known tradeoff, explicitly flagged to the user:** Single Sender Verification has weaker deliverability than a verified domain (more likely to land in spam folders) and a lower sending-reputation ceiling - it's a real stopgap to unblock real user delivery today, not the long-term answer. Migrating to full domain authentication once the user owns a domain is a straightforward follow-up (same SendGrid API, just a different verification type - no further code change needed beyond re-pointing `EMAIL_FROM` at the new domain address).

**Not yet confirmed working end-to-end** - this environment can't make outbound HTTPS calls to SendGrid either, so this is verified by compilation + full test suite only. Needs: sign up at sendgrid.com, verify a Single Sender under **Settings → Sender Authentication → Single Sender Verification** (confirms via a link sent to that email address), generate an API key, then set `SENDGRID_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_FROM` (matching the verified sender exactly) in Render.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. Full backend suite (`npm test`) run twice in a row against real local Postgres/Redis: 306/306 passing both times.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/EmailService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/health.ts`, `backend/src/tests/health.test.ts`, `backend/.env.example`, `render.yaml`, `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-29] - Migrate Outbound Email from SMTP (Gmail/Nodemailer) to Resend's HTTP API

**What:** After both SMTP submission ports failed identically (port 465, then the port 587/STARTTLS experiment from the previous entry - confirmed via a live log showing the same `ETIMEDOUT`/`command: 'CONN'` error on 587 too), it's now confirmed Render blocks outbound SMTP entirely, not just one port - a well-known anti-spam-abuse restriction on many cloud/PaaS hosts. Direct SMTP to Gmail was never going to work from this host regardless of port or credential. Asked the user which HTTP-based transactional email provider to migrate to (`AskUserQuestion`); they chose **Resend**.

**Fix:** Rewrote `backend/src/services/EmailService.ts` to send via `POST https://api.resend.com/emails` (`axios`, `Authorization: Bearer <RESEND_API_KEY>`) instead of `nodemailer`/SMTP - this runs over port 443, which isn't subject to the same outbound restriction. `sendMail()`'s public signature, retry-once-after-5-minutes behavior, and audit logging (`EMAIL_SENT`/`CRITICAL_FAILURE`) are all unchanged; only the transport underneath changed. `EmailService.isConfigured()` now checks `RESEND_API_KEY` instead of `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS`. Removed `nodemailer`/`@types/nodemailer` from `backend/package.json` entirely (regenerated `package-lock.json`). Updated `backend/.env.example` and `render.yaml`'s env var block to document `RESEND_API_KEY`/`EMAIL_FROM` in place of `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS`. `backend/src/routes/health.ts`'s `integrations.email` diagnostic (and its test in `health.test.ts`) now reflect `RESEND_API_KEY` instead of the old Gmail credential pair. Updated `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s cosmetic copy ("Gmail SMTP Service" → "Email Service (Resend)", "Email (Gmail SMTP)" → "Email (Resend)") to match.

**Not yet confirmed working end-to-end** - this environment has no way to make a real outbound HTTPS call to Resend either, so this is verified by compilation + full test suite only, not a live send. Needs a real `RESEND_API_KEY` (the user must sign up at resend.com and generate one) pasted into Render's `RESEND_API_KEY` env var, then a real registration/resend-verification attempt to confirm delivery. Until a real sending domain is verified on the Resend account, mail sends from Resend's sandbox address (`onboarding@resend.dev`) and can only be delivered to the Resend account's own registered email - `EMAIL_FROM` should be set once a real domain is verified.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. Full backend suite (`npm test`) run twice in a row against real local Postgres/Redis: 306/306 passing both times, including the updated `health.test.ts` credential-leak assertion.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/EmailService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/health.ts`, `backend/src/tests/health.test.ts`, `backend/package.json`, `backend/package-lock.json`, `backend/.env.example`, `render.yaml`, `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-29] - Try SMTP Port 587 (STARTTLS) After Port 465 Kept Timing Out on Render

**What:** After the earlier IPv6 fix (`ENETUNREACH` on `smtp.gmail.com`, fixed via `dns.setDefaultResultOrder('ipv4first')` + `family: 4`) actually deployed, real verification emails still failed - but with a different error this time: `Error: Connection timeout ... code: 'ETIMEDOUT', command: 'CONN'` on port 465. Confirmed via a live log this is a genuinely new failure mode (no more IPv6 addresses in the error), consistent with the hosting platform restricting outbound port 465 specifically - a common anti-spam-abuse restriction on cloud/PaaS hosts (port 465 uses implicit TLS; port 587, the standard SMTP *submission* port with STARTTLS, is much less commonly blocked).

**Fix:** `EmailService.ts`'s `getTransporter()` switched from `port: 465, secure: true` to `port: 587, secure: false, requireTLS: true` (STARTTLS negotiated after connecting, standard Gmail-supported submission config). Added `connectionTimeout: 10000` so a still-blocked port fails fast (10s) instead of hanging on nodemailer's much longer default timeout.

**Not yet confirmed working** - this is an experiment based on a well-known class of platform restriction, not something verifiable from the sandbox (no outbound SMTP access here either). Needs a real end-to-end test once deployed: trigger a resend-verification and check the Logs tab for `[EmailService] ✅ Email dispatched successfully` vs. another `❌`/`ETIMEDOUT`. If port 587 is *also* blocked, the durable fix is migrating off direct SMTP entirely to an HTTPS-based transactional email API (SendGrid, Mailgun, Resend, Postmark, etc.) - those aren't subject to SMTP-port restrictions since they run over port 443. Not implemented yet since port 587 hasn't been ruled out.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. No test asserts an exact SMTP port (`grep` confirmed).
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/EmailService.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-07-28] - Add "Resend Verification" So a Lost/Undelivered Verification Email Doesn't Permanently Block Login

**What:** While testing real onboarding on the live deploy, the user hit a real dead end: an account whose verification email never arrived (unrelated Gmail app-password/deliverability issue on the live environment) got stuck permanently at `POST /auth/login`'s "User account has been deactivated." error, with no way to trigger the email again short of asking someone to fix it server-side. The verification email is only ever sent once, during initial onboarding (`tenantService.ts`'s `onboardTenant`) - there was no resend path anywhere in the app.

**Fix:**
- New `POST /api/v1/auth/resend-verification` (`backend/src/routes/auth.ts`, added right after the existing `/verify` route, inherits the router-level `authRateLimiter` already applied to all of `/api/v1/auth`) - takes `{ email }`, looks up the user, and for whichever of email/SMS verification is still pending, regenerates a fresh token/code (mirrors the exact generation logic `tenantService.ts`'s onboarding flow already uses) and resends via `EmailService.sendVerificationEmail`/`SmsService.send`. Returns 400 if the account is already fully verified, 404 if no account matches. Records an `AUTH.VERIFICATION_RESENT` audit log entry.
- `frontend/src/pages/auth/Verification.tsx`: added a "Resend Verification Email" button directly under the pending-email status card (only shown while email is unverified), calling the new endpoint and showing a real success/failure message.
- `frontend/src/pages/auth/Login.tsx`: when login fails specifically with "User account has been deactivated.", the error banner now shows an inline "Resend Verification" action instead of leaving the user stuck with no next step.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` + `npm run build` (frontend) both clean. Extended `backend/src/tests/verifiedRegistration.test.ts` (using its existing single-tenant registration flow) with 3 new cases: resending for an unverified account regenerates both the email token and SMS code (proving it's not a no-op) and leaves the account still inactive; resending for an unknown email returns 404; resending for an already-fully-verified account returns 400. Full suite could not be run in this sandbox session (no local Postgres/Redis containers up) - relying on CI's `backend` job, which does run against real Postgres/Redis, plus a manual end-to-end check on the live deploy once the underlying Gmail app-password issue is confirmed fixed.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/routes/auth.ts`, `backend/src/tests/verifiedRegistration.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/auth/Verification.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/auth/Login.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-28] - Fix Real Render Deploy Failure #2: NODE_ENV=production Silently Drops devDependencies Needed by the Build

**What:** After the `moduleResolution` fix merged, the next Render deploy attempt failed one step later - `npm run build` now ran but `tsc` immediately errored with `TS7016: Could not find a declaration file for module 'cors'`/`'pdfkit'` and `TS2503: Cannot find namespace 'PDFKit'`, confirmed via the actual Render build log the user shared.

**Root cause:** `render.yaml` sets `NODE_ENV=production` as a service env var, which Render applies uniformly across both the build and runtime phases (there's no separate build-only/runtime-only scoping in the Blueprint spec). `npm ci` inherits that env var and, in this npm/Node setup, treats `NODE_ENV=production` as equivalent to `--omit=dev` - silently skipping the entire `devDependencies` block, including `typescript` itself and the `@types/*` packages the compile step needs. Reproduced exactly: a clean local `rm -rf node_modules && NODE_ENV=production npm ci` left `typescript`, `@types/cors`, and `@types/pdfkit` all absent from `node_modules`, then `npm run build` hit the identical errors seen on Render. Notably, `@types/node-cron` and `@types/nodemailer` were *already* sitting in `dependencies` (not `devDependencies`) in `backend/package.json` before this fix - a clear sign this exact class of bug was hit and worked around for those two packages previously, but not applied consistently to the rest.

**Fix:** Moved every package the actual `tsc` compile of `src/**/*` needs into `dependencies` (which `npm ci` always installs regardless of `NODE_ENV`): `typescript`, `@types/express`, `@types/node`, `@types/cors`, `@types/pdfkit`, and `prisma` (the CLI - needed by `buildCommand`'s `prisma generate`/`migrate deploy`, previously relying on `npx`'s on-demand-fetch fallback rather than the pinned local version). Left genuinely test-only packages in `devDependencies` (`jest`, `ts-jest`, `supertest`, `ts-node-dev`, `@types/jest`, `@types/supertest`, `pdf-parse`, `@types/pdf-parse`) since `*.test.ts` files are excluded from the `tsc` build (`tsconfig.json`'s `exclude`) and aren't needed for a production build. Regenerated `package-lock.json` via `npm install` (package-lock tracks each package's dev-flag independently of `package.json`'s section, so this was required for the fix to actually take effect under `npm ci`).

**Verification:** Reproduced Render's exact `buildCommand` end-to-end locally under `NODE_ENV=production` (`npm ci && npx prisma generate && npm run build`) - all three steps succeed with zero errors, matching what Render will now do. `tsc --noEmit` clean under normal (non-production) `NODE_ENV` too, confirming the local dev/CI workflow is unaffected.
**Files Affected:** `backend/package.json`, `backend/package-lock.json`.

## [Date: 2026-07-28] - Fix Real Render Deploy Failure: Legacy `moduleResolution` Rejected by tsc

**What:** The Render Blueprint deploy failed at the `ledgio-backend` build step (the Redis service deployed fine) with `tsconfig.json(5,25): error TS5108: Option 'moduleResolution=node10' has been removed. Please remove it from your configuration.` - confirmed via the actual Render build log the user shared.

**Investigation:** `backend/tsconfig.json` had `"moduleResolution": "node"`, which TypeScript treats as an alias for the legacy `"node10"` resolution strategy (confirmed via `tsc --showConfig`, which reported it back as `"node10"`). A clean local `rm -rf node_modules && npm ci` against the exact same committed `package-lock.json` (pinning TypeScript 5.9.3) built without any error, ruling out a dependency-resolution/lockfile drift explanation - the difference is environmental (Render defaults to Node.js 24.14.1; this deprecated option is apparently already being enforced as a hard error there). Regardless of the exact trigger, `"moduleResolution": "node"`/`"node10"` is a legacy, deprecated setting on an eventual hard-removal path in TypeScript itself, so fixing it now is correct independent of why it surfaced first on Render.

**Fix:** Removed the explicit `"moduleResolution": "node"` line from `backend/tsconfig.json` entirely - with `"module": "commonjs"` already set, TypeScript infers the equivalent correct resolution strategy automatically, no explicit legacy option needed. Confirmed `frontend/tsconfig.app.json` already uses the modern `"moduleResolution": "bundler"` value, so no equivalent fix was needed there.

**Verification:** Clean `npm ci` + `npm run build` succeeds with zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Full backend test suite could not be run in this sandbox session (no local Postgres/Redis containers currently up) - relying on the CI workflow's `backend` job (which does run against real Postgres/Redis) to confirm no behavioral regression, since this is a compiler-option-only change with an already-passing clean build.
**Files Affected:** `backend/tsconfig.json`.

## [Date: 2026-07-28] - Prepare Frontend for Vercel Deployment

**What:** The user asked to prepare the frontend for hosting on Vercel (the backend is already on Render, per the earlier `render.yaml` work). Investigation found two real gaps that would have broken a Vercel deployment, not just missing config:

1. **`frontend/src/lib/api.ts` hardcoded `API_BASE_URL = 'http://localhost:4000/api/v1'`** with no env-var override anywhere - every API call from a Vercel-hosted build would have tried (and failed) to reach `localhost` on the visitor's own machine. Fixed: `API_BASE_URL` now reads `import.meta.env.VITE_API_BASE_URL`, falling back to the same `localhost:4000` default for local dev when unset.
2. **No SPA rewrite config for Vercel.** The app uses `react-router-dom`'s `BrowserRouter` (confirmed in `App.tsx`), so a hard refresh or direct link to any non-root route (e.g. `/journals`) needs the host to serve `index.html` instead of 404ing - Vercel doesn't infer this automatically for a Vite project. New `frontend/vercel.json` with a catch-all `rewrites: [{ source: "/(.*)", destination: "/index.html" }]` (Vercel checks real static files first, so this doesn't intercept the actual built JS/CSS/PWA service worker/manifest assets - only unmatched routes fall through to `index.html`).

Also documented `VITE_API_BASE_URL` in `frontend/.env.example` alongside the existing `VITE_MONO_PUBLIC_KEY` entry.

**Not changed, flagged instead:** once the frontend is actually deployed to a real Vercel URL, the backend's `render.yaml` `APP_URL`/`CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` env vars (currently `sync: false`, unset) need to be filled in with that real URL in Render's dashboard - the backend will otherwise reject credentialed cross-origin requests from the deployed frontend. This is a manual step for the user after both are live, not something this change can complete ahead of time.

**Verification:** `tsc -b` clean. `npm run build` succeeds (Vite + PWA service-worker generation both complete normally); no other hardcoded `localhost` references found anywhere else in `frontend/src`.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/lib/api.ts`, `frontend/.env.example`, `frontend/vercel.json` (new).

Added a comment noting that if/when the service upgrades off the free plan, moving `prisma migrate deploy` back to `preDeployCommand` is preferable (it runs closer to actual deploy time and doesn't block the build from completing on a migration failure).

## [Date: 2026-07-28] - Wire render.yaml to Prisma Postgres Instead of Render's Own Postgres

**What:** The user asked to use Prisma Postgres (their existing Prisma Console project - this repo already has the "Prisma Compute Deploy" integration connected, see the 2026-07-27 `prisma.compute.ts` entry) as the database, rather than provisioning a separate Postgres instance through Render. Before wiring this up, checked whether Prisma Postgres is actually compatible with what this app needs - it relies heavily on raw SQL and a `SET LOCAL search_path` pattern for tenant-schema switching (`tenantClient.ts`'s `withTenantDb`), which not every "Postgres-compatible" proxy supports.

**Finding:** Prisma Postgres offers two connection modes: a **pooled** connection (`pooled.db.prisma.io`) for normal app traffic, and a **direct** TCP connection (`db.prisma.io`, standard Postgres wire protocol) for operations needing session continuity (migrations, `LISTEN/NOTIFY`, session-level `SET`). This app's `SET LOCAL search_path` pattern is already transaction-scoped (by design - it was built this way earlier in the session specifically to survive Prisma's `$transaction` connection pinning), which is exactly the kind of pattern PgBouncer-style transaction pooling is designed to support safely. So the pooled connection is fine for the app's own runtime queries; only `prisma migrate deploy`/`migrate dev` genuinely need the direct one.

**Fix:** Added `directUrl = env("DIRECT_URL")` to `schema.prisma`'s `datasource` block (the standard Prisma pattern for this exact pooled/direct split). Prisma errors on migrate commands if `DIRECT_URL` is declared but unset, even when there's no real pooler in front - so `DIRECT_URL` was added everywhere `DATABASE_URL` already is (local `.env`, `.env.example`, the CI workflow), set to the same value in all three since none of them use an external pooler. `render.yaml` no longer provisions a Render-managed Postgres `databases:` entry - `DATABASE_URL`/`DIRECT_URL` are now `sync: false` (the user pastes both connection strings from their Prisma Postgres database's "Connect" tab in Prisma Console during Blueprint setup).

**Verification:** `prisma validate`/`prisma generate` both clean with the new `directUrl` field. `prisma migrate deploy` confirmed to actually attempt applying migrations against `DIRECT_URL` (not fail on a missing-env-var error) - the only failure hit was pre-existing local-sandbox migration-bookkeeping drift, unrelated to this change and already resolved locally via `prisma migrate resolve --applied`. Full backend suite run: 302/302 passing, confirming the app's own queries work correctly with `directUrl` present in the schema. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/.env.example`, `backend/.env` (gitignored, local only), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `render.yaml`.

## [Date: 2026-07-28] - POS: Real Multi-Item Cart (Search + Basket Checkout) Replacing One-Item-At-A-Time Sales

**What:** A user reported that recording a sale for a customer buying several items was a real headache: the Point of Sale screen had no search (just a giant product `<select>`) and, worse, the backend genuinely only supported one item per "sale" at all - `POST /tills/sales` took a single `itemId`/`quantity`, with no cart or line-items concept anywhere in the schema. A 5-item purchase meant 5 separate transactions, 5 separate cash-given/change calculations, and 5 separate receipts. Confirmed with the user (via `AskUserQuestion`) that the real fix was a proper multi-item cart, not just a faster item picker.

**Backend:**
- New `CashSaleLine` model (`tenant_id`, `sale_id` FK, `item_id`, denormalized `item_name`/`item_sku` for a stable historical receipt, `quantity`, `unit_price`, `line_total`) + `CashSale.lines` relation. New migration `20260728104447_add_cash_sale_lines`.
- Rewrote `POST /tills/sales`: body is now `{ tillId, items: [{itemId, quantity}], cashGiven }` instead of a single `itemId`/`quantity`. Validates every line up front (400 on empty cart, missing item id, non-integer/zero quantity), then inside the same `withCurrentTenantDb` transaction the existing single-item handler already used: batch-fetches all items and their warehouse stock, pre-checks every line's availability for a friendly error, computes one combined total, deducts stock per line with the same atomically-guarded decrement the old code used (so a genuine concurrent race still can't oversell), writes one `CashSale` header + N `CashSaleLine` rows, and increments the till's running total once. If any line fails (unknown item, insufficient stock), the whole transaction rolls back - confirmed via a dedicated test that a valid line in the same cart as a failing line is never partially deducted.
- `GET /tills/current` now includes each sale's `lines` so the till/receipts view can show real itemized history, not just a total.

**Frontend** (`PointOfSale.tsx`, full rewrite of the sale-recording section): a search box (name/SKU, case-insensitive) replaces the dropdown; clicking a result adds it to a running basket (or increments its quantity if already present), with each search result showing stock genuinely remaining to add (physical stock minus what's already in the basket) so the UI can never let a cashier build a basket the backend would reject. The basket has per-line +/-/remove controls, a running subtotal, and a single Cash Given/Change Due/Record Sale step for the whole basket - one receipt, one change calculation. "Today's Receipts" now shows each receipt's actual item list, not just a total.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/posCart.test.ts` (5 tests): a 2-line sale produces one receipt with the correct combined total/change and two real `CashSaleLine` rows with correct per-line stock deduction; a cart with one valid line and one insufficient-stock line is rejected with **neither** line deducted (the actual atomicity regression test); unknown item rejected; non-integer/zero quantity rejected (400, before touching the DB); empty cart rejected. Updated the 3 existing tests that hit the old single-item request shape (`cashTill.test.ts`'s concurrency test, `allEndpoints.test.ts`, `scheduledReports.test.ts`) to the new `items: [...]` shape - all still pass unchanged otherwise. Full backend suite run twice in a row: 302/302 both times. Manually verified end-to-end in a real Chromium browser against the real running app (not just curl/tests): searched for a product, added it twice by clicking, added a second product, adjusted its quantity via the basket's `+` button, entered cash given, confirmed the change-due calculation, checked out, and confirmed a single itemized receipt ("2 × Coca Cola 500ml, 2 × Fanta Orange 500ml — Total GH₵20.00 — Change given GH₵10.00") was recorded, the basket cleared, and "Today's Receipts" updated correctly.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260728104447_add_cash_sale_lines/`, `backend/src/routes/cashTill.ts`, `backend/src/tests/posCart.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/{cashTill,allEndpoints,scheduledReports}.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/pos/PointOfSale.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-28] - Audit Trail UI: Filtering + CSV Export (Tenant and Platform-Wide Views)

**What:** The real audit-log data added 2026-07-27 (actor identity, structured `changes` diffs) had no way to filter or export it - `AuditLogs.tsx` (tenant-facing) and `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s "System Audit Logs" tab both just showed an unfiltered, unpaginated-in-the-UI table, and the `changes` field was captured but never rendered anywhere. Closed both gaps, flagged as a known follow-up since that work landed.

**Backend:**
- `GET /api/v1/audit-logs` (tenant-scoped) and `GET /api/v1/admin/audit-logs` (platform-wide, passcode-gated) both gained optional filters: `action`/`entity`/`userEmail` (case-insensitive substring match) and `dateFrom`/`dateTo` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, inclusive `createdAt` range, validated and rejected with 400 if malformed). The admin route also gained a `tenantId` exact-match filter. Both routes share their filter-building logic via a local `buildAuditLogWhere`/`buildAdminAuditLogWhere` helper so the list and export endpoints below can never drift out of sync with each other.
- New `GET /api/v1/audit-logs/export` and `GET /api/v1/admin/audit-logs/export` - same filters, no pagination (capped at 5,000 most recent matches), stream a real CSV of the actual matching rows (including the structured `changes` diff, JSON-stringified per row) as a download. New small `backend/src/utils/csvExport.ts` (`buildCsv`, RFC 4180 field escaping) - no new dependency, since the existing `analytics.ts` CSV export route already showed a hand-rolled approach is enough for this codebase's needs (see below).
- **Found along the way, not fixed here:** while looking at the existing CSV-export pattern for reference, `backend/src/routes/analytics.ts`'s `GET /analytics/export/csv` turned out to be **entirely hardcoded fake data** - two literal static CSV strings returned regardless of the tenant or real state, a "No Mock Data Ever" violation matching several other fake-payload bugs fixed earlier this session (the Quick Start Guide PDF, the old bank-feed demo data). Flagged to the user; not fixed as part of this change since it's unrelated to the audit trail and deserves its own real-data implementation.

**Frontend:**
- `AuditLogs.tsx`: new filter bar (Action/Entity/User Email text inputs, From/To date pickers) with explicit Apply/Clear buttons (not live-as-you-type, to avoid refetching on every keystroke) - filters are server-side, not client-side, since audit logs are paginated. New "Changes" column renders each `changes` entry as `field: from → to`. New "Export CSV" button using the existing blob-download-through-the-authenticated-`api`-client pattern (already used twice elsewhere in this codebase) so the current filters are honored in the export.
- `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s "System Audit Logs" tab: same filter bar plus a Tenant dropdown (reusing the tenant roster already fetched for the adjacent "Tenant Schemas & Tiers" tab), a "Changes" column, and an "Export CSV" button - passcode included automatically since it's already part of every request this tab makes.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/auditLogFiltersAndExport.test.ts` (7 tests: filter by action/entity/userEmail/date-range, malformed-date 400, and - the real regression test - the export CSV contains only the requesting tenant's matching rows, never another tenant's, even under an identical filter). Extended `adminAuditLogs.test.ts` (+5 tests: tenantId/action/entity/userEmail filters, malformed-date 400, export requires the passcode and correctly joins the tenant name). Full backend suite run twice in a row: 297/297 both times. Manually verified end-to-end against a real running backend: onboarded a tenant, created two real accounts, confirmed `entity=Account` and case-insensitive `action=account.created` filters return exactly the right rows, a malformed date returns 400, and the exported CSV file is real and correctly filtered.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/utils/csvExport.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/auditLogs.ts`, `backend/src/routes/adminAuditLogs.ts`, `backend/src/tests/auditLogFiltersAndExport.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/adminAuditLogs.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/audit/AuditLogs.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminCoreEngine.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-28] - Add Void Button to Journal Entries List + Close a Real Ledger-Correctness Gap

**What:** The user asked for a Void button on `JournalList.tsx`. The backend `POST /journal-entries/:id/void` endpoint already existed, but investigating how to wire it up surfaced a real correctness gap: `voidJournalEntry` only flips `JournalEntry.status` to `VOID` - it never touches the `ledgers` table. Once an entry is POSTED, its debit/credit rows are written permanently to `ledgers`, and Trial Balance/P&L/Balance Sheet (`reportRepository.ts`) all sum directly from `ledgers` joined to `accounts`, with no filter on the parent journal entry's status. So voiding an already-POSTED entry previously looked successful (status flips to "Void" in the UI) while silently leaving its numbers baked into every report - a plain Void button covering POSTED entries would have been actively misleading.

**Fix (confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion`, chose the recommended option):** Void is now correctly scoped to DRAFT entries only, matching standard accounting practice - correcting an already-posted entry requires a reversing entry, not erasing it.
- Backend: `journalEntryService.voidJournalEntry` now throws a 400 (`"...is already posted to the ledger and cannot be voided. Record a reversing journal entry instead."`) if called on a POSTED entry - closing the gap at the API level, not just hiding the button.
- Frontend: `useJournals.ts` gained `voidJournal()`; `JournalList.tsx` shows a "Void" button (with a confirmation prompt) only on rows with `status === 'Draft'`. Also fixed the status badge to show `danger` (red) for `Void` instead of falling through to the same `warning` color as `Draft`. `JournalStatus` type (`types/accounting.ts`) was missing `'Void'` entirely - added.
- A real reversing-entry feature for correcting POSTED entries is a natural follow-up, not built here.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `journalEntries.test.ts` case: create + post an entry, attempt to void it, confirm 400 with the correct message and that the entry's status is still `POSTED` afterward (not silently flipped). Full backend suite run: 287/287 passing.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/journalEntryService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/journalEntries.test.ts`, `frontend/src/hooks/useJournals.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/journals/JournalList.tsx`, `frontend/src/types/accounting.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Stock Take: Printable Blind-Count Sheets + Reconciliation

**What:** Added a real physical inventory-count workflow: print a per-warehouse stock sheet to count against on paper, then reconcile the counted numbers against system quantities. Requested directly by the user; two design decisions confirmed with them up front: (1) the printed sheet is a **blind count** - it never shows the system's current quantity, only SKU/name/unit and a blank line to write the count on, so a count isn't unconsciously biased toward matching what the app already expects; (2) counted quantities can be entered either via an on-screen editable table or by uploading a filled-in CSV, converging on the same reconciliation flow.

**Backend:**
- Extracted the inline add/remove/set upsert logic previously living directly inside `POST /inventory/adjustments` into a new shared `backend/src/services/stockAdjustmentService.ts` (`applyStockAdjustment`), so the new batch reconciliation route doesn't duplicate the same branching (including the guarded concurrency check on `remove`). Purely a refactor - existing `inventory.test.ts`/`warehousePermissions.test.ts` assertions (`previousQty`/`newQty`/`delta`/error messages) verified unchanged.
- New `generateStockTakeSheetPdf` + a small reusable `drawSimpleTable` helper in `pdfGenerationService.ts` (page-break-aware, redraws the header row on overflow), following the same left-margin-anchoring discipline as the existing Quick Start Guide generator.
- New `GET /inventory/warehouses/:id/stock-sheet.pdf` - scoped via the existing `assertWarehouseAccess` (same pattern as `/adjustments`/`/transfers`), streams the generated PDF as a download.
- New `POST /inventory/stock-take` (`Accountant`+ role) - takes `{warehouseId, counts:[{itemId, countedQty}]}`, validates every counted quantity is a non-negative integer up front (rejects the whole batch on a malformed row, since this is the app's own parser, not an independent bulk-create), then for each item calls `applyStockAdjustment(mode:'set')` only where the count differs from the system quantity (matching items are left untouched). All adjustments in a batch share a generated `stockTakeRef` embedded in their `reason` string for grouping (no new column/migration needed - `StockAdjustment` already had every field this needed), plus one `STOCK_TAKE.RECONCILED` `AuditLog` summary row per batch.

**Frontend** (`WarehouseManagement.tsx`): "Print Stock Sheet" and "Stock Take" buttons added to each warehouse card. Print uses the existing blob-download pattern (already used twice elsewhere for CSV exports) so the JWT/tenant headers are correctly attached. The Stock Take modal has a Table/CSV entry step (reusing the existing Bulk Add modal's tab pattern and CSV template/upload conventions) followed by a read-only variance preview step - system quantities are only revealed at this final review step, after every row is already locked in, so the blind-count principle holds through data entry too, not just the printed sheet.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `backend/src/tests/stockTake.test.ts` (5 tests): the PDF route returns a real `%PDF-`-signed document whose extracted text contains both item SKUs but never the real system quantities; the reconciliation route creates a `StockAdjustment` only for the variant item and leaves the matching item untouched (verified via `GET /adjustments` counts) plus one `STOCK_TAKE.RECONCILED` audit row; a malformed counted quantity rejects the whole batch (400); a Shop Manager without access to the target warehouse gets 403 from both new routes. Full backend suite run twice in a row against real Postgres/Redis: 286/286 both times. Manually verified end-to-end against a real running backend (not just mocked tests): onboarded a real tenant, created a warehouse with two items (system quantities 33 and 8), downloaded the real stock-sheet PDF and confirmed via `file`/text-extraction that it's a genuine 2-item PDF with neither quantity visible anywhere, then posted a real reconciliation (counted 30 and 8) and confirmed via `GET /inventory/items` that only the variant item's quantity actually changed (33→30) and the matching item was left untouched.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/stockAdjustmentService.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/pdfGenerationService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/inventory.ts`, `backend/src/tests/stockTake.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/inventory/WarehouseManagement.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Security Audit & Hardening Pass

**What:** A full security audit (auth/secrets, injection surfaces, dependencies/infra) turned up a real backdoor and several genuine gaps, fixed in this pass:

**Critical:**
1. **Universal SMS verification bypass** (`auth.ts`) - `POST /auth/verify` accepted `smsCode: "1234"` as valid for ANY account, unconditionally in all environments. Anyone could verify/activate any account's phone with this literal string. Removed entirely (no legitimate use found - not gated on `NODE_ENV`, no test depended on it). While here, hardened the email-token and SMS-code comparisons to use `crypto.timingSafeEqual` instead of `===`, matching the rest of the codebase's secret-comparison convention.
2. **Hardcoded third-party SMS gateway credentials** (`smsService.ts`) - real-looking Android SMS gateway credentials (`U8LXKB` / `db8lp7qnvc3qkv` / a device ID) were baked in as fallback values, same anti-pattern as the Gmail credential fixed earlier this session, and previously undocumented in `.env.example`. Removed the fallback; the gateway now fails cleanly with a `GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED` audit entry instead of silently authenticating against a shared account. **These credentials must be rotated/revoked at the sms-gate.app account independent of this code change** - same "treat as compromised" reasoning as the earlier Gmail fix.

**Real gaps closed:**
3. **No token revocation/logout mechanism at all** - JWTs were purely client-discarded; a stolen token stayed valid for its full 24h life with no server-side kill switch. Added a minimal Redis-backed denylist (`tokenRevocationService.ts`, keyed by the same token-hash `verifyJwtToken` already used for its LRU cache, TTL = remaining token lifetime so it self-expires), a new `POST /auth/logout`, and a targeted `evictFromJwtCache()` so a revoked token can't keep being served from the in-memory cache either. `verifyJwtToken` is now `async` (checks the denylist on both the cache-hit and freshly-verified paths) - a real, deliberate signature change; all 4 call sites updated. Revocation checks fail open on a Redis outage (logged loudly), matching this codebase's established caching philosophy - the alternative (hard-failing all auth on a Redis blip) was judged worse.
4. **Weak server-side password policy** - only `length < 6` was enforced at all three password-creation sites (register, accept-invitation, tenant onboarding), independently duplicated. New shared `passwordPolicy.ts` (`validatePasswordStrength`): 8+ characters, at least one letter and one number - deliberately modest, since PBKDF2-SHA512/100k-iteration hashing already provides real brute-force resistance; this just closes the "password is `a`" gap. Frontend `AcceptInvitation.tsx`'s matching client-side check updated so users aren't shown false "valid" at the old 6-character bar.
5. **Timing-unsafe admin passcode comparison** (`broadcastService.ts`) - `verifyPasscode` used plain `===`, inconsistent with the timing-safe comparisons used everywhere else in this codebase for secrets (JWT signatures, password hashes). Now uses `crypto.timingSafeEqual` with a length check first. Gates `GET /tenants`, `/admin/audit-logs`, and admin broadcast send - single centralized fix.
6. **Unescaped user input in outbound HTML emails** - tenant/user names (only `.trim()`ed, never character-filtered) were interpolated directly into HTML email templates in `EmailService.ts` and `broadcastService.ts`. New small `htmlEscape.ts` helper applied at all 6 interpolation points. Low practical severity (recipient is the tenant's own admin; modern email clients sanitize aggressively) but genuinely unescaped.

**Small/safe hardening, bundled in:**
7. `app.set('trust proxy', ...)` - now opt-in via a new `TRUST_PROXY` env var (unset by default, preserving current behavior exactly). Inert today (nothing sits in front of this API yet), but avoids a real "every user shares one rate-limit bucket" bug the moment the existing `Dockerfile`/`nginx.conf` setup goes live behind a reverse proxy.
8. `helmet()` now sets an explicit restrictive CSP (`default-src 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`) instead of relying on defaults - safe because this backend is a pure JSON API (the React SPA is a separate deployment, never served from here), so there's no legitimate need for it to ever load scripts/styles/frames.

**Explicitly deferred (documented, not fixed here) - see TASKS.md for reasoning:** no schema validation library (zod/joi) across routes - large mechanical effort, not a critical vuln since Prisma's parameterized queries and `sanitizeSchemaName()` already prevent real SQL injection (confirmed via full audit of all raw-SQL call sites); `react-router`/`react-router-dom`'s known high-severity advisory - needs a breaking major-version bump, its own scoped upgrade; OpenTelemetry/dev-tooling dependency findings (27 high, 36 moderate) - build-time/observability only, tracked via existing CI `npm audit`; narrow tenant-isolation test coverage (only the low-level search_path mechanism is tested, not HTTP-level cross-tenant leakage) - a coverage-expansion task, not a fix.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` (backend) and `tsc -b` (frontend) both clean. New `tokenRevocation.test.ts` (3 tests: logout invalidates that token; a different user's token is unaffected; a cache-warmed token is still rejected after revocation), `htmlEscape.test.ts` (4 tests), extended `verifiedRegistration.test.ts` (bypass code rejected), `auth.test.ts` (weak password rejected), `warehousePermissions.test.ts` (weak password rejected at accept-invitation), `tenantOnboarding.test.ts` (weak password rejected at onboarding, message updated), `adminBroadcastEncrypted.test.ts` (same-length-wrong-value passcode rejected). Full backend suite run twice in a row: 281/281 both times.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/routes/auth.ts`, `backend/src/services/{smsService,broadcastService,EmailService,tenantService}.ts`, `backend/src/utils/{jwt,passwordPolicy,htmlEscape}.ts`, `backend/src/services/tokenRevocationService.ts` (new), `backend/src/middleware/authMiddleware.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/.env.example`, `frontend/src/pages/auth/AcceptInvitation.tsx`, plus the test files listed above.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Real Audit Trail Coverage (Journal Entries, Invoices/Bills, Auth, Accounts, Inventory, Team Access) + Two Real Bugs Found Along the Way

**What:** The audit trail was previously almost decorative: only 3 action types were ever written across the whole backend (`GATEWAY_OFFLINE`, `SYSTEM_BROADCAST`, `EMAIL_SENT`) - none of the real business mutations (journal entries, invoices, bills, chart of accounts, inventory, team/role changes, login) ever wrote an audit entry, and the `userId`/`userEmail`/`ipAddress` schema columns existed but were never populated by any call site.
**Fix:** Added `AuditLog.changes Json?` (structured before/after diff, e.g. `{status: {from: 'DRAFT', to: 'POSTED'}}`, alongside the existing free-text `details`). New shared `backend/src/services/auditLogService.ts` (`recordAuditLog`, `actorFromRequest`, `diffFields`) is now the single write path, used by the 3 pre-existing sites (refactored, not behavior-changed) plus real coverage added across:
- **Journal entries**: create/post/void (`journalEntryService.ts`)
- **Invoices/bills**: payment (`invoices.ts`/`bills.ts` `/pay`)
- **Auth**: login success/failure (all 3 failure reasons collapsed into one `AUTH.LOGIN_FAILED` action so the action name itself never leaks which branch), invitation accepted
- **Team management**: invitation sent, warehouse/shop access reassignment (`tenants.ts`)
- **Chart of accounts**: create/update/delete (`accountService.ts`)
- **Inventory**: stock adjustments, transfers, item/warehouse creation (`inventory.ts`)

Actor identity (`userId`/`userEmail`/`ipAddress`) is now genuinely populated on every new write, sourced from `req.user`/`req.ip` at the route layer and threaded into services via an optional trailing `actor` parameter.

**Two real bugs found and fixed along the way (not part of the original ask, but directly encountered while building/testing this):**
1. **Hardcoded Gmail credentials in source** (`backend/src/services/EmailService.ts`): `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` had a real Gmail address and what appears to be a live Gmail App Password baked in as literal fallback values, undocumented in `.env.example`, meaning this credential has been sitting in git history. Removed the fallback (`getTransporter()` now throws a clear "not configured" error caught by the existing retry/failure path instead of silently using the hardcoded account); added `EMAIL_USER`/`EMAIL_PASS` to `.env.example`. **The exposed credential must be rotated in Google Account settings independent of this code change** - a hardcoded secret that reached a pushed repo has to be treated as compromised.
2. **Stale Redis tenant cache never invalidated on tenant deletion** (`backend/src/repository/tenantRepository.ts`'s `deleteTenantBySlug`): `tenantCache.ts` already had `invalidateTenantCache()` helpers, but nothing ever called them on delete. This meant deleting a tenant left its Redis cache entries (id/slug/schema keys, 30-minute TTL) alive; if a new tenant were ever onboarded reusing the same slug within that window, requests would resolve to the *deleted* tenant's stale cached identity for any `tenantId`-filtered public-schema data (this was concretely reproduced via the new audit-log assertions: an entry's `tenantId` didn't match the freshly-onboarded tenant's real id). Fixed by invalidating all three cache keys (id/slug/schema) on delete.

**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. New `auditLogService.test.ts` (10 tests: write correctness, `changes` persistence, tenant-context fallback, non-throwing on DB failure, `actorFromRequest`, `diffFields`). Extended `journalEntries.test.ts`, `paymentPosting.test.ts`, `accounts.test.ts`, `auth.test.ts`, `inventory.test.ts`, `warehousePermissions.test.ts` with real assertions that a matching `AuditLog` row (correct action/entity/entityId/actor/changes) was actually written. Full backend suite run twice consecutively **without flushing Redis between runs** (270/270 both times) - the second run is the real regression test for the cache-invalidation fix, since the first run's version of this suite reproducibly failed on the second pass before that fix landed.
**Known limitation (explicitly out of scope, follow-up phase):** No UI changes - `frontend/src/pages/audit/AuditLogs.tsx` and `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s audit tab still show the same unfiltered, unexported table; the new `changes` field is captured but not yet rendered anywhere. `backend/src/app.ts` still has no `app.set('trust proxy', ...)`, so `req.ip`-derived `ipAddress` values (and rate limiting) are only accurate when not behind a reverse proxy - a separate, pre-existing gap noted but not fixed here.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260727165827_add_changes_to_audit_log/`, `backend/src/services/auditLogService.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/{smsService,broadcastService,EmailService,journalEntryService,accountService}.ts`, `backend/src/routes/{journalEntries,invoices,bills,auth,tenants,accounts,inventory}.ts`, `backend/src/repository/tenantRepository.ts`, `backend/.env.example`, `backend/src/tests/auditLogService.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/{journalEntries,paymentPosting,accounts,auth,inventory,warehousePermissions}.test.ts`.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Fixed Stale Shop List in Team Management's Invite/Manage-Access Modals

**What:** A user reported that after creating a shop ("Shop A") under Inventory, the "Invite Staff Member" modal on `/team` still showed "No shops/warehouses exist yet - create one under Inventory first," blocking assignment of Shop Manager/Cashier roles. Root cause: `TeamManagement.tsx`'s `fetchTeamData()` (which loads the `warehouses` list used by both the invite form and the "Manage Access" modal) only runs once, in a `useEffect` on initial mount. If a shop is created after that page has already loaded (the common flow: land on Team Management, notice no shops, go create one under Inventory, come back to the same still-mounted tab), the `warehouses` state stays stale at its original empty snapshot with no way to refresh short of a full page reload.
**Fix:** Both the "Invite Staff Member" button and the "Manage Access" button now re-fetch `GET /inventory/warehouses` and refresh the `warehouses` state at the moment their modal opens, so the shop list is always current without requiring a page reload.
**Verification:** `tsc -b` clean.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/pages/team/TeamManagement.tsx`.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Fixed Broken Staff Invitation Link ("Cannot GET /accept-invite")

**What:** A user reported that a worker's invitation email link produced "Cannot GET /accept-invite" — the literal default 404 message Express returns for an unrecognized route. Root cause: `POST /tenants/invite` (`backend/src/routes/tenants.ts:217`) built the invite link from the *backend API's own request host* (`` `${req.protocol}://${req.get('host')}/accept-invite?token=...` ``) instead of the frontend's URL. `/accept-invite` only exists as a React Router route in the frontend SPA (`frontend/src/App.tsx:133`), not as a backend Express route, so any link built from the backend's own host always 404s in the backend, regardless of environment.
**Fix:** Changed the link to use `process.env.APP_URL` (falling back to `http://localhost:5173`), the same convention already used for the email-verification link (`EmailService.ts`) and the Quick Start Guide PDF's links (`pdfGenerationService.ts`), so it correctly points at the frontend.
**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. `warehousePermissions.test.ts` and `allEndpoints.test.ts` (which exercise the invite flow) pass, 27/27.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `STATUS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Fixed CI-Only Tenant Provisioning Failure (Connection-Pool Race in Migration Runner)

**What:** PR #15's CI (`backend` check) failed reproducibly with `Raw query failed. Code: 42804 ... Key columns "journal_entry_id" and "id" are of incompatible types: uuid and text` inside `onboardTenant()`, but the same suite passed 260/260 twice locally. Root-caused to a real, pre-existing connection-pool race in `runMigrationsForSchema` (`backend/src/database/tenantMigrationRunner.ts`): `SET search_path TO "<tenant_schema>", public;` and the subsequent unqualified DDL statements (`CREATE TABLE journal_entries ...`, etc.) were each dispatched as separate, non-transactional `$executeRawUnsafe` calls. `SET search_path` is connection-scoped in Postgres, but Prisma's connection pool is free to route each call to a different pooled connection — under CI's higher concurrency this happened often enough to reliably reproduce, while locally (lower pool contention) it usually didn't. When the DDL statements landed on a connection still on the default `public` search_path, `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS journal_entries` silently no-op'd against the *pre-existing* Prisma-managed `public.journal_entries` table (whose `id` column is `TEXT`, from `String @id @default(uuid())` with no `@db.Uuid`), so the next statement's `journal_entry_lines.journal_entry_id UUID REFERENCES journal_entries(id)` FK failed with the exact uuid/text mismatch seen in CI. Not introduced by this branch's 3 commits (none touch this file); it's a latent bug that could have hit any tenant onboarding under enough concurrent DB load, in CI or production.
**Fix:** Wrapped the `SET search_path` + tracking-table creation + full migration loop + `SET search_path TO public` reset in a single `prismaClient.$transaction(async (tx) => {...}, { timeout: 30000, maxWait: 10000 })`, which pins every statement to one physical connection for the duration, guaranteeing the search_path actually applies to the DDL that depends on it. Updated the two unit tests that hand-roll a mock Prisma client (`accountingSchema.test.ts`, `tenantMigrationRunner.test.ts`) to mock `$transaction` as `(fn) => fn(mockPrisma)` so existing assertions on `$executeRawUnsafe` calls keep working.
**Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full backend suite (`npm test`) run twice in a row: 260/260 both times, including `tenantSchemaSwitching.test.ts` (which exercises `ensureTenantSchemaMigrated` against a real Postgres connection, not a mock) confirming the fix works against the real database, not just the mocked unit tests.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/database/tenantMigrationRunner.ts`, `backend/src/tests/accountingSchema.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/tenantMigrationRunner.test.ts`, `STATUS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Real Point-of-Sale Screen (Closes the "No Frontend for Cash Till" Gap)

**What:** Closes the gap found earlier the same day: the Cash Till backend (`/tills/open`, `/tills/sales`, `/tills/close`, `/tills/closeouts`) was fully built, tested, and now permission-scoped (Shop Manager/Cashier), but had no frontend at all - nobody could actually record a real sale. New `frontend/src/pages/pos/PointOfSale.tsx`, routed at `/pos` and linked from the Sidebar's "Sales & Purchases" section.
1. **Shop selector** - auto-selects the user's only accessible warehouse; shows a dropdown if they have more than one (respects the location-scoping just shipped).
2. **Open Till**: prompts for the opening cash float when no till is currently open for the selected shop.
3. **Record a Sale**: product dropdown (only items with stock at this shop), quantity, cash given - computes total and change live, validates against available stock and insufficient cash before submitting, shows a receipt confirmation and a running list of the session's receipts after each sale.
4. **Close Till**: shows opening cash + cash sales = expected drawer total, takes the actual counted cash, and displays the resulting Balanced/Over/Short reconciliation - reusing the exact backend endpoint and math that already existed.
**A real bug found and fixed during verification, not just typechecked**: the closeout summary initially showed "Short by GH₵0.00" for an exactly-balanced till. Root cause: `DailyCloseoutReport.discrepancy` is a Prisma `Decimal` field, which serializes to JSON as a string (e.g. `"0.00"`); the component compared it with strict equality (`discrepancy === 0`), which is always false for a string. Fixed by coercing to `Number(...)` once before comparing. Caught by driving the full flow in a real browser against the real backend (Playwright), not by unit tests.
**Verification:** Backend unchanged, so the full suite (260/260) was re-run once to confirm the baseline is unaffected by this frontend-only change; frontend `tsc -b` clean. End-to-end live verification: opened a real till, sold 3 units at GH₵5 with GH₵20 given (confirmed exact GH₵15.00 total / GH₵5.00 change), closed the till with the exact expected cash counted, and confirmed "Till Balanced" displays correctly after the fix.
**Known limitation (schema, not this change)**: `CashSale` doesn't store which item/quantity was sold (only the receipt total) - the "Today's Receipts" list shows receipt number, timestamp, and amount, not a line-item description. Fixing that would require a schema change to `CashSale`, out of scope for wiring up the existing backend's frontend.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/src/pages/pos/PointOfSale.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Real Location-Scoped Permissions (Shop Manager / Cashier)

**What:** Closes the gap found while explaining how "assigning a shop manager" actually worked: `User.role` was a free-text string, and any custom title (including the platform's own suggested "Shop Manager") fell through `rbacMiddleware.ts`'s catch-all to full company-wide access - no way to restrict anyone to a single shop. Researched how comparable systems (Square, Lightspeed Retail, Shopify POS, QuickBooks Online, Zoho Books, Odoo, TallyPrime) solve this - the consistent pattern across all of them is two independent axes: a closed role vocabulary (*what* actions) plus a separate location assignment (*where* they apply), never conflated. Implemented that model, scoped specifically to Inventory/Warehouses/Cash-Till (the parts of this schema that are actually warehouse-attributable - general ledger accounts have no warehouse concept, so company-wide financial reports are explicitly out of scope for this phase, not silently left leaky).
1. **New `WarehouseAccess` join table** (`backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, migration `20260727144217_add_warehouse_access_permissions`): many-to-many between `User` and `Warehouse`. `Invitation` gets a `warehouseIds String[]` column to carry the selected shops from invite time through to acceptance.
2. **`backend/src/services/warehouseAccessService.ts`** (new): `CLOSED_ROLES` (`Admin`, `Accountant`, `Auditor`, `Viewer`, `Shop Manager`, `Cashier`), `assertWarehouseAccess()` (throws 403 only for the two new location-scoped roles - Admin/Accountant/Auditor/Viewer and any pre-existing legacy free-text role are completely unaffected, so this is purely additive with zero regression risk to existing behavior/tests), and `getAccessibleWarehouseIds()` for filtering list endpoints.
3. **Enforcement** in `inventory.ts` (warehouses/items list filtering, item creation, bulk-create, transfers - both sides, adjustments) and `cashTill.ts` (current/open/sales/close, closeouts list) - a Shop Manager or Cashier now only sees and can act on their assigned warehouse(s); everyone else's access is unchanged.
4. **Invite flow**: `POST /tenants/invite` now validates `role` against the closed set and requires at least one `warehouseIds` entry for a location-scoped role; `POST /auth/accept-invitation` creates the `WarehouseAccess` rows on acceptance. New `PUT /tenants/members/:id/warehouse-access` (Admin-only) lets an admin change an existing member's shop assignment after the fact.
5. **Frontend** (`TeamManagement.tsx`): the free-text role input is now a fixed dropdown; selecting "Shop Manager" or "Cashier" reveals a required shop checklist. The members table gets a new "Shop Access" column (shows assigned shop names, or "All shops" for company-wide roles) and a "Manage Access" button per scoped member.
**Verification:** New `backend/src/tests/warehousePermissions.test.ts` (9 tests) - grants access on invite acceptance, filters warehouse/item lists correctly, blocks adjustments/transfers/till-open outside the assigned shop with a clear 403, confirms Admin/Accountant remain completely unrestricted (regression check), and confirms an admin re-assigning a member's shops takes effect immediately. Updated `allEndpoints.test.ts`'s stale "accepts custom worker job titles" test to reflect the new closed-role reality. Full backend suite: 260/260 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean. Also verified live in a real browser against the real backend (Playwright): the role dropdown, the conditional shop checklist, the required-shop validation error, and the invite-success flow all confirmed working exactly as built.
**Known scope boundary (explicitly not solved here)**: this scoping applies to Inventory/Warehouses/Cash-Till only. The Dashboard's company-wide Total Revenue/Expenses/Net Profit figures are unaffected, because `JournalEntry`/`Ledger`/`Account` have no warehouse concept anywhere in this schema - a location-scoped Shop Manager can still see full company financial totals on the Dashboard. Fixing that would require attributing general-ledger postings to a warehouse, a materially larger schema change out of scope for this phase.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260727144217_add_warehouse_access_permissions/` (new), `backend/src/services/warehouseAccessService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/inventory.ts`, `backend/src/routes/cashTill.ts`, `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `backend/src/routes/auth.ts`, `backend/src/tests/warehousePermissions.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/allEndpoints.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/team/TeamManagement.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Real Stock Adjustments (Restock Existing Items + Fix Mistaken Entries)

**What:** Closes the gap flagged in the previous entry: once an inventory item existed, there was no way to add more stock to it (only a one-time initial quantity at creation, or transfers between warehouses that already had stock). The user then asked specifically how to fix it when someone mistakenly types in more quantity than they should - the same underlying gap from the other direction (a correction is just an adjustment that happens to go down instead of up).
1. **New `StockAdjustment` model** (`backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, migration `20260727112356_add_stock_adjustments`): a permanent, queryable audit record - previous quantity, new quantity, delta, mode, a required reason, who made the change, and when. Mirrors the existing `StockTransfer` model's role as a dedicated audit trail rather than a generic log entry, since adjustment history is data users will want to filter and review per item.
2. **New `POST /api/v1/inventory/adjustments`** (`backend/src/routes/inventory.ts`): a single endpoint with three modes covering every real scenario - `add` (restock: increment by a quantity, e.g. a new supplier delivery), `remove` (subtract a quantity, guarded so it can never go negative - "Cannot remove 999 units - only 12 currently on hand"), and `set` (declare the true, physically-counted total outright - the direct fix for "I typed 120 instead of 12"). A reason is mandatory on every call; `add`/`remove` use the same atomic guarded-update pattern already established for transfers and till sales (no lost-update races), while `set` is intentionally an authoritative overwrite since a manual recount is meant to supersede whatever was there. New `GET /api/v1/inventory/adjustments` lists the history, filterable by item/warehouse.
3. **Frontend** (`frontend/src/pages/inventory/WarehouseManagement.tsx`): a new "Restock / Adjust Stock" modal (Add / Remove / Set Exact Count, with the current on-hand quantity shown for context) reachable both from a global header button and a per-item "Adjust" quick-action next to each shop's existing "Transfer" badge. A new "Adjustment History" modal lists every past adjustment (when, product, warehouse, delta, reason, who) so corrections stay transparent and traceable.
**Verification:** New tests in `backend/src/tests/inventory.test.ts` covering all three modes, including the exact scenario that prompted this (`mode='set'` correcting 50 → 12 with a `-38` delta), the over-removal guard, the required-reason validation, and history retrieval. Full backend suite: 249/249 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260727112356_add_stock_adjustments/` (new), `backend/src/routes/inventory.ts`, `backend/src/tests/inventory.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/inventory/WarehouseManagement.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-27] - Bulk Product Import (Quick-Add Table + CSV)

**What:** The user asked how stock gets into a warehouse/shop, which surfaced a real gap: once an item exists, there was no way to add more stock to it (only a one-time initial quantity at item creation, or transfers between warehouses that already have stock). They then asked for a faster way to add many products at once instead of the one-at-a-time "Add Product" modal.
1. **New `POST /api/v1/inventory/items/bulk`** (`backend/src/routes/inventory.ts`): accepts an array of up to 500 item rows and creates each independently (not one all-or-nothing transaction) - a single bad row (e.g. a duplicate SKU) is reported in a `failed` array with a friendly reason instead of discarding every other correctly-typed row in the batch. Auto-generates a unique SKU per row when omitted (de-duplicated within the batch), validates any `initialWarehouseId` belongs to the tenant, and reuses the exact same item/stock-seeding logic as the existing single-item `POST /items` route.
2. **Frontend** (`frontend/src/pages/inventory/WarehouseManagement.tsx`): new "Bulk Add Products" modal with two tabs - a spreadsheet-style **Quick-Add Table** (add/remove rows inline, warehouse picked from a dropdown) and a **CSV Import** tab (upload a CSV with a downloadable template; parsed client-side via `papaparse`; the CSV's `warehouseName` column is resolved against the tenant's actual warehouse names before submitting, since a CSV can't reference internal database IDs). Both paths funnel into the same rows-table UI and the same bulk-create call. After submission, a results summary shows how many succeeded and lists exactly which rows failed and why, and only the failed rows are kept in the editor so the user isn't stuck retyping everything that already succeeded.
**Verification:** New tests in `backend/src/tests/inventory.test.ts`: bulk-creates multiple valid rows and confirms initial stock is actually seeded in the named warehouse, confirms a duplicate-SKU row is reported as a per-row failure while the other valid row still succeeds, and rejects an empty items array. Full backend suite: 244/244 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean.
**Known follow-up (flagged, not built in this change)**: the underlying "can't add more stock to an existing item after creation" gap that started this conversation is still real - bulk import only makes *creating new items* faster, it doesn't add a general "restock an existing item" action. That remains a separate, not-yet-built feature.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/routes/inventory.ts`, `backend/src/tests/inventory.test.ts`, `frontend/package.json` (added `papaparse` + `@types/papaparse`), `frontend/src/pages/inventory/WarehouseManagement.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-26] - Fixed Real Layout Bug + Missing Features in the Quick Start Guide PDF

**What:** The user registered a real business and inspected the actual "Quick Start Guide" PDF their account received by email, and found two real problems: (1) a genuine layout bug - bullet points progressively indented further right down the page ("staircase" effect) and body text was visibly clipped at the right page edge mid-word (e.g. "journa|entry", "summari|automatically"), and page 2 was almost entirely blank; (2) the feature list only covered the original day-1 module set and was missing every module built later this session (Tax Rates, Fiscal Periods & Budgets, Recurring Transactions, Approval Workflows, Multi-Currency).
1. **Root cause of the layout bug** (`backend/src/services/pdfGenerationService.ts`'s `addSectionHeading`/`addBullet` helpers): both read `doc.x` to find the left edge to draw from, but `doc.x` is pdfkit's mutable text cursor, which had already drifted rightward from the *previous* bullet's indented body-text call (drawn at `startX + 16`). Each subsequent bullet then started 16pt further right than the last, compounding down the page - and because the wrap width was still computed as if starting from the true left margin, the drifted, further-right text ran past the actual right page edge, clipping mid-word. **Fix:** both helpers now anchor to `doc.page.margins.left` explicitly on every call and reset `doc.x` back to it afterward, instead of trusting the carried-over cursor position.
2. **Missing features fixed**: the "What You Can Do Today" section was split into "Core Day-to-Day Features" (the original 4: bookkeeping, invoicing/bills, inventory, cash till) and a new "Automation & Compliance Features" section covering every module actually shipped: Tax Rates, Fiscal Periods & Budgets, Recurring Transactions, Approval Workflows, Multi-Currency, plus Bank Reconciliation, Automated Executive Reports, AI-Assisted Categorization, and Custom Fields (all of which existed in the code but weren't previously mentioned in the guide). Also added a one-line tip pointing to the new PWA "Install App" section on the homepage.
**Verification:** Regenerated the PDF locally and visually inspected both pages directly (not just unit-tested) - confirmed every bullet now aligns at the same left margin with zero clipped text, and page 2 is properly filled with content instead of nearly blank. Extended `backend/src/tests/pdfGenerationService.test.ts` to assert the new feature-section text (Tax rates, Fiscal periods, Recurring transactions, Approval workflows, Multi-currency support, Custom fields) is actually present in the generated PDF. Full backend suite: 241/241 passing, twice in a row.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/services/pdfGenerationService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/pdfGenerationService.test.ts`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.
