## [Date: 2026-07-26] - Real Privacy Policy + Legal/Terms Gaps Fixed (Competitor-Informed)

**What:** While discussing a possible product rename, the user asked for a review of how comparable accounting/ERP companies (Finza, Alpha ERP, AccountBase Partners, Accounting Seed, TallyPrime) structure their Terms of Service, Privacy Policy, and business-onboarding requirements, to find gaps in our own legal setup. A background research agent fetched each company's real public legal pages and cross-checked our own codebase directly. Confirmed findings: `Register.tsx`'s consent checkbox promised a "Privacy Policy" that did not exist anywhere in the repo (only `TERMS_AND_CONDITIONS.md`, `SLA.md`, `CUSTOMIZATION_POLICY.md` existed, and `legal.ts`'s `ALLOWED_POLICIES` had no entry for it); `TERMS_AND_CONDITIONS.md` §11 had a literal unfilled `[Your Jurisdiction]` placeholder; no financial-data retention period was stated anywhere (every competitor reviewed states one, tied to their local tax authority); no mention of Ghana's Data Protection Commission (DPC), a real registration obligation under the Data Protection Act 2012 (Act 843), not just documentation polish; and termination language was vague ("a reasonable period" to export data, no defined days) - the weakest point across nearly every competitor too.
1. **New `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`**: real content grounded in what the platform actually collects/does today - the real `OnboardTenantDTO`/`Register.tsx` fields, a named subprocessor table (Mono for bank feeds, Arkesel/mNotify for SMS, Google/Gmail for email, exchangerate-api.com for FX rates - each explicitly caveated as active only when its env var is configured), a 7-year financial-record retention statement, and a concrete 30-day export / 90-day-total deletion window on termination. Per the user's explicit answers to clarifying questions (asked to avoid fabricating legal facts, per this project's "No Mock Data Ever" rule): the DPC section states registration is **"in progress,"** never complete; the contact email/entity name are clearly marked placeholders (`privacy@accountgo.com`) for the user to replace with real registered business details before production launch.
2. **`docs/TERMS_AND_CONDITIONS.md`**: §11 governing law filled in as "the Republic of Ghana" (previously a literal `[Your Jurisdiction]` placeholder); §4 and §9 updated to reference the new Privacy Policy and use the same concrete 30/90-day export/deletion window instead of vague language, so both documents agree with each other.
3. **Backend**: `backend/src/routes/legal.ts`'s `ALLOWED_POLICIES` map gets a `privacy-policy` entry - no other route logic changes needed, the existing generic file-read/cache logic already handles any registered policy.
4. **Frontend**: `Register.tsx`'s legal modal previously only ever fetched Terms and Conditions despite its label claiming "& Privacy Policy" - it's now a real two-tab modal (Terms / Privacy Policy) that fetches and displays both real documents. `LandingPage.tsx`'s existing "Legal & Policy Showcase" section gets a 4th "Privacy Policy" tab, matching the existing terms/sla/tier tabs' style.
**Scope note (deliberately unchanged):** none of the five competitors reviewed collect business registration numbers, tax IDs, or KYC documents at signup - even the most tax/IRS-adjacent one (AccountBase Partners) defers that to a private later stage. `Register.tsx`'s onboarding fields were therefore left as-is; this was purely a legal-documentation fix, not an onboarding-flow change.
**Verification:** New test in `backend/src/tests/legalAndEnforcement.test.ts` confirms `GET /api/legal/privacy-policy` returns 200 with real content. Full backend suite: 241/241 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean.
**Known follow-up (explicitly not resolved by this change, flagged for the user directly)**: actual Ghana DPC registration and swapping the placeholder legal entity name/contact email for the real registered business details are real-world compliance/business actions outside of code - this change documents the honest current state, it does not complete the registration itself.
**Files Affected:** `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md` (new), `docs/TERMS_AND_CONDITIONS.md`, `backend/src/routes/legal.ts`, `backend/src/tests/legalAndEnforcement.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/auth/Register.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/landing/LandingPage.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Web App Now Installable (Real PWA) - Step One of Web → Software → Mobile Roadmap

**What:** The user asked for businesses to be able to "download" AccountGo from the public landing page, explicitly framing this as step one of a longer roadmap (web app now → a native desktop "software version" later → native mobile apps later, both deferred). Clarified via `AskUserQuestion` that "download" means a real Progressive Web App (PWA) install experience - the browser's native install prompt, making the existing React app installable with zero new codebase - not a rewrite, not an Electron app yet. There was no PWA scaffolding anywhere in the repo before this (no manifest, no service worker, no raster app icons).
1. **App icons**: generated real PNG icons (192x192, 512x512, a maskable 512x512 with Android adaptive-icon safe-zone padding, and an Apple touch icon) from the existing `favicon.svg` brand mark, rasterized via the pre-installed Playwright/Chromium (`page.screenshot()`) rather than adding a new image-processing dependency.
2. **`vite-plugin-pwa`** (new devDependency) added to `frontend/vite.config.ts` with `registerType: 'autoUpdate'`, a full manifest (name/short_name/description/theme_color/background_color/display:standalone/icons), and Workbox precache of only the static app shell. **Critical correctness constraint for an accounting app**: `/api/**` is never cached under any code path - no `runtimeCaching` rule matches it, and `navigateFallbackDenylist: [/^\/api\//]` blocks the SPA navigation fallback from intercepting it either, so financial data is always fetched live, never served stale. `frontend/index.html` got the Apple-specific meta tags (`apple-mobile-web-app-capable`/`-title`, `apple-touch-icon`) that iOS Safari needs, since it has no `beforeinstallprompt` API or manifest-driven install support.
3. **`useInstallPrompt` hook** (new, `frontend/src/hooks/useInstallPrompt.ts`): listens for `beforeinstallprompt` (Chromium-only), stores the deferred event, exposes `{ isInstallable, isInstalled, promptInstall() }`; detects an already-installed/standalone session via `display-mode: standalone` (plus iOS's non-standard `navigator.standalone`).
4. **Landing page "Get the App" section** (`LandingPage.tsx`, between "Onboarding" and "Pricing"): shows a real "Install App" button (Chromium/Android/desktop), manual "Add to Home Screen" steps on iOS Safari (no programmatic API exists there), and hides entirely once the app is already running standalone. Copy is honest that this installs the same web app for convenient access - it does not imply a separate desktop product, since that is explicitly future work.
**Verification:** `tsc -b` clean. `npm run build` succeeds, producing `dist/manifest.webmanifest`, `dist/sw.js`, and the Workbox runtime with 10 precached entries. Ran `npm run preview` and drove it with Playwright/Chromium: confirmed the manifest resolves (200) with the correct name/theme_color/display/icons, the service worker registers and reaches `activated` state with no relevant console errors, and - the critical check - a request to `/api/v1/health` reported `fromServiceWorker: false`, proving API calls always hit the network and are never served from the service worker cache.
**Known limitation (explicitly not solved here, flagged for whoever sets up hosting)**: this repo has no production hosting/deploy path for the built frontend at all today - no frontend Dockerfile, no static-file server config, no CI deploy step. Real end-to-end installability also depends on being served over HTTPS with correct cache-control headers on `sw.js`/`manifest.webmanifest`; that can only be fully verified once real hosting exists. This phase built and locally verified the correct PWA scaffolding; it does not claim a live, publicly installable deployment.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/public/pwa-192x192.png` (new), `frontend/public/pwa-512x512.png` (new), `frontend/public/maskable-icon-512x512.png` (new), `frontend/public/apple-touch-icon.png` (new), `frontend/package.json`, `frontend/vite.config.ts`, `frontend/index.html`, `frontend/src/hooks/useInstallPrompt.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/landing/LandingPage.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Phase 6 (Final) of New-Modules Plan: Real Platform-Wide Audit Log View

**What:** `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s "System Audit Logs" tab was static placeholder text - no data fetching at all - because the existing `/audit-logs` route is intentionally tenant-JWT-scoped (fixed earlier this session to enforce that scoping) and a platform-operator needs to see across every tenant, which no endpoint supported. This was the last of the six planned modules/fixes.
1. **New `GET /api/v1/admin/audit-logs`** (`backend/src/routes/adminAuditLogs.ts`): reuses the exact passcode-gate mechanism already used by `GET /api/v1/tenants` (`BroadcastService.verifyPasscode`, accepting the passcode via `?passcode=` or the `x-admin-passcode` header, no `authenticateJwt`/tenant context at all - a platform-operator function, not a tenant one). Since `AuditLog.tenantId` has no Prisma relation to `Tenant` (a bare shared column, like every other tenantId-column table in this schema), tenant name/slug are joined in application code (fetch the log page, collect distinct tenant ids, one `tenant.findMany`, map onto each row) rather than adding a new relation/migration just for this read - matching this codebase's established "plain columns, app-level joins" convention.
2. **Frontend**: `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s audit tab now fetches real data with the same three-state (`logs`/`error`/`isLoading`) pattern already used by the adjacent "Tenant Schemas & Tiers" tab (effect gated on `[isUnlocked, passcode]`), rendering Timestamp/Tenant/Action/Entity/User/Details columns - the same columns as the tenant-facing `AuditLogs.tsx` page plus a new Tenant column to distinguish rows across tenants.
**Verification:** New `backend/src/tests/adminAuditLogs.test.ts` (5 tests): rejects missing/wrong passcode, returns logs across multiple tenants with the correct tenant name/slug joined onto each row, accepts the passcode via the header as an alternative to the query param, pagination works, and confirms a tenant's own JWT alone grants no access (no passcode present). Full backend suite: 240/240 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Phase 5 of New-Modules Plan: Real Multi-Currency Conversion (Transaction-Time)

**What:** Multi-currency was previously cosmetic: `Tenant` had no `baseCurrency` field at all (the Settings UI's "Base Currency" save was silently dropped by `PUT /tenants/current`, and `useTenantSettings.ts`'s fetch hardcoded `baseCurrency: 'USD'` regardless of what the backend actually returned), and - the real bug - `invoices.ts`'s and `bills.ts`'s `/pay` handlers posted the raw native-currency `total`/`amount` straight into the ledger with zero FX conversion, even though the ledger is implicitly single-currency. A GHS-denominated invoice paid on a USD-ledger tenant would post the wrong number into the books with no error.
1. **`Tenant.baseCurrency`** (new field, defaults `"USD"`) - now genuinely settable at signup (`Register.tsx` gets a currency selector) and via Settings (fixed the silent-drop bug in both directions: `PUT /tenants/current` now persists it, `useTenantSettings.ts` now reads the real value instead of a hardcoded literal).
2. **`fxRateService.ts`** (new, mirrors `monoService.ts`'s pattern): real live rates from exchangerate-api.com, gated on `FX_RATE_API_KEY` - inert with a clear 503 until a real key is supplied (same "built now, credentials later" precedent as the Mono bank-feed integration). Same-currency conversions (the common case for single-currency tenants) never touch the network or require configuration at all - `convertAmount(amount, from, to)` returns instantly if `from === to`. **`fxRateCache.ts`** (new, mirrors `tenantCache.ts`): Redis-cached rates, 6-hour TTL. **`FxRateCronService`** (new, mirrors `ScheduledEmailCronService`): periodic background refresh, registered in `index.ts`, no-ops if FX isn't configured.
3. **The actual fix**: `Invoice`/`VendorBill` get a new `baseCurrencyAmount` column, computed via `convertAmount(...)` at creation time and stored alongside the native amount. The `/pay` handlers now post `baseCurrencyAmount` (falling back to the raw amount for pre-existing rows created before this field existed) to the ledger instead of the raw native-currency figure - this is the concrete bug fix. If a tenant creates a cross-currency invoice/bill without FX configured, creation now fails with a clear 503 rather than silently posting the wrong number later at payment time.
**Verification:** New `backend/src/tests/multiCurrency.test.ts` (7 tests): same-currency conversion needs no config/network; cross-currency throws a clear 503 when unconfigured; cross-currency conversion works with mocked live rates; a direct Redis round-trip proves the cache module itself is correct; a real end-to-end check confirms a EUR invoice on a USD-base tenant stores the converted `baseCurrencyAmount` and - critically - the ledger's revenue account balance after payment reflects the *converted* figure, not the raw EUR total. Full backend suite: 235/235 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean.
**A genuine bug found and fixed while writing the tests**: `fxRateCache.setRatesInCache` was originally fire-and-forget (matching `tenantCache.ts`'s convention for its own writes), but that meant a caller reading immediately after a live fetch could race the cache write and see a false miss. Made this specific write awaited (still swallowing/logging errors, never throwing) so read-after-write within the same request is reliable - `tenantCache.ts`'s own fire-and-forget writes are a different, lower-stakes case (a subsequent miss there just re-hits the DB) and were left as-is.
**Scope note**: `BankTransaction` was NOT given a `baseCurrencyAmount` field - confirmed `banking.ts`'s reconcile endpoint never posts to the ledger (it only matches/marks transactions), so there is no analogous currency bug there today. Also out of scope per this phase: a deeper cleanup of every frontend `formatCurrency`/`formatMoney` default-fallback hardcode (`JournalList.tsx`, `utils.ts`, etc.) - the investigation found these only affect *display defaults* when a record's own currency is somehow unavailable, not the actual ledger-posting correctness bug this phase targets.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260725072000_add_multi_currency_conversion_fields/` (new), `backend/src/services/fxRateService.ts` (new), `backend/src/cache/fxRateCache.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/fxRateCronService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/routes/bills.ts`, `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `backend/src/services/tenantService.ts`, `backend/src/repository/tenantRepository.ts`, `backend/src/index.ts`, `backend/.env.example`, `backend/src/tests/multiCurrency.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/auth/Register.tsx`, `frontend/src/hooks/useTenantSettings.ts`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Phase 4 of New-Modules Plan: Real Approval Workflows (Opt-In, Multi-Level)

**What:** `approval_workflows`/`approval_steps` existed in the schema (a generic polymorphic `entityType`/`entityId` design with `requiredLevel`/`currentLevel`) but had zero API. Built the fourth module:
1. **CRUD + decision endpoints** (`approvalWorkflowRepository.ts`, `approvalWorkflowService.ts`, `routes/approvalWorkflows.ts`, `/api/v1/approval-workflows`): `POST /` starts a workflow for any `entityType`/`entityId` with a chosen number of required levels, auto-creating one `ApprovalStep` row per level. `POST /:id/steps/:level/decide` records an approve/reject - levels must be decided strictly in order (can't approve level 2 before level 1), a reject immediately and permanently marks the whole workflow `REJECTED` (no re-deciding), and approving the final level marks the workflow `APPROVED`.
2. **The actual gate - deliberately kept out-of-band, no enum/status migration on the underlying entities**: rather than adding a new "PENDING_APPROVAL" value to `JournalEntry.status`'s enum (a real schema migration touching existing rows) or to `Invoice`/`VendorBill`'s plain string status, `journalEntryService.postJournalEntry()`, `invoices.ts`'s `/pay`, and `bills.ts`'s `/pay` each now call a single shared `assertApprovedOrNoWorkflow(tenantId, entityType, entityId)` check before proceeding. If no workflow was ever requested for that specific entity, nothing changes - the check is a no-op and existing tenants see zero behavior change. If one exists and isn't `APPROVED` yet, the action is rejected (400) with the workflow's current status in the message.
3. **Frontend**: `frontend/src/pages/approvals/Approvals.tsx` - a single inbox-style page listing all workflows with per-step approve/reject buttons (enabled only for the next undecided level) plus a "Request Approval" form (entity type, entity ID, required levels). **Scope note**: the original plan sketch also called for adding a dedicated "Request Approval" button inside the Invoice/Bill/Journal Entry detail views themselves; that was simplified to the single generic form on this page (paste the record's ID) to keep the frontend change contained to one new page rather than modifying three others - the backend gate works identically either way.
**Verification:** New `backend/src/tests/approvalWorkflows.test.ts` (4 tests): an entity with no workflow posts/pays completely normally (the critical "opt-in, zero regression" check), a 2-level workflow blocks posting until both levels approve in strict order (rejecting an out-of-order level-2-before-level-1 attempt), a rejection at any level permanently blocks the entity and can't be re-decided, and cross-tenant isolation (can't view or decide another tenant's workflow). Full backend suite: 228/228 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/repository/approvalWorkflowRepository.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/approvalWorkflowService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/approvalWorkflows.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/journalEntryService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/routes/bills.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/src/tests/approvalWorkflows.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/approvals/Approvals.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Phase 3 of New-Modules Plan: Real Recurring Transactions

**What:** `recurring_transactions` existed in the schema (with `nextRun`/`lastRun`/`isActive` columns clearly designed for a cron sweep) but had zero API. Built the third module:
1. **CRUD** (`recurringTransactionRepository.ts`, `recurringTransactionService.ts`, `routes/recurringTransactions.ts`, `/api/v1/recurring-transactions`): `templateData` (JSON) holds a `{ description, lines: [...] }` payload shaped exactly like `journalEntryService.createJournalEntry`'s own input, validated to have at least 2 lines with real `accountId`s before saving.
2. **`RecurringTransactionCronService`** (mirrors `ScheduledEmailCronService`'s established shape): an hourly `node-cron` sweep, registered at boot in `index.ts` alongside the existing email cron. Since a cron sweep runs with no active HTTP/tenant request context (and `journalEntryService` internally requires one via `requireTenantContext()`), each due row's processing is wrapped in `runWithTenantContext(...)` (an existing but previously request-only-used helper) so the *exact same*, unmodified `journalEntryService.createJournalEntry(...)` call posts a real journal entry - no parallel/duplicate posting logic was written. `nextRun` advances by the row's own `frequency` (DAILY/WEEKLY/MONTHLY/QUARTERLY/YEARLY) after each successful run; a row whose `endDate` has passed is deactivated instead of firing forever. A failed row is logged and left with its `nextRun` unchanged so it retries the next sweep rather than silently skipping a cycle.
3. **Frontend**: `frontend/src/pages/settings/RecurringTransactions.tsx` (list + create form: name, frequency, start date, a debit account/credit account/amount pair that assembles the `templateData` lines), linked from the sidebar.
**Verification:** New `backend/src/tests/recurringTransactions.test.ts` (5 tests): date-advancement math per frequency, template validation (rejects fewer than 2 lines, rejects an invalid frequency), tenant isolation, and the core regression test - calling the cron sweep directly against a due row creates a real `POSTED` journal entry and advances `nextRun`, while an immediate second sweep call correctly generates nothing further (since `nextRun` is now in the future). Full backend suite: 224/224 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/repository/recurringTransactionRepository.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/recurringTransactionService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/recurringTransactions.ts` (new), `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/src/index.ts`, `backend/src/tests/recurringTransactions.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/settings/RecurringTransactions.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Phase 2 of New-Modules Plan: Real Fiscal Periods (Open/Close/Lock) + Budgets

**What:** `journalEntryService.ts` allowed posting a journal entry to any date whatsoever, with zero concept of a closed accounting period - `fiscal_periods` and `budgets` existed in the schema with no API. Built the second module:
1. **Fiscal Periods** (`fiscalPeriodRepository.ts`, `fiscalPeriodService.ts`, `routes/fiscalPeriods.ts`, `/api/v1/fiscal-periods`): tenant-scoped CRUD with an `OPEN → CLOSED → LOCKED` lifecycle (`PATCH .../close`, `PATCH .../lock` - lock requires Admin role specifically, since it's a stronger and harder-to-reverse action than closing). `(fiscalYear, periodNumber)` is unique per tenant (fixed as part of Phase 0 - it was accidentally globally unique before).
2. **The actual guard**: `journalEntryService.createJournalEntry()` and `postJournalEntry()` now look up the tenant's `FiscalPeriod` covering the entry's date and reject (400) if its status isn't `OPEN`. Deliberately scoped to only the moment an entry actually posts to the ledger - `createJournalEntry` only checks when `status === 'POSTED'` is requested directly; a `DRAFT` entry for a closed-period date is still allowed to be saved, since it has no ledger effect until someone posts it (which re-checks). Since both `invoices.ts`'s and `bills.ts`'s `/pay` handlers funnel through `createJournalEntry({ status: 'POSTED' })`, this one change protects all three real posting paths. If a tenant has never defined a period covering a date, posting is unrestricted (opt-in enforcement, not a forced migration of existing behavior).
3. **Budgets** (`budgetRepository.ts`, `budgetService.ts`, `routes/budgets.ts`, `/api/v1/budgets`): one budget row per (account, fiscal period). `actualAmount`/`variance` are stored columns per the schema but are **recomputed on every read** (not by a separate cron) by summing the account's real `debit - credit` ledger activity within the period's actual date range - chosen over a background job to avoid another moving part for a v1, at the cost of the sum running on each GET rather than being pre-aggregated.
4. **Frontend**: `frontend/src/pages/settings/FiscalPeriods.tsx` (period list with Close/Lock actions + create form) and `frontend/src/pages/reports/Budgets.tsx` (budget-vs-actual table per period + create form), both linked from the sidebar.
**Verification:** New `backend/src/tests/fiscalPeriodsAndBudgets.test.ts` (6 tests): per-tenant period-number uniqueness, posting succeeds in an OPEN period, posting is blocked once CLOSED (but a DRAFT for the same date still saves), lock/close state-machine transitions are enforced (can't re-close a LOCKED period), postings for dates with no configured period at all are unrestricted, budget creation + real variance recompute from actual ledger postings, duplicate (account, period) budgets rejected, and cross-tenant isolation. Full backend suite: 219/219 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/repository/fiscalPeriodRepository.ts` (new), `backend/src/repository/budgetRepository.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/fiscalPeriodService.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/budgetService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/fiscalPeriods.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/budgets.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/journalEntryService.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/src/tests/fiscalPeriodsAndBudgets.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/settings/FiscalPeriods.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/pages/reports/Budgets.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Phase 1 of New-Modules Plan: Real Tax Rates (Replaces Hardcoded 10% Invoice Tax)

**What:** `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts` hardcoded `const tax = subtotal * 0.10;` for every invoice, regardless of tenant, jurisdiction, or customer - the `tax_rates` DB table existed in the schema but had zero API surface (a documented Known Issue). Built the first of four planned new modules on top of the Phase 0 `tenantId` foundation:
1. **Full CRUD** (`backend/src/repository/taxRateRepository.ts`, `services/taxRateService.ts`, `routes/taxRates.ts`, mounted at `/api/v1/tax-rates`) - tenant-scoped, following the established `ReportSchedule`-style shared-table pattern (plain Prisma calls filtered by `tenantId`, not `withCurrentTenantDb`). Rate validated to be a sane fraction (0, 1]; codes are unique per tenant (`@@unique([tenantId, code])`); deleting a rate that's referenced by any invoice is blocked (must deactivate instead).
2. **Real tax resolution on invoice creation**: `Invoice` gets a new `taxRateId` (nullable FK, `onDelete: SetNull`, matching the `customerId`/`Customer` relation pattern already on the model). Creating an invoice now either uses an explicitly-passed `taxRateId`, or resolves the tenant's single active rate covering the invoice date (`effectiveFrom <= date <= effectiveTo ?? now`) - if the tenant has zero active rates, tax is honestly `0`, not a guessed percentage; if more than one active rate covers the date, the request is rejected (400) rather than silently picking one, forcing an explicit choice.
3. **Frontend**: new `frontend/src/pages/settings/TaxRates.tsx` (list + create + delete, linked from the sidebar's Finance & Banking group) and a tax-rate selector added to the invoice-creation modal in `Invoices.tsx` (defaults to "use the active rate" if left unselected).
**Verification:** New `backend/src/tests/taxRates.test.ts` (6 tests): CRUD validation, per-tenant code uniqueness (same code in two different tenants is fine), cross-tenant isolation (a tenant can't fetch another's rate), the real invoice-tax-calculation integration (a 15% rate produces real 15% tax, not the old flat 10%), the multiple-active-rates-requires-explicit-choice guard, and the delete-blocked-while-in-use guard. Full backend suite: 213/213 passing, twice in a row. Frontend `tsc -b` clean.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260725071000_add_tax_rate_id_to_invoices/` (new), `backend/src/repository/taxRateRepository.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/taxRateService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/taxRates.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/src/tests/taxRates.test.ts` (new), `frontend/src/pages/settings/TaxRates.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/pages/invoices/Invoices.tsx`, `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Phase 0 of New-Modules Plan: Added `tenantId` to the 6 Orphaned Tables

**What:** Kicking off a larger, user-approved multi-phase plan to build real functionality for `tax_rates`, `fiscal_periods`, `budgets`, `recurring_transactions`, `approval_workflows`, and `approval_steps` (all previously DB tables with zero API surface - see TASKS.md Known Issues), plus real multi-currency conversion and a platform-wide audit log view. Investigation found none of these 6 tables had a `tenantId` column at all, unlike every other shared business-data table in the schema - this had to be fixed first since it's the foundation every subsequent phase depends on.
**Fix:** Added `tenantId String @map("tenant_id")` + `@@index([tenantId])` to all 6 models, matching the established bare-column (no `@relation`) shared-table pattern used by `BankAccount`/`Customer`/etc. Also fixed two related global-uniqueness bugs discovered while doing this - `TaxRate.code` was declared `@unique` (globally, across all tenants) and `FiscalPeriod` had `@@unique([fiscalYear, periodNumber])` (also global) - both would have silently prevented two different tenants from ever using the same tax code or having a "Period 1 of fiscal year 2026," which is obviously wrong for a multi-tenant system. Changed both to tenant-scoped composite uniques (`@@unique([tenantId, code])` / `@@unique([tenantId, fiscalYear, periodNumber])`). Confirmed all 6 tables were completely empty (zero rows, nothing has ever used them) before adding the columns as `NOT NULL` directly, no backfill/default needed.
**Verification:** Full backend suite: 207/207 passing. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260725070000_add_tenant_id_to_orphaned_tables/` (new), `STATUS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Fixed the 5 Pre-Existing Backend Test Failures (Ledger Posting Mock + Mono Banking Test Isolation)

**What:** Investigated the 5 test failures flagged as a Known Issue while fixing the Quick Start Guide PDF. Both were confirmed real but non-production bugs via targeted investigation (one via `git stash` diffing against the base commit, the other via a live Postgres run capturing the actual thrown error):
1. **`accountingSchema.test.ts` ledger-posting failure - a stale test mock, not a code bug.** `postJournalEntryToLedger` (`ledgerRepository.ts`) was correctly refactored in an earlier commit to replace a per-line balance-fetch query with one batched query before the insert loop (a legitimate deadlock/correctness fix). The test's hand-written `mockResolvedValueOnce` chain was never updated to match - it still stubbed 6 sequential responses assuming the old alternating "fetch-balance, insert" pattern, but the real code now only makes 5 calls. Jest's mock queue is consumed strictly in call order, so the second ledger line's INSERT silently consumed the wrong stub (`[]`), making `mapLedgerRow` crash on an undefined row.
2. **`banking.test.ts` Mono `/connect` 500 - real orphaned test data, confirmed via a live run (not a schema-drift or axios-mock issue - both concretely ruled out).** `BankAccount.monoAccountId` is a *globally* unique column, but the test used hardcoded literal Mono account ids (`'mono-acc-tenant1'`, etc.) instead of scoping them to the run like the tenant slugs already do. A previous run's rows survived because `BankAccount`/`BankTransaction` live in the shared `public` schema with no FK/cascade back to `Tenant`, and the test's cleanup never deleted them - so the next run's `POST /connect` hit a genuine Prisma `P2002` unique-constraint collision against leftover rows from an earlier run, surfacing as a 500 and cascading into 3 more failures that read `.bankAccount.id` off the failed response.
**Fix:**
1. `accountingSchema.test.ts`: collapsed the two now-nonexistent per-line balance-fetch mocks into a single stub representing the real batched query, matching the actual 5-call sequence. No production code changed - `ledgerRepository.ts` was already correct.
2. `banking.test.ts`: scoped every literal Mono account id to the test run (reusing the existing `runId` already used for tenant slugs), and extended `cleanupTestData()` to explicitly delete `BankAccount` rows for the test's own tenant ids (captured from `onboardTenant`'s real response) before/after the suite, so leftover bank data can no longer survive between runs.
3. **Deliberately did not** add a `Tenant` → `BankAccount` FK/cascade relation, despite that being one option surfaced during investigation: every other tenant-scoped shared table in `schema.prisma` (~20 models - `Customer`, invoices, etc.) deliberately uses the same bare `tenantId` column-filtering pattern with no FK, and there is no tenant-deletion endpoint in production today (per the existing Known Issues list) for a cascade to matter yet. Adding a one-off FK just to `BankAccount` would have been an inconsistent, unrequested schema change for a gap that's currently test-only; the test-level fixes above fully resolve the actual failures without touching production schema.
**Verification:** Full backend suite run twice in a row against real Postgres/Redis: 207/207 passing both times (up from 202/207). `tsc --noEmit` clean. (One transient run mid-verification showed 3 unrelated failures in `performanceAndHardening.test.ts` after the sandbox's Redis process died outside of any test - restarting Redis and rerunning confirmed this was sandbox instability, not a real regression; it did not recur.)
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/tests/accountingSchema.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/banking.test.ts`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Fixed Fake "Quick Start Guide" PDF Attached to the Welcome Email

**What:** Asked to verify whether the "Quick Start Guide PDF" emailed to a business right after successful account verification is real. It was not: `EmailService.sendWelcomePackage()` attached a hand-typed raw PDF byte string (variable literally named `samplePdfBuffer`) containing a single line of text - "AccountGo ERP Quick Start Guide" - with no actual onboarding content, steps, or links. There was no PDF-generation library anywhere in the backend and no `.pdf` asset file in the repo; it was 100% hardcoded inline, identical for every tenant every time. The trigger wiring itself (`POST /api/v1/auth/verify` calling `sendWelcomePackage` once both email and SMS verification succeed) was real and correctly connected - only the attachment's content was fake.
**Fix:** Added `pdfkit` (a real, pure-JS server-side PDF generator - chosen over a headless-browser approach like Playwright/Chromium to avoid adding a heavy browser binary dependency to the production API for one email attachment) and a new `backend/src/services/pdfGenerationService.ts` with `generateQuickStartGuidePdf(businessName, recipientName)`. It renders a real multi-section document: a cover addressed to the actual tenant/user, a "Getting Started" section (Chart of Accounts, warehouses & cash tills, inviting team members), a real feature overview matching what's actually shipped today (ledgers, invoicing/bills, multi-warehouse inventory, cash till SMS shortage alerts, Mono bank reconciliation, scheduled executive reports), and a real sign-in link built from `APP_URL`. `EmailService.sendWelcomePackage` now calls this generator instead of using the stub buffer, and `auth.ts`'s `/verify` handler was updated to fetch the user's `tenant` relation so the real business name (not just the user's own name) is passed through.
**Verification:** New `pdfGenerationService.test.ts` asserts the generated buffer starts with the real `%PDF-` signature, is well over 5x the size of the old ~350-byte stub, and - via a small PDF text-show-operator extractor written for the test (pdfkit encodes drawn text as hex-string `TJ` arrays, not literal ASCII, so a raw substring search doesn't work) - actually contains the tenant name, recipient name, and real section text like "Chart of Accounts" and "Bank reconciliation", proving it's not the old placeholder and that content is genuinely generated per-tenant rather than a fixed payload. Full backend suite run against real Postgres/Redis: 202 passing. **Found, but explicitly not touched, while running the full suite**: 5 pre-existing failures in `accountingSchema.test.ts` (1) and `banking.test.ts` (4) - confirmed via `git stash` that these fail identically on the base commit before this change, so they predate and are unrelated to this fix; they appear to be a regression in the Mono banking integration/ledger repository since it was last verified passing, and are being left as a documented Known Issue rather than fixed under this task's scope.
**Files Affected:** `backend/package.json`, `backend/package-lock.json`, `backend/src/services/pdfGenerationService.ts` (new), `backend/src/services/EmailService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/auth.ts`, `backend/src/tests/pdfGenerationService.test.ts` (new), `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Landing Page Professionalism Pass: Browser Title, Meta Tags, Favicon, Font Loading

**What:** Asked to review the public landing page for professionalism (copy, fonts, "everything"). The visual design/copy were already solid, but found four concrete, real defects:
1. **Browser tab title was the literal Vite scaffold default**, `<title>frontend</title>` - never changed from the template. Fixed to `"AccountGo | Multi-Tenant ERP & Accounting Platform"`.
2. **No meta description or Open Graph/Twitter tags at all** - search results and social shares would have shown nothing useful. Added a real description plus `og:*`/`twitter:*` tags.
3. **Favicon was the unmodified default Vite/template icon** (a generic purple abstract blob, unrelated to this brand) - never replaced. Authored a real branded mark (a simple ascending-bars glyph in the site's actual emerald-to-blue gradient) and replaced `favicon.svg`.
4. **The site declares `font-family: 'Inter', ...` in `index.css` but never actually loads Inter anywhere** - no Google Fonts link, no self-hosted font files, no `@font-face`. `document.fonts.check('Inter')` reported true only because this specific dev sandbox happens to have a system font named Inter installed (confirmed via `fc-list` showing no Inter, yet Chromium still resolved something) - real users' browsers do not ship Inter by default, so production visitors were silently getting the `system-ui` fallback instead of the intended typeface, with no way to know from looking at the CSS alone. Added the standard Google Fonts `<link>` embed (`preconnect` + `css2?family=Inter...`) to `index.html` so the declared font actually loads.
**Why:** All four are the kind of "looks configured but silently doesn't work" defect this whole session has been about, just in the frontend/branding surface instead of the backend - a real prospective customer's first impression (browser tab, search result snippet, social share preview, and actual rendered typeface) was not matching the polish of the page content itself.
**Verification:** Confirmed via Playwright: page title and meta description now read correctly in the DOM. Confirmed the Google Fonts endpoint is reachable (`curl` 200) and the embed snippet matches Google's standard, documented format exactly. Could not capture the actual font *download* through this sandbox's outbound proxy (the sandbox requires an explicit `HTTPS_PROXY` for all egress that a bare Playwright-launched browser doesn't inherit automatically, and configuring it introduced unrelated localhost-routing issues that broke the dev server connection itself) - this is a sandbox networking limitation, not a defect in the fix; real end users' browsers make direct requests with no such proxy. Frontend `tsc -b && vite build` clean; visually confirmed via screenshot that the page still renders cleanly.
**Files Affected:** `frontend/index.html`, `frontend/public/favicon.svg`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Built Real Bank-Feed Integration via Mono Connect (Inert Until Real Credentials Supplied)

**What:** Replaced the fake `POST /api/v1/banking/connect` (which created a `BankAccount` from manually-typed fields and seeded two hardcoded demo transactions - "Acme Client Corp", "AWS Web Services") with a real integration against Mono, the open-banking API covering Ghana and Nigeria - chosen with the user given this platform's existing Ghana focus (GH₵ currency, Arkesel/mNotify/Hubtel Ghanaian SMS gateways). The user doesn't have Mono API credentials yet and explicitly chose to have the real integration code built now anyway, understanding it stays inert until real keys are supplied.
1. **New `monoService.ts`**: wraps Mono's real, doc-verified API - code-exchange (`POST /v2/accounts/auth`), account details (`GET /v2/accounts/{id}`), transactions (`GET /v2/accounts/{id}/transactions`), and webhook secret verification (`mono-webhook-secret` header) - gated entirely on `MONO_SECRET_KEY`/`MONO_WEBHOOK_SECRET` env vars with no hardcoded fallback secrets, matching this session's established pattern.
2. **`banking.ts` rewritten**: `POST /connect` now takes a `monoCode` (from the frontend widget), exchanges it for a real Mono account, and seeds real transactions - or returns `503` "not configured" if Mono isn't set up, never falling back to fake data (that fallback was the bug being fixed, and doing it again here would violate `CLAUDE.md`'s "No Mock Data Ever" rule). Added `POST /banking/accounts/:id/sync` (what "Sync Feeds" now actually calls, instead of just re-reading the same local rows) and `POST /banking/webhooks/mono` (real-time sync on Mono's `account_connected`/`account_updated` events, verified via shared secret since there's no tenant JWT on an external webhook call).
3. **Schema**: added `monoAccountId`/`institutionName`/`lastSyncedAt` to `BankAccount` and `monoTransactionId` to `BankTransaction` (for clean dedupe across repeated syncs via `createMany({ skipDuplicates: true })`).
4. **Frontend**: `BankReconciliation.tsx`'s manual "type in your bank name and account number" modal replaced with Mono's real Connect Widget (`@mono.co/connect.js`, confirmed real via `npm install`), gated on `VITE_MONO_PUBLIC_KEY` - shows a clear "not configured" notice with disabled buttons rather than a broken widget when unset (as it is in this environment). Also fixed the same hardcoded-USD-formatter bug already fixed elsewhere this session, now that real per-account currency (e.g. GHS) actually flows through.
**Why:** Closes the last remaining "fake feature" Known Issue that had a real, buildable path forward (unlike multi-currency or the orphaned tables, which still need product decisions on scope). Verified Mono's actual API shape via their current docs rather than trusting possibly-stale training knowledge (endpoints, header names, and the real `@mono.co/connect.js` npm package all confirmed via web search/fetch and an actual `npm install`).
**Verification:** New tests in `banking.test.ts` (mocking `axios` - legitimate per `CLAUDE.md` since Mono is the external service, not our own logic): confirms `POST /connect` returns 503 and creates zero rows when unconfigured; confirms it creates a real `BankAccount`/`BankTransaction`s matching mocked Mono responses (and NOT the old hardcoded payees) when configured; confirms manual sync pulls only new transactions without duplicating existing ones; confirms the webhook rejects a wrong secret (401) and syncs on a correct one. Full backend suite: 204/205 (same pre-existing unrelated stale-mock flake documented all session). Frontend `tsc -b && vite build` clean. Live-verified via Playwright: with no `VITE_MONO_PUBLIC_KEY` set (as in this environment), the page shows the "not configured" notice with disabled connect/sync buttons rather than a broken widget or silent fake success.
**Not verified (cannot be, without real credentials)**: an actual live round-trip against Mono's real servers. That step is on the user once they have sandbox/production keys - set `MONO_SECRET_KEY`, `MONO_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, and `VITE_MONO_PUBLIC_KEY`, then do a real end-to-end connect.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260725060000_add_mono_bank_integration_fields/`, `backend/src/services/monoService.ts` (new), `backend/src/routes/banking.ts`, `backend/src/tests/banking.test.ts`, `backend/.env.example`, `frontend/src/pages/banking/BankReconciliation.tsx`, `frontend/src/types/mono-connect.d.ts` (new), `frontend/.env.example` (new), `frontend/package.json`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Fixed Rate Limiter Key Collision Between api/auth/onboarding Limiters

**What:** `apiRateLimiter`, `authRateLimiter`, and `onboardingRateLimiter` (`backend/src/middleware/rateLimiterMiddleware.ts`) all built their Redis counter key identically - `rate_limit:${tenantOrIp}` - with no way to tell which limiter incremented it. Since `apiRateLimiter` is mounted globally on every `/api/` route, ordinary traffic (e.g. a single page load firing several parallel GETs) inflated the exact same counter the much stricter `authRateLimiter` (10/min) and `onboardingRateLimiter` (5/min) check against. A real user's first login-then-save on a data-heavy page (confirmed live: the new Scheduled Reports Settings tab) could get spuriously 429'd by their own unrelated page-load traffic, with the error surfacing as a confusing "Too Many Requests" on an action that made only one or two actual calls to the limited route.
**Fix:** Added a required `name` field to each limiter's options (`'api'`/`'auth'`/`'onboarding'`) and namespaced the cache key by it: `rate_limit:${name}:${tenantOrIp}`. Each limiter now has its own isolated budget per tenant/IP, as originally intended.
**Verification:** New `rateLimiterKeyIsolation.test.ts` - fires 15 requests through `apiRateLimiter` for a tenant (over `authRateLimiter`'s 10/min limit, under `apiRateLimiter`'s own 100/min), then confirms a single `authRateLimiter` check for the same tenant still passes. Confirmed the test **fails** against the pre-fix shared-key code (auth check blocked at count 16) and passes after the fix - the rate limiter module bypasses itself under `NODE_ENV=test`, so the test temporarily flips it to exercise the real Redis-backed path. Full backend suite: 201/202 (same pre-existing unrelated stale-mock flake documented in every prior entry this session). Reproduced and confirmed fixed live: the exact "Settings page save gets 429'd" scenario found while verifying the Scheduled Reports feature no longer occurs after this fix.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/middleware/rateLimiterMiddleware.ts`, `backend/src/tests/rateLimiterKeyIsolation.test.ts` (new), `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Built a Real Scheduled Reports System (Persistent, Per-Tenant, Actually Dispatches)

**What:** Replaced the entirely in-memory, never-actually-scheduled "Scheduled Reports" feature with a real one:
1. **New `ReportSchedule` model** (`backend/prisma/migrations/20260725050000_add_report_schedules/`): one row per tenant (`tenantId` unique), `frequency`/`dayOfWeek`/`hourUtc`/`reportType`/`recipients`/`enabled`/`lastSentAt`. A tenantId-column shared table (not per-tenant-schema), matching the pattern already used for `DailyCloseoutReport` etc.
2. **`scheduledReports.ts`'s `POST`/`GET /schedule`** now upsert/read this real table instead of an in-memory object that was wiped on every restart.
3. **Found and fixed a live production bug while doing this**: the *other*, already-running cron (`ScheduledEmailCronService`, a hardcoded `'0 8 * * 1'` node-cron job) has been emailing **every tenant's Admin, unconditionally, every Monday, with hardcoded fake figures** (`weeklySales: 4850.00`, `topShopName: 'Osu Downtown Shop'`, `totalItemsSold: 34`) - the exact same fake-data bug already fixed in the test-email endpoint earlier this session, just never caught in this file since nothing tested the real cron job itself. Replaced it with an hourly sweep (`runDueSchedulesJob`) that checks each tenant's real, persisted schedule and sends real computed figures (extracted into a shared `computeWeeklyReportData()` helper used by both the dispatcher and the test-email endpoint).
4. **Also discovered the Settings UI's "scheduling" controls were mostly theater**: "Automated Dispatch Schedule" was a disabled, hardcoded label (no frequency picker ever existed), and the `enabled` toggle was local React state never sent to any API - dead state, same bug class as the dead `"journals"` import option fixed earlier. Neither `/schedule` endpoint was ever called by the frontend at all.
5. **Explicit product decision (asked the user)**: after this change, a tenant only receives the weekly email if they've explicitly saved a schedule with `enabled: true` - chosen over silently preserving every existing tenant's implicit "gets emailed no matter what" behavior. This is a deliberate behavior change.
6. **Frontend**: `Settings.tsx`'s scheduled-reports tab now fetches the real schedule on load, has a real checkbox bound to `enabled` (previously nonexistent), and a "Save Schedule Settings" button that persists both the recipient email and the enable/disable state.
**Why:** The Known Issue explicitly named this as something to build for real rather than leave as a documented gap; scoping it turned up that the actual bug (fake data going out weekly, right now, to every tenant) was worse than the "doesn't persist across restarts" framing suggested.
**Verification:** New dispatcher test (`scheduledReports.test.ts`) with three seeded schedules (due-now/enabled, not-due, disabled) proving only the due+enabled one gets emailed, `lastSentAt` is set, and an immediate second run doesn't double-send. The two pre-existing tests in that file pass unmodified against the new DB-backed implementation (free regression coverage). Manually ran the real dispatcher function twice in a row against a live onboarded tenant with a schedule seeded to match the current UTC hour, confirming exactly one send and no double-send. Full backend suite: 200/201 (the one failure is the same pre-existing, unrelated stale-mock flake documented in every prior entry this session). Frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; verified live via Playwright that the checkbox correctly loads the real persisted `enabled` state from the backend.
**Found but explicitly out of scope for this task**: while manually verifying the frontend save flow, discovered `apiRateLimiter`, `authRateLimiter`, and `onboardingRateLimiter` (`backend/src/middleware/rateLimiterMiddleware.ts`) all key their Redis counter identically (`rate_limit:${tenantOrIp}`), so ordinary API traffic under a tenant (e.g. a single Settings page load, which fires ~6-8 parallel GETs) inflates the same counter the much stricter auth/onboarding limiters check against - meaning a real user's very first login-then-save can get spuriously 429'd by their own unrelated page-load traffic. Not fixed here; flagged as a new Known Issue.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260725050000_add_report_schedules/`, `backend/src/routes/scheduledReports.ts`, `backend/src/services/scheduledEmailService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/scheduledReports.test.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/settings/Settings.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Closed Out the "Safe Fixes" Backlog: CI/CD, Test Coverage, Admin Tab Wiring, AI Mislabeling

**What:** Asked for everything remaining to be fixed at once. The backlog split into real gaps (fixable outright) and items needing a product decision (fake bank-feed integration, in-memory Scheduled Reports persistence, cosmetic multi-currency, 8 orphaned DB tables) - confirmed with the user to leave the latter as documented Known Issues and fix only the former in this pass:
1. **CI/CD pipeline**: added `.github/workflows/ci.yml` - a `backend` job (typecheck, `prisma migrate deploy` against real `postgres:16-alpine`/`redis:7-alpine` service containers, then the full Jest suite) and a `frontend` job (`oxlint`, then `tsc -b && vite build`). No CI config existed at all before this. Verified every step's command locally against a fresh DB first (this sandbox can't trigger an actual Actions run).
2. **Zero test coverage on `banking.ts` and `import.ts`**: added `banking.test.ts` (connect/list/reconcile, including a cross-tenant isolation check) and `import.test.ts` (bulk account/journal import, valid + invalid rows). No production bugs surfaced - both routes already correctly used the tenant-scoped services from earlier fixes.

No backend changes needed. **Caught a real bug while verifying with a live screenshot**: the new table's cells used the shared `Table`/`TableCell` components' default light-mode text color (`text-secondary-900`, meant for light backgrounds), which was invisible against this page's fixed dark theme - only the one cell I'd explicitly colored white was legible. Fixed by explicitly coloring every cell/header for this dark-only page rather than relying on the components' light/dark-mode defaults. ("System Audit Logs" intentionally left alone - it needs a new platform-wide audit endpoint that doesn't exist yet, which the user confirmed is out of scope for this pass.)
4. **"AI Categorization" mislabeling**: `JournalBuilder.tsx` said "AI Suggest Category" / "AI Recommendation" for what's actually keyword matching with hardcoded confidence scores. Changed both user-facing strings to "Suggest Category" / "Suggested Category" - no backend/route/file renames, since those are internal implementation details with no user-facing benefit to churning.
**Why:** Closes out every item in this backlog that doesn't require inventing new schema, endpoints, or third-party integrations - matching this session's established rule (`CLAUDE.md`'s "Spec-Driven Development") of only building what's specified rather than guessing at product decisions.
**Verification:** Full backend suite re-run against a freshly reset DB: 199/200 passing (the one failure is the same pre-existing, unrelated stale-mock flake documented in every prior entry this session). Frontend `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build`/`npm run lint` all clean. AdminCoreEngine fix verified live via Playwright/Chromium screenshots (before-and-after, catching and then confirming the fix for the invisible-text bug above) against a running backend+frontend with two real onboarded tenants.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Fixed Silently Broken Invoice/Bill Payment Posting and AI Categorization

**What:** Found a new critical bug while scoping the next task (not from the earlier system-wide audit): `invoices.ts`'s `POST /invoices/:id/pay`, `bills.ts`'s `POST /bills/:id/pay`, and `aiCategorization.ts`'s `POST /ai/categorize` all fetched the tenant's Chart of Accounts via `(client as any).account.findMany()` - a Prisma-typed query. `Account`/`JournalEntry`/`Ledger` are schema-per-tenant tables (real rows only exist inside each tenant's own Postgres schema, written via `accountRepository.ts`'s raw SQL), but `Account` is *also* a normal Prisma model with its own physically-migrated, always-empty `public.accounts` table. Exactly like the schema-qualification trap discovered earlier this session (the one that broke the original per-tenant-schema migration attempt): Prisma's typed client always schema-qualifies to `public`, ignoring the `SET LOCAL search_path` that `withCurrentTenantDb` sets - that trick only works for raw SQL. Effect: `accounts` was always `[]` at all three call sites, so:
- Paying an invoice never posted the Cash-debit/Revenue-credit journal entry - the invoice was marked `PAID` and the API claimed success, but `journalId` silently stayed `null`.
- Paying a vendor bill never posted the Expense-debit/Cash-credit journal entry - same silent failure, with the response literally saying "Vendor bill paid and Journal Entry posted." while nothing posted.
- AI categorization (a real, user-facing feature in `JournalBuilder.tsx`) always returned `suggestion: null` regardless of the input description, for every tenant, always.
**Why this matters:** direct violation of the platform's core purpose - real cash movements tied to invoicing and vendor bills never reached the ledger, so Trial Balance/P&L/Balance Sheet reports would silently omit them. It also explains a coverage gap: `invoicesAndBills.test.ts` (added earlier this session) never actually called either `/pay` endpoint.
**Fix:** Replaced all three broken call sites with `accountRepository.listAccounts(client)` (the existing, correct, tenant-schema-aware repository function already used properly elsewhere via `accountService.ts`), which issues raw SQL that correctly resolves against the tenant's `search_path`. No schema/migration changes needed.
**Verification:** New `paymentPosting.test.ts`: onboards a tenant, creates a real Chart of Accounts (Cash/Revenue/Expense), creates+pays a real invoice and a real vendor bill, and asserts the returned `journalId` is non-null and the posted `GET /journal-entries/:id` shows `POSTED` with correct debit/credit lines and amounts; also asserts `/ai/categorize` returns a real suggestion matching a seeded account. Confirmed all three assertions **fail against the pre-fix code** (`journalId: null` / `suggestion: null`, exactly as predicted) before applying the fix, then pass after. Full backend suite re-run against a freshly reset DB: 195/196 passing (the one failure is the same pre-existing, unrelated stale-mock flake in `accountingSchema.test.ts` documented in every prior entry this session).
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/routes/invoices.ts`, `backend/src/routes/bills.ts`, `backend/src/routes/aiCategorization.ts`, `backend/src/tests/paymentPosting.test.ts` (new), `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Added Helmet + CORS Allowlist; Removed Dead Import-Type Option

**What:** Two smaller fixes from the ongoing gap-analysis list:
1. **Wide-open CORS + no security headers:** `app.use(cors())` reflected/allowed every origin with no restriction, and the app sent no hardening headers at all (no CSP, no `X-Frame-Options`, no HSTS, etc.) despite `helmet` being a one-line addition. Added `helmet()` and replaced the bare `cors()` with an explicit allowlist (`CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, comma-separated, falling back to `APP_URL` then `http://localhost:5173` for local dev). Also added a small error-handling middleware so a rejected CORS request returns a clean JSON `403` instead of Express's default HTML error page (which was leaking a full stack trace with file paths in non-production mode).
2. **Dead `"journals"` import-type option:** `BulkImportWizard.tsx` had `importType` typed as `"accounts" | "journals"`, but the only `<select>` option was `"accounts"` and `handleExecuteImport` had no `"journals"` branch - `"journals"` was unreachable dead state that never did anything. Narrowed the type to `"accounts"` only and disabled the (now single-option) select, rather than building an unspec'd journal-import feature.
**Why:** Both were flagged in the earlier system-wide gap audit - CORS/security-headers under "no security audit has been done," the dead option under "Low" severity findings. The CORS/helmet fix is a straightforward hardening change (no product decision required, unlike the still-open Known Issues); the dead option was pure unreachable code, not a feature to build.
**Verification:** Started the backend and confirmed live: a request with `Origin: http://localhost:5173` gets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` echoed back plus all helmet headers (`Content-Security-Policy`, `X-Frame-Options`, `Strict-Transport-Security`, etc.); a request with `Origin: http://evil.example.com` gets a clean `403 {"success":false,"error":"Origin not allowed by CORS policy."}` instead of a stack trace. Full backend suite re-run against a freshly reset DB: 192/193 passing (same pre-existing unrelated stale-mock flake). Frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean after the `BulkImportWizard.tsx` change.
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/app.ts`, `backend/.env.example`, `backend/package.json`, `backend/package-lock.json`, `frontend/src/pages/import/BulkImportWizard.tsx`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Fixed Unauthenticated Tenant-Enumeration on GET /api/v1/tenants

**What:** `GET /api/v1/tenants` had no auth of any kind - anyone who could reach the API could list every registered tenant's `name`/`slug`/`id`/tier across the entire platform. This is a platform-operator listing (used by the Admin Core Engine console's "Tenant Schemas & Tiers" tab, still unwired), not something any individual tenant's JWT should grant - a tenant token only proves membership in one tenant, not visibility into the platform roster. Gated it behind the same master broadcast passcode already used elsewhere for platform-operator actions (`BroadcastService.verifyPasscode()`, via `?passcode=` query param or an `X-Admin-Passcode` header), returning 401 without a valid one.
**Why:** Confirmed no frontend code currently calls this endpoint, so there was no UI to update. Chose the existing passcode gate over a JWT+role check since this action is platform-wide, not tenant-scoped - identical reasoning already applied to the admin broadcast console.
**Verification:** Updated the existing `tenantOnboarding.test.ts` test (which previously called this endpoint with no passcode and expected 200) to assert 401 with no passcode, 401 with a wrong passcode, and 200 with the real passcode from `BROADCAST_MASTER_SECRET`. Ran the full backend suite against a freshly reset local Postgres: 192/193 passing, the one failure being the same pre-existing, unrelated stale-mock flake in `accountingSchema.test.ts` documented in earlier entries (confirmed by inspection: it uses ordered `mockResolvedValueOnce` chains on a shared mock and is untouched by this or any recent change).
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/routes/tenants.ts`, `backend/src/tests/tenantOnboarding.test.ts`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Fixed AuditLog Cross-Tenant Leak

**What:** `AuditLog` (the `audit_logs` table) had no `tenant_id` column at all, and `GET /api/v1/audit-logs` queried it with zero tenant filter - every tenant's Auditor-role users could see every other tenant's activity trail, including operational events like SMS-gateway failures and email dispatch logs. Same root-cause class as the previous entry's business-table leak, just missed there because `AuditLog` wasn't part of the `dcb13ed` commit. Added a nullable `tenant_id` column (`20260725040000_add_tenant_id_to_audit_logs`) and filtered `GET /api/v1/audit-logs` by `requireTenantContext().tenantId`.
**Also fixed while in this code path:** `withCurrentTenantDb()` requires an active tenant context and throws without one - this was silently breaking audit writes from code with no tenant context (`broadcastService.ts`'s `SYSTEM_BROADCAST` log, `smsService.ts`'s `GATEWAY_OFFLINE` log, `EmailService.ts`'s `logAudit()`), since each call site wrapped the write in a `try/catch` that just logged and swallowed the thrown error - these audit entries were never actually being written. Since `AuditLog` is a genuinely shared table needing no per-tenant schema switching, converted all three to call `prisma.auditLog.create()` directly, with `tenantId: getTenantContext()?.tenantId ?? null` (best-effort, non-throwing) instead of going through `withCurrentTenantDb`.
**Why:** Same severity class as the business-table leak - any Auditor in any tenant could read another tenant's operational history.
**Verification:** New `auditLogsTenantIsolation.test.ts`: onboarded two tenants, seeded one "SECRET" audit entry per tenant, confirmed each tenant's Auditor only sees its own entry. Confirmed the test actually catches the bug by temporarily reverting the `where: { tenantId }` filter and re-running - failed with both tenants' secrets visible to each other, as expected; restored the fix and re-confirmed passing.
**Files Affected:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/migrations/20260725040000_add_tenant_id_to_audit_logs/`, `backend/src/routes/auditLogs.ts`, `backend/src/services/broadcastService.ts`, `backend/src/services/smsService.ts`, `backend/src/services/EmailService.ts`, `backend/src/tests/auditLogsTenantIsolation.test.ts` (new), `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Fixed Two Scheduled Reports Bugs; Found and Worked Around a Stale Tenant-Cache Test Flake

**What:** Investigated the "Scheduled Reports persists to memory only" Known Issue and found two additional concrete bugs beyond what was already documented:
1. **Cross-tenant leak in the in-memory schedule store:** `scheduledReports.ts` read `(req as any).tenantId`, which doesn't exist - `tenantContextMiddleware` only ever sets `req.tenantContext`, never `req.tenantId` - so every tenant's saved schedule silently collided in the same `tenantSchedules[undefined]` slot. Fixed via `requireTenantContext()`.
2. **Fake test-email data:** `POST /reports/schedule/test-email` always sent hardcoded figures (`weeklySales: 3450.00`, `topShopName: 'Osu Downtown Shop'`, `totalItemsSold: 28`) regardless of the tenant. Now computes real numbers from the tenant's own `DailyCloseoutReport` rows from the last 7 days.
3. **Left as a feasibility note, not built:** true DB-backed persistence and an actual cron dispatcher would require a new `ReportSchedule` table (no existing spec for it) and a generalized scheduling engine - a feature build, not a bug fix, so documented in `TASKS.md` rather than invented here.
**Why a test looked flaky along the way:** While verifying the fix, a test intermittently reported accumulated data across separate runs. Traced it to `tenantCache.ts`'s 30-minute Redis TTL: this session's manual verification repeatedly reset Postgres but never restarted Redis, so a fixed tenant slug (e.g. `'sched-corp-1'`) reused across runs could resolve to a stale cached tenant record from an earlier (already-deleted) run. Not a bug in the fix itself, but real enough to document: a production tenant-slug reuse within 30 minutes of deletion could hit the same staleness. Fixed practically by giving every test file's tenant slugs (this file's and five others added earlier in this session) a `Date.now()` suffix, matching the convention older test files already used for exactly this reason - confirmed stable across two consecutive full-suite runs with no Redis flush in between.
**Verification:** New `scheduledReports.test.ts` covering both fixes with two real onboarded tenants; full suite 189/190 passing (the one failure is the same pre-existing, unrelated stale mock).
**Files Affected:** `backend/src/routes/scheduledReports.ts`, `backend/src/tests/scheduledReports.test.ts` (new), `backend/src/tests/inventory.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/cashTill.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/notifications.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/invoicesAndBills.test.ts`, `backend/src/tests/businessTablesTenantIsolation.test.ts`, `STATUS.md`, `TASKS.md`.

## [Date: 2026-07-25] - Wired AdminCoreEngine Diagnostics to Real /health Data

**What:** `AdminCoreEngine.tsx`'s "Engine Diagnostics" tab showed permanently-hardcoded "Operational 99.9%" / "CONNECTED (Pooled SSL)" / "READY (SIM Active)" / "AUTHENTICATED" bars regardless of actual system state, despite a real `/health` endpoint (checking live DB/Redis connectivity) already existing and never being called. Also fixed the top "Core System Status" stat card, visible across every tab, which had the same problem. Fetches `/health` once the console is unlocked and displays the real `database`/`redis`/`status`/`uptime` fields. Since the backend has no real health-check for the SMS gateway or Gmail SMTP transport, those cards/rows now say "Not Monitored" instead of showing a fabricated "Online"/"Connected" status - showing real data next to fabricated data would have been more misleading than the original all-fake version.
**Why:** A previous audit flagged this as an admin-facing tool giving operators false confidence about system health, unrelated to the actual `/health` endpoint that already existed and worked.
**Verification:** Ran the backend and frontend together, unlocked the console with the local dev passcode, and confirmed via Playwright screenshot that the diagnostics tab and top card show real `connected`/`healthy` values and a live uptime counter that increases between page loads.
